### [MED] save_quote and create_direct_order accept NaN/Infinity in money and quantity fields — bulk import rejects them, these seams poison orders that later fail at invoice time

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:317-346; supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:95-131,147-171; supabase/migrations/20260805220757_reject_nonfinite_bulk_import_values.sql:112-137

**Detail.** The 2026-08-05 Sol review established that PostgreSQL numeric accepts 'NaN'/'Infinity'/'-Infinity' and blocked the bulk-import migration until they were explicitly rejected. Neither save_quote nor create_direct_order gained that guard: both cast payload strings straight to numeric (grep for NaN/Infinity/is_finite in 20260730235031 returns 0 matches; create_direct_order likewise has none). A NaN price/quantity/profit flows into quote_items → quote totals → (via convert) orders/order_items — orders.total_price/total_profit are plain numerics that store NaN. convert_quote_to_order would fail late at `ROUND(v_quote.total_price * 100)::bigint` (NaN cannot cast to bigint), aborting conversion with an opaque error; create_direct_order has no cents cast at all, so a NaN order commits successfully and every later invoice creation (`round(v_item.total_price * 100)::bigint`) fails, leaving an order that can never be billed, with NaN commissions attempted.

**Evidence.** save_quote: `(v_item->>'actual_rate')::numeric`, `COALESCE((v_item->>'profit')::numeric, 0)` etc. with no finiteness checks; create_direct_order: `COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0)`; contrast the applied bulk-import follow-up 20260805220757 which 'rejects all non-finite quantity/price/cost values and rounds unit and extended dollar amounts to cents before writes' (Section 4 doc line 56).

**Failure scenario.** A buggy or malicious client sends `{"quantity": "NaN"}` to create_direct_order. The order, order_items, inventory prebook of NaN units, and inventory_transactions rows all commit (NaN propagates through numeric arithmetic); inventory.quantity_prebooked for that product becomes NaN, corrupting Net Position for every later quote/order of that product, and any attempt to invoice the order errors with 'cannot convert NaN to bigint'.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED against current source. (1) Latest save_quote is 20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql (no later migration redefines it); its item loop (lines ~317-346) casts payload text straight to numeric — `COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0)`, `(v_item->>'actual_rate')::numeric`, `(v_item->>'acres')::numeric`, `COALESCE((v_item->>'profit')::numeric, 0)` — with zero finiteness checks (grep for NaN/Infinity/is_finite in the file matches only 'finance'/'maintenance' substrings). (2) Latest create_direct_order is 20260614142939 (no later redefinition); it computes `v_qty := COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0)` and guards only `IF v_qty <= 0 THEN CONTINUE` — in PostgreSQL numeric, NaN compares GREATER than all non-NaN values, so 'NaN'::numeric passes both this guard and the table constraint `chk_inventory_qty_prebooked CHECK (quantity_prebooked >= 0)` (20260209200000:20); it then executes `quantity_prebooked = quantity_prebooked + (v_item->>'quantity')::numeric`, which yields NaN and corrupts Net Position. No trigger on quote_items/order_items/inventory validates finiteness (only total-recompute and cost-snapshot triggers exist). (3) The only non-finite guard in the entire migration set is 20260805220757 (`v_qty::text IN ('NaN','Infinity','-Infinity')`, lines 116-120), which covers bulk import only. (4) Downstream failure points verified: latest convert_quote_to_order (20260702172500:224) has `ROUND(v_quote.total_price * 100)::bigint` (NaN→bigint raises), and invoice creation (20260614150249:106,113) has `round(v_item.total_price * 100)::bigint`, so a NaN direct order commits but can never be invoiced. (5) Not in the baseline: KNOWN_ISSUES.md and the 2026-08-05 section-04 doc discuss non-finite rejection exclusively for the bulk-import RPC; neither mentions the save_quote/create_direct_order gap. The failure scenario is real for any authenticated admin/sales_rep client sending the JSON string "NaN" ('NaN'::numeric is a valid cast). MED severity is appropriate: requires a buggy/malicious authenticated client, impact is inventory/AR integrity corruption requiring manual cleanup, not direct money loss.

### [MED] Soft-deleted invoices (deleted_at set, status unchanged) permanently block create_invoice_from_order and create_split_invoices_from_order, and silently suppress complete_delivery's auto-invoice — the 20260712170000 deleted_at fix covered only the backfill creator

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260620190000_invoice_creator_provenance_totals.sql:60-66; supabase/migrations/20260707090000_u7_split_gate_allow_predelivery.sql:86-90; supabase/migrations/20260716120104_gauntlet_access_boundaries.sql:292-296; supabase/migrations/20260617031416_delete_invoices_softdelete_rpc.sql:66-68

**Detail.** delete_invoices soft-deletes by setting only `deleted_at = now()` — status stays 'draft'/'unposted'/'voided' (the 20260719060256 wrapper adds a status='cancelled' flip ONLY for split-provenance-governed invoices). Three active-invoice guards count `status NOT IN ('voided','cancelled')` with no `deleted_at IS NULL` filter: (1) create_invoice_from_order:60-63 — a soft-deleted draft makes every re-invoice attempt fail with 'Active invoice already exists… Void or cancel the existing invoice first', but the invoice is deleted and invisible, so the instruction is unactionable; (2) the split engine impl:86-88 — same message; the auto-split call from complete_delivery swallows the exception into the needs_split_billing fallback, and the manual split pass then hits the same wall; (3) complete_delivery's auto-invoice guard:292-296 — the delivery completes with no invoice, no flag, and no notification. KNOWN_ISSUES documents the 2026-07-12 fix ('unbilled-delivery guard now ignores soft-deleted invoices') for create_invoice_for_unbilled_delivery only — that function (20260718202607:40) and the tote copy (20260716120104:208) carry `deleted_at IS NULL`; these three guards verifiably do not (checked each body). Mitigation: the admin backfill path still works, and the unbilled-deliveries dashboard should surface case (3), so this is an over-refusal/workflow-deadlock class, not over-billing.

**Evidence.** create_invoice_from_order: `WHERE order_id = p_order_id AND status NOT IN ('voided', 'cancelled');` (no deleted_at); split impl: `SELECT COUNT(*) INTO v_existing_count FROM invoices WHERE order_id = p_order_id AND status NOT IN ('voided', 'cancelled');`; complete_delivery: `WHERE order_id = v_delivery.order_id AND status NOT IN ('voided', 'cancelled') AND (delivery_id = p_delivery_id OR delivery_id IS NULL);`; delete_invoices: `UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;` with `IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft', 'unposted', 'voided') THEN CONTINUE`.

**Failure scenario.** Admin creates a draft invoice on a delivery-less order, spots a mistake, and soft-deletes it from Invoices.tsx. Every subsequent 'Create Invoice' on that order raises 'Active invoice already exists (1 existing invoice(s))' though no visible invoice exists and none can be voided; on a field-allocated order the split-billing flow is likewise permanently refused, so the order can never be billed through the governed paths.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source with migration-order tracing. All three cited guards verifiably lack a deleted_at filter in their live bodies: (1) create_invoice_from_order's guard (20260620190000:60-66) survives unchanged behind the 20260721014858/20260721145936 rename+idempotency wrappers (md5-pinned at 20260721014858:1805); (2) the current create_split_invoices_from_order (20260719060256:361-548) adds no guard and delegates to the renamed 20260707090000 impl whose guard at :86-90 lacks deleted_at; (3) complete_delivery's latest definition (20260716120104:292-296) lacks deleted_at and silently skips invoicing/flagging/notification when the count is nonzero, with the auto-split exception swallowed at :331-357. delete_invoices confirmed to set only deleted_at for non-split-governed invoices (20260711050000:793,806); the 20260719060256 wrapper's cancelled flip is scoped to split-provenance ids only (:628-632). The contrasting fixed paths (20260718202607:38-40 and 20260716120104:208) do carry deleted_at IS NULL, exactly as claimed. Not in KNOWN_ISSUES.md (only the 20260712170000 unbilled-delivery fix at line 1066) and zero mentions in the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline. Scenario UI-reachable: Invoices.tsx:439 calls delete_invoices and the list filter .is('deleted_at', null) at :135 hides the blocking invoice. Minor uncited mitigation: void_invoice's lookup (20260711050000) does not filter deleted_at, so an admin with the hidden UUID could void it to clear the block — consistent with the reviewer's own MED over-refusal classification, which stands.

### [MED] create_direct_order skips the customer-active check that quick delivery enforces

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:90-91; supabase/migrations/20260706130000_stock_policy_warn_not_block.sql:79

**Detail.** create_quick_delivery raises 'Customer is inactive' (`IF NOT v_customer.is_active`); create_direct_order only checks existence, so a confirmed order with inventory prebooking and commissions can be created for a deactivated customer. Verified absence in the current body — the only customer gate is `IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Customer not found'`.

**Evidence.** direct order: `SELECT * INTO v_customer FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id; IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Customer not found: %', p_customer_id; END IF;` — no is_active predicate; quick delivery: `IF NOT v_customer.is_active THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Customer is inactive'; END IF;`.

**Failure scenario.** A rep creates a direct order for a customer deactivated for credit reasons; stock is reserved, commissions are created, and the order proceeds to delivery/invoice with no gate anywhere flagging the inactive account.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. The cited migration 20260614142939 is the timestamp-latest create_direct_order body (later migrations only reference the function, none redefine it) and its only customer gate is lines 90-91 'Customer not found' with no is_active check; the timestamp-latest create_quick_delivery (20260706130000:78-79) does raise 'Customer is inactive', so the asymmetry is real. Checked migrations for a compensating trigger on orders referencing customers.is_active — none exists (checked, not merely not-found). The scenario is reachable end-to-end: NewOrder.tsx:174 fetches customers with no is_active filter and line 704 renders them all in the picker unmarked, while Customers.tsx:247 provides a real deactivation path, so a rep can create a confirmed order with inventory prebooking and commissions for a deactivated customer through the normal UI. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md or the 2026-08-05 gauntlet section-04 baseline. Severity raised to MED because the reviewer understated exposure: the gate is missing at every layer (DB and UI), not just the RPC, and the effect touches money (commissions) and inventory for a possibly credit-hold account.

### [LOW] delivery_remainders has no transition enforcement — pending→fulfilled settable by direct update without a follow-up delivery

- **Finder:** `state-machines`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260225200000_delivery_system_enhancements.sql:127-135 (del_rem_admin_all / del_rem_rep_update policies); .claude/schema-registry.json status_enums delivery_remainders.status; supabase/migrations/20260620130000_commission_batch_freeze_guard.sql:767-768 (the RPC-side transitions that can be bypassed)

**Detail.** delivery_remainders.status (pending/scheduled/fulfilled/cancelled) is the only Section-4 lifecycle status with no BEFORE UPDATE transition trigger at all (verified by trigger inventory; the 20260228300000 C3 set covers orders/deliveries/invoices/quotes/jobs/POs/returns but not delivery_remainders). RLS grants admins ALL and reps UPDATE. cancel_delivery/complete_delivery maintain remainders with careful rules (cancelled only when no followup_delivery_id, fulfilled when followup exists); a direct update can set any of the four statuses in any order — e.g. 'fulfilled' with followup_delivery_id NULL — silently dropping an owed remainder from the follow-up queue.

**Evidence.** Policies: `CREATE POLICY "del_rem_admin_all" ON delivery_remainders ... CREATE POLICY "del_rem_rep_update" ON delivery_remainders ...`; no `CREATE TRIGGER ... ON delivery_remainders` matching any enforce/transition pattern exists in migrations. RPC discipline it bypasses: `UPDATE delivery_remainders SET status = 'cancelled' ... WHERE ... AND followup_delivery_id IS NULL; UPDATE delivery_remainders SET status = 'fulfilled' ... AND followup_delivery_id IS NOT NULL;`

**Failure scenario.** A rep marks a pending remainder 'fulfilled' directly (or a buggy client does). The customer's short-shipped quantity leaves the remainder queue with no follow-up delivery ever scheduled; the shortfall is never delivered or credited.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed. No CREATE TRIGGER on delivery_remainders exists anywhere in supabase/migrations/ (multiline grep, zero matches); the C3 transition set in 20260228300000_critical_prelaunch_fixes.sql covers orders/deliveries/invoices/quotes/jobs/POs/returns only. Current RLS (del_rem_update in 20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:181-184, which superseded the finding's cited 20260225200000 policies — stale citation, same substance) grants UPDATE to any admin or sales_rep with no transition/column restriction, and the RPC-only table-write REVOKE in 20260716120104 covers only PO/AP tables, not delivery_remainders. The bypassed RPC discipline quoted from 20260620130000:767-768 matches source exactly. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md or the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet baseline (zero remainder mentions). Failure scenario requires a direct API write (no UI path mutates the table directly — frontend only reads and calls create_followup_delivery), which is consistent with the reviewer's LOW severity; severity stands.

### [LOW] update_order_items status guard names nonexistent order status 'pending' (stale literal vs CHECK constraint)

- **Finder:** `state-machines`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260617123503_refresh_item_profit_on_sameproduct_order_edit.sql:71-72

**Detail.** The editability guard reads `IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %'`. Per .claude/schema-registry.json, orders.status CHECK allows only ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided'] — 'pending' cannot exist, so the branch is dead weight and the effective rule is confirmed-only. Behaviorally safe (fails closed), but it is a status literal that does not match the CHECK constraint (the class this audit was asked to flag). Caveat: the live invariant sweeps carry 3 allowlisted 'status-literals' rows (KNOWN_ISSUES §0f live-proof note); this may already be one of them — I could not verify the allowlist contents from the repo, so this may be known/accepted rather than new.

**Evidence.** `IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %', v_order.status; END IF;` vs schema-registry `orders.status => ['confirmed', 'partially_fulfilled', 'fulfilled', 'cancelled', 'voided']`.

**Failure scenario.** No runtime failure (fails closed). Risk is maintenance: a future reader assumes a 'pending' order state exists, or a partially_fulfilled order edit is refused with a message implying 'pending' is reachable.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED against current source. (1) Factual accuracy: the latest CREATE OR REPLACE of update_order_items across all migrations is the cited 20260617123503 (lines 71-73: `IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %'`), and .claude/schema-registry.json (lines 1117-1123, 2094-2105) confirms orders_status_check allows only ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided'] — 'pending' is unreachable, so the effective rule is confirmed-only, exactly as claimed. (2) No superseding enforcement: the only later migration touching the function, 20260714221000_order_and_cycle_count_concurrency_guards.sql (lines 4-88), RENAMEs it to _update_order_items_impl and wraps it in a new public update_order_items that adds auth/lock/inventory-snapshot guards but NO status guard — it delegates straight to the impl, so the stale 'pending' literal remains the live status gate (one accuracy nit: the function containing the literal is now named _update_order_items_impl at runtime, behind the public wrapper). (3) The reviewer's allowlist caveat resolves in the finding's favor: scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/status-literals.sql (lines 16-19) matches only ASSIGNMENT forms (`SET col = 'lit'`, `col := 'lit'`) and deliberately excludes read/comparison forms, so this NOT IN guard can never appear in the sweep; the sole status-literals allowlist entry in scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/allowlist.json (line 335-338) is normalize_product_category(), unrelated. (4) Not in the exclusion baselines: docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no mention of this dead branch, and the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet doc contains zero occurrences of update_order_items. Caveat on novelty (not grounds for refutation under the stated baseline rules): this exact issue was previously documented in docs/audits/2026-06-10-sell-side-excellence-audit.md line 81 and line 252 ("update_order_items allows status 'pending' which isn't a legal order status (dead branch; tidy when touching the function...)") and noted as a parked finding in docs/archive/2026-summer-closeout/audits/2026-06-10-codex-sell-side-ship-batch-prompt.md line 43 — so it is a rediscovery of a known-parked tidy item, not a new defect. Behavior fails closed; severity LOW stands.

### [LOW] OrderDetail manual status-change fallback is dead code that would raise a raw trigger error if ever reached

- **Finder:** `state-machines`
- **Location:** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:622-641

**Detail.** executeStatusChange's else-branch performs a direct `orders.update({status})` gated by a local map `validTransitions = { confirmed: ['cancelled'], partially_fulfilled: ['cancelled'] }`. But targetStatus 'cancelled' is intercepted by the `if (targetStatus === 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'cancelled')` RPC branch above, so the direct-update code is unreachable for every value its own map allows — except the degenerate case where order.status is already 'cancelled', where the map lookup yields [] and it errors out. If the map were ever widened, the direct write would now be rejected by `guard_order_delivered_activity_cancel` ('ORDER_TERMINAL_TRANSITION_USE_RPC') as an unhandled raw error. Dead-lane cleanup candidate; harmless today.

**Evidence.** `if (targetStatus === 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'cancelled') { ... supabase.rpc('cancel_order', ...) } else { const validTransitions: Record<string, string[]> = { confirmed: ['cancelled'], partially_fulfilled: ['cancelled'] }; ... await supabase.from('orders').update({ status: targetStatus, ... }) }`

**Failure scenario.** None reachable today; latent confusion/regression risk only — a future edit adding a status to the map would produce an unfriendly ORDER_TERMINAL_TRANSITION_USE_RPC toast instead of routing to the RPC.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. pendingStatus's only setter is setPendingStatus('cancelled') at OrderDetail.tsx:1341, so targetStatus is always 'cancelled' and the else-branch (622-641) is entered only when order.status is already 'cancelled'; there validTransitions['cancelled'] -> [] and the guard at 630-632 returns with an error toast, making the direct orders.update at 634-638 unreachable — the finding's analysis is accurate and even slightly conservative. The trigger claim also checks out: guard_order_delivered_activity_cancel (migrations/20260721014858...govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:1409-1424) raises ORDER_TERMINAL_TRANSITION_USE_RPC on any non-override direct status update into cancelled/voided, fires BEFORE UPDATE OF status ON orders, and no later migration modifies it. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md or the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet baseline. Failure scenario correctly stated as none-reachable-today / latent-only; LOW severity is appropriate.

### [LOW] duplicate_quote silently drops is_planned and needed_by_date from the copy

- **Finder:** `planned-holds`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql:1017-1049

**Detail.** duplicate_quote's quotes INSERT omits is_planned (column default false applies), and its quote_sections INSERT copies only quote_id/section_name/sort_order/section_notes - dropping needed_by_date AND section_header_notes. A duplicated planned program is therefore silently unplanned (no holds will be created for it) and loses its need-by scheduling data even after the user re-toggles Mark Planned. No orphan risk, but the copy quietly diverges from the source in exactly the fields that drive the hold/forecast machinery, with no UI indication.

**Evidence.** 1017-1021: `INSERT INTO quotes (quote_number, customer_id, created_by, tier, status, commission_split, total_price, total_cost, total_profit, total_margin_pct, valid_days, expires_at, header_notes, footer_notes)` - no is_planned. 1042-1044: `INSERT INTO quote_sections (quote_id, section_name, sort_order, section_notes)` - no needed_by_date, no section_header_notes.

**Failure scenario.** A rep duplicates last year's planned program to reuse for the same customer, assumes it is still a planned booking, and sends it; no inventory holds exist for it, so the season's demand is never reserved or forecast, and the rep only discovers this if they notice the Mark Planned toggle is off.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. The 20260526151856 migration (lines 1017-1049) holds the latest definition of duplicate_quote — the only later mentions (20260331600000:402,534) are idempotency-scope name lists, not redefinitions — and its quotes INSERT omits is_planned while its quote_sections INSERT omits needed_by_date and section_header_notes, all of which existed before this migration (added in 20260316300000 and 20260316500000). Both UI duplicate paths (Quotes.tsx:112, CustomerDetail.tsx:1418) call this RPC, so the drop is live behavior. sync_planned_holds (20260316500000:42) exits early for non-planned quotes, so the copy gets no holds and re-toggling Mark Planned cannot recover the never-copied needed_by_date. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md or the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet baseline. Severity stays LOW: resetting is_planned on a copy is a defensible default (auto-reserving inventory on duplicate would be worse), and the loss is visible in QuoteBuilder as an off toggle/empty dates — but the section_header_notes/needed_by_date drop is genuine silent data loss on copy with no documented rationale.

### [LOW] Crop-program holds are unchecked soft holds: creation never checks stock, nothing blocks overselling, and excess surfaces only on the Forecast tab

- **Finder:** `planned-holds`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260702171000_layer2_sync_planned_holds_job_aware.sql:89-104; supabase/migrations/20260716120120_gauntlet_inventory_accuracy.sql:169; src/pages/InventoryPage.tsx:393-396

**Detail.** Checked the migrations: _sync_planned_holds and create_planned_holds perform no inventory read whatsoever, and net_position is `quantity_available - quantity_prebooked + quantity_on_order` - active holds are deliberately NOT subtracted (documented in src/types/index.ts:1007). So two planned quotes can jointly hold more than on-hand with no error, no warning, and an unchanged Net column. The only stock-vs-hold warning in the product is for MANUAL holds on InventoryPage (todaysFree check); marking a quote planned in QuoteBuilder produces no shortage indication at all (searched QuoteBuilder - no shortfall/exceed check on the planned path), and the Forecast tab is the sole surface comparing planned demand to available+on_order - which misses undated holds entirely (finding 3). This soft-hold model is plausibly intended (the Layer 2 owner decision for job holds was explicitly 'WARN, never block', and holds are described as forecast bookkeeping), but the WARN half is not implemented for crop programs: there is no warn at mark-planned time and no over-held indicator on InventoryPage. Reporting so the intended/unintended call can be made explicitly.

**Evidence.** _sync_planned_holds hold insert has no reference to the inventory table anywhere in the function body. net_position: `(b.quantity_available - b.quantity_prebooked + COALESCE(oo.qty, 0)) AS net_position`. InventoryPage.tsx:393: `const todaysFree = invItem.quantity_available - invItem.quantity_prebooked - invItem.holds_qty;` exists only in the manual-hold creation flow.

**Failure scenario.** Two reps each mark 300-unit planned programs against 400 units on hand. Both saves succeed silently; Net still shows 400. The first real conversion prebooks 300 and the second program's holder discovers at delivery time that the product was never obtainable - the oversell was visible nowhere except a Forecast row (and not even there if the sections lacked needed-by dates).

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. _sync_planned_holds (latest def, 20260702171000:23-110) contains no inventory-table reference; the latest create_planned_holds (20260613191120) has only role guards, no stock check; no trigger on inventory_holds enforces stock. net_position deliberately excludes holds (20260716120120:169; documented src/types/index.ts:1007). The warn asymmetry is real: manual holds default-BLOCK server-side (create_inventory_hold, 20260507170000:109-121, INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY) with a client todaysFree warn (InventoryPage.tsx:393), and job holds implement the §6.1 'WARN never block' owner decision by returning shortfalls (20260702174000:185-208) — but the crop_program planned path computes/returns no shortfalls and QuoteBuilder.tsx:1543-1556 just toasts success, so two planned programs can jointly hold more than on-hand with zero indication. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md, the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline (its oversell item is the unrelated CSV order-import side door), or DECISION_LOG as a settled crop-program decision. Failure scenario is reproducible as described. LOW severity stands: forecast-bookkeeping/warning gap, not a money or hard-inventory defect.

The test would NOT catch the orphaned-hold classes above because it never asserts on holds after a delete/unplan, and its inventory assertions are vacuous for the hold mechanism.

(3) The uncovered exits (cancelled, expired, closed_short, closed_by_application, unplan toggle, soft delete, revert recreation, resize, expiry) are exercised with hold assertions by no other e2e spec: comprehensive-ui-workflow toggles is_planned without asserting on holds; quote-builder-v2 touches inventory_holds only in teardown; stream0-db-integrity DB6's checkQuoteHoldParity (reconciliation-checks.ts:293-317) checks only the missing-hold direction and cannot detect orphaned active holds on terminal/deleted quotes. ### [LOW] Quotes-list delete guard misses planned quotes with active zero-draw scheduled jobs

- **Finder:** `planned-holds`
- **Location:** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:387-400

**Detail.** The delete guard checks quote_product_draws and job_product_draws for quantity_drawn > 0. A scheduled/in_progress job attached to the quote whose demand drew nothing from the booking (its chemicals are not among the quote's items, so the crop pool for those products is 0 and _sync_quote_job_reservations writes a 'job' hold but no draw row) leaves no draw evidence, so the quote soft-deletes while the job remains scheduled and dispatched, referencing a hidden quote. The job's own 'job' hold survives correctly (source_id = job_id, released by the job lifecycle), but the operational link is broken: QuoteBuilder can no longer be opened to see or manage the booking the job descends from, and the close_quote_as_short active-jobs refusal (20260706030000:92-98) that protects the status-based exits has no counterpart on the delete exit. Narrow edge (requires a job whose products diverge from the quote's items), reported for completeness of the exit-path matrix.

**Evidence.** Quotes.tsx:388-389: `supabase.from('quote_product_draws').select('quote_id').in('quote_id', ids).gt('quantity_drawn', 0), supabaseUntyped.from('job_product_draws').select('quote_id').in('quote_id', ids).gt('quantity_drawn', 0)` - existence of scheduled jobs themselves is never checked, unlike the closed_short trigger guard: `IF NEW.status = 'closed_short' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM jobs WHERE quote_id = NEW.id AND deleted_at IS NULL AND status IN ('scheduled','in_progress'))`.

**Failure scenario.** A planned booking has one scheduled job whose recipe products differ from the quote lines (zero draws). Staff delete the quote; the job stays on the dispatch board holding stock for a booking nobody can open, and cancelling/completing the job later resyncs against a deleted quote (safe but confusing: crop pool 0, draws dropped).

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. (1) The delete guard at src/pages/Quotes.tsx:387-400 checks only quote_product_draws and job_product_draws with .gt('quantity_drawn', 0); it never checks for scheduled/in_progress jobs themselves — quoted code matches the finding verbatim. (2) The zero-draw mechanism is real: in _sync_quote_job_reservations (20260706030000:246-296, preserved in the latest version 20260710120000:239-244), a job product absent from quote_items yields v_booking=0 → v_crop_pool=0 → v_draw=LEAST(demand,0)=0, and the job_product_draws INSERT is gated by `IF v_draw > 0`, while the 'job' inventory_hold is written unconditionally for v_hold=demand>0 — so an active job can exist with zero draw evidence. (3) The asymmetry with the status exits is real: both close_quote_as_short (20260706030000:377-383) and the direct-status trigger guard (:92-98, `IF NEW.status = 'closed_short' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM jobs WHERE quote_id = NEW.id AND deleted_at IS NULL AND status IN ('scheduled','in_progress'))`) refuse active jobs; the soft-delete exit (plain UPDATE of deleted_at) has no counterpart. (4) I checked the migrations for a DB-side enforcement the reviewer might have missed: every trigger on quotes fires on UPDATE OF status or commission_split (plus a row_version bump that checks nothing); _enforce_quote_status_transition early-returns when status is unchanged; no later migration adds a deleted_at guard — so the DB does not enforce this (checked, not merely unfound). (5) Post-delete behavior matches: _sync_quote_job_reservations looks up the quote with `deleted_at IS NULL`, so a later job cancel/complete resyncs gracefully with crop_pool=0 — "safe but confusing" as stated. (6) Not in the baseline: KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no match for the quote delete guard / zero-draw job gap, and the 2026-08-05 section-04 doc's only Quotes.tsx mention is the convert_quote_to_order caller; no mention of active jobs or the delete exit. Severity LOW is correct: the edge requires every active job on the quote to have drawn nothing (any single positive draw row blocks the delete), inventory stays correct (the 'job' hold is keyed to the job and released by the job lifecycle), and the impact is an orphaned operational link (job on the dispatch board referencing a hidden quote), not money or stock corruption.

### [LOW] convert_quote_to_order copies live quote rows, never the sent/accepted quote_versions snapshot — a quote edited after send ('revised', or a DB-level sent→sent item rewrite) converts at prices the customer never saw

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260702172500_layer2_convert_quote_to_order_job_aware.sql:120,167-179; supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:107-111,242-247; src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:445,455; docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:34-35,53-54

**Detail.** convert_quote_to_order builds order_items directly `FROM quote_items` and copies quote-header totals; it never reads quote_versions and never verifies that live rows still match the last sent snapshot. Two paths make live rows diverge from what the customer accepted: (1) Sanctioned path — status 'revised' is editable in the UI (canEdit includes 'revised') AND convertible (canConvert and the RPC's `status NOT IN ('sent','revised','accepted')` gate both allow 'revised'), and the workflow doc says a new snapshot is only created 'on next send' — so a revised-but-never-resent quote converts at edited prices with no snapshot of what was converted. (2) DB-level path — save_quote's transition matrix only runs `IF v_status IS DISTINCT FROM v_old_status`, so a sent→sent save (same status) skips validation entirely and rewrites every section/item at new prices while `sent_at` is preserved; no guard I could find prevents item rewrites while status stays 'sent' (the UI blocks it — canEdit excludes 'sent' — but the SECURITY DEFINER RPC does not; a quote owner via PostgREST can do it). The doc's own 'common mistakes' list warns 'Changing product prices after a quote was sent (the snapshot preserves the original)' — but the snapshot only preserves evidence; conversion, ordering, delivery, and invoicing all flow from the mutable rows.

**Evidence.** convert (20260702172500:126-179): `INSERT INTO order_items ... SELECT ... qi.price_per_unit, qi.current_cost, ... FROM quote_items qi JOIN quote_sections qs ... WHERE qi.quote_id = p_quote_id` — no quote_versions reference anywhere in the body; :120 `IF v_quote.status NOT IN ('sent', 'revised', 'accepted') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'BOOKING_CLOSED...'`; save_quote (20260730235031:242): `IF v_status IS DISTINCT FROM v_old_status THEN` guards the only transition check; QuoteBuilder.tsx:445 `const canEdit = ['draft', 'revised'].includes(currentStatus);` :455 `const canConvert = ['sent', 'revised', 'accepted'].includes(currentStatus);`

**Failure scenario.** Rep sends a quote at $10/unit (v1 snapshot), customer verbally accepts. Rep flips to 'revised', raises to $11/unit, and clicks Convert without re-sending. The order, delivery, and invoice all carry $11; no quote_versions snapshot contains $11, so the dispute trail shows the customer accepted $10. Same result via a direct sent→sent save_quote API call with rewritten items, with the quote still displaying as an untouched 'sent' quote.

**Verifier verdict.** Factually confirmed against current source: convert_quote_to_order (latest definition = 20260702172500, no later migration redefines it) builds order_items FROM live quote_items (:167-179) with no quote_versions reference, and allows 'revised' (:120); QuoteBuilder.tsx:445/:455 canEdit includes 'revised' and canConvert includes 'revised'; save_quote (latest = 20260730235031) runs its transition matrix only IF v_status IS DISTINCT FROM v_old_status (:241), so a sent→sent save rewrites all items with no DB guard (only status-transition triggers exist on quotes; KNOWN_ISSUES ~line 217 shows draft→sent→sent is a deliberately supported save path); create_quote_version fires only on send/email, never in the convert handlers (QuoteBuilder.tsx:2434-2513). Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md nor in the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline (whose sole finding is the Bulk Order Import bypass). However, severity is corrected to LOW: (1) the 'sanctioned path' half is documented intended design — QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:34-35 explicitly defines 'revised' as edits-after-send with snapshot deferred to next send, and converting a revised quote at revised prices is the normal verbal-renegotiation flow; the system nowhere promises conversion matches the last-sent snapshot; (2) the evidence-loss claim is overstated — order_items durably snapshots conversion-time prices with quote_item_id linkage, and financial_audit_log records converted totals (20260702172500:212-230), so the trail does show sent-v1 $10 / revised / order $11; (3) the DB-level sent→sent rewrite is owner-or-admin-only (NOT_QUOTE_OWNER + active-role gates), requires the current row_version, bumps row_version/updated_at, and its only marginal harm over the sanctioned revised path is concealing the 'revised' status label — an insider-concealment hardening gap, not a money-math or privilege defect.

### [LOW] Quick delivery drops unit_size on order_items and invoice_items, unlike every other seam

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260706130000_stock_policy_warn_not_block.sql:203-226

**Detail.** create_quick_delivery's order_items insert omits the unit_size column entirely (delivery_items get `COALESCE(v_item->>'unit_size', v_product.unit_size)` but order_items and invoice_items do not), and its invoice_items insert has no unit_size, acres, or rate_per_acre columns. convert_quote_to_order and create_direct_order both persist order_items.unit_size, and create_invoice_from_order / the delivery auto-invoice copy unit_size onto invoice lines. Consequence: quick-delivery invoices and any later create_invoice_from_order over a quick order render lines with NULL unit_size (PDF/statement display gap), a field-fidelity break relative to the quote-born path.

**Evidence.** order_items insert columns: `(order_id, product_id, product_name, total_units_needed, price_per_unit, cost_per_unit, quantity_delivered, quantity_remaining, total_price, profit, net_margin, sort_order)` — no unit_size; invoice_items insert columns: `(invoice_id, order_item_id, product_id, description, quantity, unit_price_cents, extended_cents, cost_cents, sort_order)` — no unit_size; contrast delivery_items: `COALESCE(v_item->>'unit_size', v_product.unit_size)`.

**Failure scenario.** A quick-delivery invoice PDF shows '12 × Product X' with no pack/unit size while the same product invoiced via the quote path shows '12 × Product X (2.5 gal)'; downstream unit-conversion or usage reports keyed on invoice_items.unit_size treat the line as unitless.

**Verifier verdict.** Confirmed against current source. The live create_quick_delivery body (20260706130000, lines 203-226) omits unit_size from its order_items insert and omits unit_size/acres/rate_per_acre from its invoice_items insert, while its delivery_items insert does set unit_size via COALESCE(v_item->>'unit_size', v_product.unit_size). The only later migration touching the function (20260803010917) renames that body to _create_quick_delivery_intent_impl_20260802 and adds an idempotency wrapper without changing any insert columns, so the gap is live. No trigger backfills unit_size on order_items or invoice_items (only cost-snapshot, updated_at, and lock-guard triggers exist). The contrast is real: create_direct_order (20260805211951:176,191) and convert_quote_to_order persist order_items.unit_size, and split/auto-invoice paths persist invoice_items.unit_size (20260611211058:1333). Failure scenario is reachable: InvoiceDetail.tsx:1796-1797 renders item.unit_size conditionally (blank when NULL), and the CRM card-volume rollup (20260717112533:132,145) groups invoice_items by unit_size, so quick-delivery lines aggregate under a NULL unit separately from quote-path lines. Not documented in KNOWN_ISSUES.md or the 2026-08-05 gauntlet section-04 baseline. LOW severity stands — display/reporting fidelity only; inventory-driving delivery_items do carry unit_size and no money math depends on it.

### [LOW] Cent-rounding formula differs across the three invoice-line paths for the same delivered quantity

- **Finder:** `conversion-seams`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260716120104_gauntlet_access_boundaries.sql:273,406,417; supabase/migrations/20260706130000_stock_policy_warn_not_block.sql:225

**Detail.** Line totals for identical economic facts are computed three ways: quick delivery uses `safe_cents_qty(unit_cents, qty)`; the delivery auto-invoice uses `ROUND(quantity_delivered * price_per_unit * 100)` (dollars first, one round); the partial-completion adjustment rewrites the same line as `ROUND(di.quantity_delivered * ii.unit_price_cents)` (cents-basis). For fractional quantities with sub-cent unit prices these differ by a cent (e.g. price 10.005, qty 3: round(3×10.005×100)=3002 vs 3×round(10.005×100)=3003). Header cost totals similarly differ: creators use ROUND(cost×qty×100) while the partial recompute uses SUM(cost_cents × quantity). Self-consistent per invoice (header is recomputed from lines where it matters) but a partial completion can shift an already-created line by a cent versus what a full completion would have billed, and cross-path cent drift shows up in reconciliation against orders.total_price.

**Evidence.** :273 `extended_cents = ROUND(di.quantity_delivered * ii.unit_price_cents)` vs :417 `ROUND(v_item.quantity_delivered * v_item.price_per_unit * 100)::bigint` vs quick delivery `safe_cents_qty(v_item_price_cents, v_item_qty)`.

**Failure scenario.** Delivery of 3 units at $10.005 invoiced via auto-invoice bills 3002 cents; the same 3 units on a partially-completed quick delivery are re-extended to 3003 cents; AR reconciliation against the order total shows a 1-cent unexplained variance per affected line.

**Verifier verdict.** All three cited formulas verified verbatim in current source: 20260716120104:273 (ROUND(qty * unit_price_cents), cents-basis partial rewrite), :417 (ROUND(qty * price_per_unit * 100), dollars-first auto-invoice), and 20260706130000:225 (safe_cents_qty(price_cents, qty), quick delivery). Later migrations (20260716173342/191000/202000 for complete_delivery; 20260803010917 for create_quick_delivery) only rename-and-wrap the implementations for authorization/preflight/idempotency — the arithmetic is unchanged and live. The failure scenario is real: order_items.price_per_unit is unconstrained numeric with no rounding trigger, so sub-cent unit prices can reach these paths, and for such prices dollars-first vs cents-basis rounding differ by a cent (3×$10.005 → 3002 vs 3003 cents, valid under Postgres half-away-from-zero rounding). Not previously documented: KNOWN_ISSUES.md covers a different rounding issue (parked split-billing Feature B independent-rounding blocker) and the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline covers bulk_import_order sub-cent normalization only. Severity LOW is correct — divergence requires sub-cent prices or the fractional-qty partial path, is cent-scale, and each invoice remains internally self-consistent.

## Phase 2: Money & Idempotency (19)

### [HIGH] Void→rebill of an order invoice permanently cancels the order's pending commissions — no path ever re-mints them

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260718221505_preserve_voided_payment_allocation_history.sql:250-256 (latest void_invoice re-emit); supabase/migrations/20260620190000_invoice_creator_provenance_totals.sql:60-66 (rebill path); supabase/migrations/20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:404-477 (mint at order creation only)

**Detail.** Order-channel commissions are minted exactly once, at order creation (_insert_commissions_for_order called from convert_quote_to_order / create_direct_order / create_quick_delivery / bulk_import_order). The posted→voided path of void_invoice cancels ALL of the order's pending commissions whenever the voided invoice was the last live invoice for that order — with no condition on the order's own status and no delivery_id scoping. create_invoice_from_order contains zero commission logic, and no function anywhere transitions a commission from cancelled back to pending (the only cancelled-excluded reset is void_commission_payment, which explicitly skips cancelled rows via `AND c.status <> 'cancelled'`). create_invoice_from_order's own error text sanctions the void-then-recreate flow ('Void or cancel the existing invoice first if you need to recreate'), so the routine correction workflow — void a posted order invoice with a wrong price, recreate it, post it, collect payment — leaves the rep's commissions cancelled forever. The admin void notification does mention 'N pending commission(s) cancelled' but offers no recovery path; the UI has no re-pend feature, so recovery requires a manual admin DB edit. This also fires for per-delivery invoices on a partially_fulfilled order: voiding the sole live delivery invoice cancels ALL pending commissions for the whole order even though the order remains active and future deliveries will be billed. The job channel (U8) handles the same lifecycle correctly by minting per-invoice generations that are re-minted on re-invoice; the order channel has no equivalent.

**Evidence.** void_invoice (20260718221505:250-256): `IF v_inv.order_id IS NOT NULL THEN IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM invoices WHERE order_id = v_inv.order_id AND id != p_invoice_id AND status NOT IN ('voided', 'cancelled') AND deleted_at IS NULL) THEN UPDATE commissions SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE order_id = v_inv.order_id AND status = 'pending';` — create_invoice_from_order (20260620190000:64-65): `RAISE EXCEPTION 'Active invoice already exists for order % ... Void or cancel the existing invoice first if you need to recreate.'` (body has no commissions reference at all) — void_commission_payment reset excludes cancelled rows (20260707060000:1650-1653): `-- a row already 'cancelled' ... must never be resurrected to payable by voiding the batch. AND c.status <> 'cancelled';`

**Failure scenario.** Order O-1234 fulfilled, commissions pending for rep. Admin posts invoice, notices a wrong unit price, voids it (last live invoice → all pending commissions for O-1234 set cancelled). Admin recreates via create_invoice_from_order, posts, customer pays in full. The rep's commissions remain status='cancelled' permanently; CommissionPayments' pending-only picker and get_commission_balance_report never show them; the rep is silently never paid unless an admin hand-edits the commissions table.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Four refutation routes tried; all failed.

1) Stale-body check (reviewer's weakest point, not fatal). The cited `public.void_invoice` at supabase/migrations/20260718221505_preserve_voided_payment_allocation_history.sql:4 was RENAMED twice after that date: `RENAME TO _void_invoice_split_provenance_impl_20260719;` (20260719060256_allow_governed_split_terminal_lifecycle.sql:675) and that wrapper in turn `RENAME TO _void_invoice_group_guard_impl_20260720;` (20260720175946_protect_governed_split_edit_and_void_group.sql:93). I read both wrappers (20260719060256:681-760, 20260720175946:99-142): they add bound-idempotency claiming and a governed-split group gate, then `PERFORM public._void_invoice_split_provenance_impl_20260719(...)` / `PERFORM public._void_invoice_group_guard_impl_20260720(...)`. Neither adds commission logic. The cited block is still the effective body reached from the public entry point — only the function NAME in the citation is one rename generation stale. Same for `create_invoice_from_order`: renamed to `_create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718` (20260721014858:154) behind a new public wrapper (20260721014858:168-243) and an idempotency wrapper (20260721145936:84). I read the wrapper in full — it adds auth, request fingerprinting, and an `ORDER_INVOICE_TERMINAL` check and contains zero commission statements. "create_invoice_from_order contains zero commission logic" holds for both wrapper and impl.

2) Missed re-mint path — none exists. Every `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_order(...)` call site is an order-CREATION path (create_direct_order 20260614142939:190, quote draw-down 20260610181726:248, quick delivery 20260622090000:248, etc.) plus exactly one non-creation caller, `price_order` (20260614145356:139), gated on `IF v_was_pending THEN` — the needs_pricing→priced transition only, unreachable on an already-priced order. Grepping every `SET status = 'pending'` on `commissions` across all migrations returns only (a) the 20260332900000 one-time invalid-status cleanup DO block and (b) `void_commission_payment`, whose reset the reviewer correctly quotes as excluding cancelled rows (rationale at 20260622070000_void_commission_payment_dead_order_guard.sql:8-11). The order-item edit path explicitly freezes them: 20260617115903_recompute_pending_commissions_on_order_item_edit.sql:14-15 "PAID and cancelled rows are FROZEN — never silently change an already-paid payout." Only one trigger exists on `commissions` (`trg_commissions_recipient_resolved`, 20260722134252:220), a NULL-recipient backstop.

3) Reachability — both ends are wired in the UI: `supabase.rpc('create_invoice_from_order', ...)` at src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:878 and `supabase.rpc('void_invoice', ...)` at src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:861. The order branch is reached only for posted/overdue invoices (draft/unposted short-circuits to 'cancelled' and returns, 20260718221505:31-100) — exactly the described scenario. Re-creation is not blocked: the existence check excludes `('voided','cancelled')` (20260620190000:61-66), so the voided invoice is invisible to it.

4) Already documented? No. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md's commission entries cover the OPPOSITE direction (commission RESURRECTION via void_commission_payment, closed by 20260622070000 — §1 line 925 and line 937), name-vs-id split identity (lines 941-953, 1040, 1053), and the parked blend-ticket mint (lines 993/1020). The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline discusses only bulk_import_order's commission omission. Neither mentions void→rebill order-commission loss. docs/reference/gotchas.md:185 documents only the batched-commission freeze invariant.

AGGRAVATING fact the reviewer missed (why I keep HIGH rather than downgrade): after the void cancels the rows, the order also becomes un-closable. `_close_undelivered_order_remainder_20260718` hard-refuses any order carrying a non-pending commission — `IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.commissions WHERE order_id = p_order_id AND deleted_at IS NULL AND status <> 'pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_COMMISSION_STATE_UNSAFE';` (supabase/migrations/20260721014858_20260721010000_govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:988-992). A partially_fulfilled order whose sole delivery invoice was voided loses all pending commissions AND can no longer close its undelivered remainder.

PARTIAL MITIGATION found (argues MED, insufficient to refute): InvoiceDetail exposes Unpost for chemical-sale invoices too, not just field-application — src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:111-115 "Unpost — reverse a posting on a posted field-application or chemical-sale invoice (returns it to the editable Unposted list). The RPC is type-agnostic." `unpost_invoice` (20260625130000) contains no `UPDATE commissions` at all, so unpost→edit→repost preserves commissions and is a safe correction path for a wrong price. But it is not the path the DB's own error text advertises ("Void or cancel the existing invoice first if you need to recreate", 20260620190000:64-65), void sits one admin click away on the same page, and the delivery-invoice variant has no unpost analogue: the predicate at 20260718221505:251-256 has no `delivery_id` scoping and no order-status condition, so voiding the sole live delivery invoice of a still-active order wipes ALL of that order's pending commissions.

Net: real, undocumented, silently under-pays a rep with no in-app recovery, plus a second-order lifecycle lock. Severity HIGH stands.

Note for whoever fixes it: cite the live function names (`_void_invoice_split_provenance_impl_20260719`, `_create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718`), not the pre-rename public names.

### [MED] OrderDetail invoice query omits deleted_at filter — soft-deleted drafts inflate AR tiles, permanently hide Create Invoice, and are targeted by Post All Drafts

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:240-245, 903-905, 931, 1100-1107, 1169, 1195; supabase/migrations/20260711050000_credit_apply_reversal_and_lifecycle.sql:793-806

**Detail.** OrderDetail loads linked invoices filtering only by status ('voided','cancelled' excluded) with no `.is('deleted_at', null)`. The current delete_invoices RPC soft-deletes draft/unposted/voided invoices by setting deleted_at only — status stays 'draft'/'unposted'. So after an admin deletes a draft invoice (it disappears from the Invoices page, which does filter deleted_at), OrderDetail still (a) counts it in the Total Invoiced / Amount Paid / Balance Due tiles (lines 1100-1107 sum the unfiltered array), (b) treats it as active in hasActiveInvoice (903-905), which hides the Create Invoice / re-bill buttons at 1169 and 1195 — the order can never be re-billed from this page, and (c) includes it in handlePostAllDrafts targets (931); the post attempt then fails server-side with INVOICE_DELETED (post_invoice guard) producing a spurious per-target error toast. Other invoice list pages (e.g. Invoices.tsx:135) do filter deleted_at; OrderDetail is the outlier.

**Evidence.** OrderDetail.tsx:240-245: `.from('invoices').select('*').eq('order_id', id!).not('status', 'in', '("voided","cancelled")')` — no deleted_at filter. OrderDetail.tsx:903-905: `const hasActiveInvoice = invoices.some((inv) => !['voided', 'cancelled'].includes(inv.status));` OrderDetail.tsx:931: `const drafts = invoices.filter((i) => i.status === 'draft' || i.status === 'unposted');` 20260711050000:793: `IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft', 'unposted', 'voided') THEN CONTINUE; END IF;` then :806: `UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;` (status unchanged).

**Failure scenario.** Admin creates a draft invoice from an order, decides it is wrong, deletes it via the Invoices page (delete_invoices). Returning to the order: Balance Due tile still shows the deleted draft's amount, the Create Invoice button is gone (hasActiveInvoice true forever), and Post All Drafts errors on the deleted row. The order becomes unbillable through OrderDetail until someone notices the hidden soft-deleted row.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — I could not refute it. Every cited fact checks out against current source, and I found no enforcement mechanism elsewhere.

1) Query is unfiltered. src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:239-245: `const { data: invoiceData } = await supabase.from('invoices').select('*').eq('order_id', id!).not('status', 'in', '("voided","cancelled")').order('invoice_number');` — no `.is('deleted_at', null)`. OrderDetail only uses `deleted_at` in three unrelated spots (grep: lines 271 customers, 715, 946), never on this load.

2) RLS does not compensate (checked the migrations, not just "didn't find"). The only `invoices_select` policy in the whole migration tree is supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262: `CREATE POLICY invoices_select ON invoices FOR SELECT USING (is_admin() OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))` — no `deleted_at` predicate, and no later migration redefines it. So the DB genuinely returns soft-deleted rows to this client query.

3) The CURRENT delete_invoices still leaves status='draft' for ordinary invoices. The reviewer cited 20260711050000, which is superseded — I traced the chain: 20260719044912_trust_only_post_revoke_split_provenance.sql:604 renames the then-live function to `_delete_invoices_split_provenance_impl_20260719`, and the newest definition is supabase/migrations/20260719060256_allow_governed_split_terminal_lifecycle.sql:555. That newest wrapper calls the impl (:622) and then only cancels GOVERNED rows: `UPDATE public.invoices i SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE i.id = ANY(v_governed_ids) AND i.deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND i.status IN ('draft','unposted')`, where `v_governed_ids` comes solely from `public.split_invoice_provenance`. A plain (non-split) order draft has no provenance row, so it keeps status='draft' — the impl body (20260711050000:806) does `UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;` and nothing to status. The reviewer's conclusion survives on the current chain; only the citation is one migration stale.

4) Failure path is reachable. src/pages/Invoices.tsx:217 `const selectedDeletable = selectedInvoices.filter((i) => ['draft','voided'].includes(i.status));` → :439 `supabase.rpc('delete_invoices', ...)`, and Invoices.tsx:135 filters `.is('deleted_at', null)` so the row vanishes there. OrderDetail then still: sums it into the tiles (1098-1101: `invoices.reduce((sum, inv) => sum + (inv.total_amount_cents || 0), 0)` / `... inv.balance_cents ...`); sets `hasActiveInvoice` (903-905) which gates BOTH the Create Invoice button (1169) and Create Split Invoices (1195); and feeds handlePostAllDrafts (931). The 946 re-check `supabase.from('invoices').select('status').is('deleted_at', null).eq('id', ...)` returns zero rows, so `currentRows.length > 0` is false and the code does NOT skip — it calls post_invoice, which raises per 20260721014858_20260721010000_govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:652 `RAISE EXCEPTION 'INVOICE_DELETED: a soft-deleted invoice cannot be posted'`. Spurious error toast confirmed.

5) No server-side backstop makes the hidden button harmless. I searched the current create_invoice_from_order chain (20260620190000, 20260721014858, 20260721145936) for an "order already invoiced" duplicate guard and found none — only idempotency-argument guards. So the UI `hasActiveInvoice` gate is effectively the sole block on re-billing, and it is stuck true forever.

6) Not baselined. No `deleted_at` / `hasActiveInvoice` / `Total Invoiced` / OrderDetail-tile entry exists in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md or docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md (the only related KNOWN_ISSUES hit is the 2026-07-12 fix for the unbilled-delivery guard, migrations 20260712170000/180000 — a different surface, and it establishes soft-deleted invoices as a recognized bug class rather than covering this instance).

Two corrections to the write-up, neither fatal: (a) the row is NOT hidden on OrderDetail — it renders in the Linked Invoices table (1549-1576) as a normal `draft` Badge with no deleted indicator, so it is discoverable there, which softens "until someone notices the hidden row"; (b) an additional un-cited impact: it also feeds `handleConsolidateDrafts`, gated at 1524 `invoices.filter((i) => i.status === 'draft').length >= 2`, so a soft-deleted draft can trigger/participate in consolidation.

Severity stays MED: no ledger corruption or money miscalculation is written (balance_cents is per-invoice generated and the real AR pages filter deleted_at); the damage is a wrong per-order AR display, a permanently unbillable order via this page, and a spurious post error — operational, admin-only, and recoverable by an engineer clearing deleted_at.

### [MED] Non-finite numeric rejection exists only on the bulk-import path — create_direct_order and save_quote accept 'NaN'/'Infinity' quantities and prices via direct API and store them into money and inventory columns

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:118-125, 147-188; supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:321-427; contrast supabase/migrations/20260805220757_reject_nonfinite_bulk_import_values.sql:112-137

**Detail.** The Section-4 gauntlet established (Sol blocked publication over it) that PostgreSQL numeric accepts NaN/Infinity and that import RPCs must reject them; the fix was applied ONLY to bulk_import_order. Checked the migrations: no equivalent guard exists in create_direct_order or save_quote (grep for 'NaN'/finiteness guards matches only bulk-import and supplier-pricing files). Both RPCs cast jsonb strings straight to numeric: a JSON payload with quantity/price of the string "NaN" passes `(v_item->>'quantity')::numeric` (yielding numeric NaN), passes the `<= 0` skip test (NaN <= 0 is false), and in Postgres numeric semantics NaN compares GREATER than any finite value, so `CHECK (quantity_prebooked >= 0)` also passes. create_direct_order keeps everything in numeric (no bigint cast to fail-closed), so NaN lands in orders.total_price/total_profit, order_items, inventory.quantity_prebooked, inventory_transactions.quantity, and the commission amount via _insert_commissions_for_order(NaN). save_quote likewise: `COALESCE((v_item->>'total_price')::numeric, 0)` at :342 and the recompute CTE (ROUND(NaN,2) = NaN) poison quotes.total_price/total_profit, then convert_quote_to_order copies them into orders and commissions. The browser cannot produce this (JSON.stringify(NaN) → null), so exploitation requires an authenticated admin/sales_rep calling PostgREST directly — but the same was true of the bulk-import variants that were treated as publication blockers, and the poisoned NaN silently breaks Net Position, AR aging and commission math for everyone afterwards. This is 'the DB does not enforce X' — verified against migrations on disk; live bodies not directly inspected.

**Evidence.** 20260614142939:120: `+ COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0)` — no finiteness check anywhere in the body; :172-175: `UPDATE inventory SET quantity_prebooked = quantity_prebooked + (v_item->>'quantity')::numeric`. 20260730235031:342: `COALESCE((v_item->>'total_price')::numeric, 0)`. Repo-wide guard grep hits only: 20260805220757_reject_nonfinite_bulk_import_values.sql, 20260806*.sql, and two supplier-pricing files. Section-04 doc: 'The first exact-commit Sol review correctly blocked publication because PostgreSQL accepts non-finite numeric values.'

**Failure scenario.** A signed-in sales rep (or any script holding a rep JWT) POSTs rpc/create_direct_order with items:[{product_id:..., quantity:"NaN", price_per_unit:"NaN"}]. Order is created with NaN totals; inventory.quantity_prebooked becomes NaN for that product (NaN >= 0 passes the CHECK), Net Position and every subsequent aggregate over that inventory row becomes NaN, and a pending commission row carries NaN. Dashboards and reports render NaN/blank; cleanup requires manual SQL.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED (with one evidence correction). Verified independently against migrations on disk; no live DB access used.

CLAIM VERIFIED — no guard exists:
1. create_direct_order's last definition is supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:49. No later migration redefines it — `grep -rn "FUNCTION public.create_direct_order"` ends at that file, and the only later file naming it, 20260805211951_harden_bulk_order_import_lifecycle.sql:7, is a prose comment ("downstream work as create_direct_order"). The body contains zero finiteness checks and the quoted evidence holds verbatim: :118-121 `v_total_price := v_total_price + COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0);`; skip test at :97-98 `v_qty := COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0); IF v_qty <= 0 THEN CONTINUE;` (NaN <= 0 is false in Postgres, so no skip); inventory write at :172-175 `UPDATE inventory SET quantity_prebooked = quantity_prebooked + (v_item->>'quantity')::numeric`; ledger insert :180-187; `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_order(...)` :190-193. Still reachable: :225 `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO authenticated`, role gate limited to admin/sales_rep at :78-82.
2. save_quote's last definition is 20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:86 (grant at :1279 to authenticated). No finiteness check anywhere in the file — the repo-wide 'NaN'/'Infinity' grep does not match it.

NO FAIL-CLOSED MECHANISM EXISTS ELSEWHERE — every candidate checked:
- `chk_inventory_qty_prebooked CHECK (quantity_prebooked >= 0)` (20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:20) and `orders_total_price_non_negative CHECK (total_price >= 0)` (20260228310000_high_priority_fixes.sql:550) are both satisfied by NaN (NaN sorts greater than all finite values).
- The only bigint cast on this path, `_snapshot_order_item_cost` (20260513050000_order_items_cost_at_time_snapshot.sql:44-46) `SELECT ROUND(current_cost * 100)::bigint ... FROM public.products`, reads products.current_cost, not caller input — it cannot raise "cannot convert NaN to bigint" on a poisoned payload. There is no other numeric→bigint cast on the direct-order path.
- Commissions do not fail closed: latest `_insert_commissions_for_order` (20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:404) routes through `compute_commission_amount`, defined at 20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql:169-178 as `SELECT GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_profit,0) * COALESCE(p_percentage,0) / 100, 2), 0);` — all numeric, NaN propagates into commissions.commission_amount and order_profit.
- `trg_recalc_order_totals` (20260311200000_invoice_ar_single_source.sql:41-83) re-aggregates orders totals from order_items in pure numeric with ROUND(), re-writing NaN rather than rejecting it.
- No `CREATE DOMAIN` exists anywhere in supabase/migrations, so no domain-level finiteness constraint.

GENUINE COVERAGE GAP, NOT MISSED ENFORCEMENT: identical guards exist on sibling paths — receive_po_items at 20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:154 (`IF v_qty::text IN ('NaN', 'Infinity', '-Infinity') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Receiving quantity must be finite';`), blend-ticket table CHECKs at 20260714230100:107-117, supplier pricing at 20260717112011:67 and 20260718124517:269, bulk import at 20260805220757:116-124. The pattern was applied per-RPC and never reached create_direct_order or save_quote.

NOT IN THE BASELINE: KNOWN_ISSUES.md mentions non-finite only as fixed for bulk import (line 3: "imports are ... non-finite-safe"). The 2026-08-05 section-04 doc scopes it to bulk_import_order (:56) and cites create_direct_order at :38 only as the canonical reference implementation, without flagging this gap. Beyond baseline, not a re-report.

EVIDENCE CORRECTION (the one thing the reviewer got wrong): for save_quote the cited :342 `COALESCE((v_item->>'total_price')::numeric, 0)` is NOT the live poisoning vector — that inserted value is overwritten in the same call by the server-side recompute at :423 `total_price = f.total_price`, and the quote header totals come from the SUM at :469/:497. The real NaN vector is the other caller-supplied numerics feeding the recompute CTE: :331 `(v_item->>'price_override')::numeric` (becomes `ppu` via `COALESCE(qi.price_override, ...)` at :361), :334 actual_rate (`ar`), :338 acres (`ac`), :339 total_units_needed (`client_units`). `ROUND(ppu * total_units, 2)` yields NaN, and the guard branches `WHEN ppu * total_units > 0` and `AND ac > 0` all evaluate TRUE for NaN, so the poisoned value is stored and copied onward by convert_quote_to_order. Conclusion unchanged; only the quoted line is misattributed.

SEVERITY: keep MED. Requires an authenticated admin/sales_rep (role gate at 20260614142939:78-82) crafting a non-browser PostgREST call — the UI cannot emit it since JSON.stringify(NaN) is null — so this is an insider data-integrity/cleanup hazard, not anon-reachable and not privilege escalation. Not BLOCKER/HIGH.

### [MED] quotes.total_cost is stored unrounded and quote vs order use different profit formulas — total_price − total_cost ≠ total_profit, and quote→order conversion changes the commission basis by cents on first item touch

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:466-472, 497-499; supabase/migrations/20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:204-224; supabase/migrations/20260702172500_layer2_convert_quote_to_order_job_aware.sql:157-161, 207-208; supabase/migrations/20260617123503_refresh_item_profit_on_sameproduct_order_edit.sql:7-9

**Detail.** save_quote sets quotes.total_price = SUM(per-line 2dp-rounded total_price) and total_profit = SUM(per-line 2dp-rounded (ppu−cc)×units), but total_cost = SUM(current_cost × total_units_needed) with NO rounding — products.current_cost can carry >2 decimals and units are 2dp, so quotes.total_cost stores sub-cent dollars and the identity total_price − total_cost = total_profit does not hold exactly. The order side uses a different formula entirely: trg_recalc_order_totals computes total_profit = ROUND(SUM(line total_price) − SUM(cost×units), 2) (difference-of-sums) rather than the quote's sum-of-rounded-line-profits. convert_quote_to_order copies the quote's totals verbatim into orders and mints commissions from v_quote.total_profit (20260702172500:207-208); the first subsequent order_items touch fires the trigger and rewrites orders.total_profit under the other formula, so the stored profit — and any later commission recompute from it — shifts by cents relative to the minted basis. No fin-* sweep covers these header identities (docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md §4b lists 'order/quote total_profit, net_margin_pct, per-line commissions still unwritten' — the missing sweep is known; the formula inconsistency itself is not listed anywhere).

**Evidence.** 20260730235031:467-469: `COALESCE(SUM(total_price), 0) AS total_price, COALESCE(SUM(current_cost * total_units_needed), 0) AS total_cost, COALESCE(SUM(profit), 0) AS total_profit` — no ROUND on total_cost; :497-499 stores them verbatim. 20260209200000:206-212,218-221: `SUM(COALESCE(cost_per_unit,0) * total_units_needed) ... v_total_profit := v_total_price - v_total_cost; ... total_profit = ROUND(v_total_profit, 2)`. 20260702172500:207-208: `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_order(v_order_id, v_quote.customer_id, v_quote.total_profit, ...)`.

**Failure scenario.** Quote with lines whose per-line profits round individually to a total 2 cents higher than (rounded total price − unrounded total cost): commissions are minted at convert from the quote-formula profit; an admin later edits any order line quantity, the trigger recomputes profit under the order formula, and the commission-recompute path (20260617115903) reprices pending commissions from a basis that differs by cents from what the same economic order minted originally — penny-level answer-depends-on-path drift in commission liability.

**Verifier verdict.** NOT REFUTED — the mechanism is real, and the project's own 2026-08-06 work corroborates it on the sibling path. Two corrections to the finding, one of which makes it worse than described.

VERIFIED FACTS:

1) Quote header math (confirmed, current source). supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:408-409 rounds each line: `ROUND(ppu * total_units, 2) AS total_price, ROUND((ppu - cc) * total_units, 2) AS profit`. The header rollup at :467-469 is `COALESCE(SUM(total_price), 0) AS total_price, COALESCE(SUM(current_cost * total_units_needed), 0) AS total_cost, COALESCE(SUM(profit), 0) AS total_profit` — total_cost carries NO ROUND, stored verbatim at :497-499. quote_items.current_cost and total_units_needed are unconstrained `numeric` (20260206172436_create_full_schema_v2.sql:180,187), and units are themselves 2dp (`ROUND(client_units, 2)`, :387-388), so cost x units yields 4 decimals — sub-cent arises even with a whole-cent product cost. The identity total_price - total_cost = total_profit does not hold exactly. This is a DB-level fact checked against the migrations, not an "I could not find enforcement" claim: no ROUND, no CHECK, and no later migration touches save_quote after 20260730235031 (latest ledger file is 20260807220323).

2) Order-side formula (confirmed, but the reviewer's citation is STALE). 20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:184 is superseded by 20260311200000_invoice_ar_single_source.sql:41-84, which is the current trg_recalc_order_totals. The rewrite only removed the balance_due line; the arithmetic is byte-identical difference-of-sums: `COALESCE(SUM(COALESCE(cost_per_unit,0) * total_units_needed), 0) ... v_total_profit := v_total_price - v_total_cost; ... total_profit = ROUND(v_total_profit, 2)`. Substance stands; cite 20260311200000:60-78, not 20260209200000.

3) The failure scenario is MECHANICALLY WRONG, in the reviewer's favor. The reviewer says drift appears on "the first subsequent order_items touch." It appears immediately, inside convert_quote_to_order itself. 20260702172500:167-181 does `INSERT INTO order_items (...) SELECT ... qi.total_price, qi.profit, ...`, and `CREATE TRIGGER after_order_items_change AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE ON order_items FOR EACH ROW` (20260209200000:231-233, function rewritten by 20260311200000) fires on every one of those inserts. So orders.total_profit has ALREADY been rewritten under the difference-of-sums formula by the time line :207 runs `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_order(v_order_id, v_quote.customer_id, v_quote.total_profit, ...)`. _insert_commissions_for_order (current def: 20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:404-424) stores the passed value verbatim as commissions.order_profit. Result: commissions.order_profit != orders.total_profit from the moment of conversion, and SUM(order_items.profit) != orders.total_profit as well, since the lines carry the quote's per-line-rounded values. The later-edit recompute (20260617115903:261-262, `SET order_profit = ROUND(COALESCE(o.total_profit,0),2), commission_amount = compute_commission_amount(o.total_profit, c.split_percentage)`; still reached via the 20260714221000 wrapper -> _update_order_items_impl) then silently corrects the basis on the first edit. So the drift is real but the direction is "minted wrong, healed on edit," not "minted right, drifts on edit."

4) NO superseding fix exists for this path. Checked every convert_quote_to_order definition: the last body is 20260702172500; 20260730235031:986-993 only renames it to _convert_quote_to_order_owner_impl and wraps it with a (uuid,uuid,text,bigint) row-version guard. No later migration reconciles the commission basis on the convert path.

5) NOT covered by the baseline — and the baseline explicitly (and wrongly) cleared it. The identical bug class was found and fixed on the OTHER order-creation path: 20260806004644_bind_bulk_import_intent_and_profit.sql:327-345 comments "Item triggers own the canonical order totals. Read them only after the last insert, then make the item-profit aggregate agree exactly with the stored header before creating commission liability," then does `UPDATE order_items SET profit = profit + (v_total_profit - v_line_profit_sum) WHERE id = v_last_item_id` and mints from the reread v_total_profit. The gauntlet section-04 doc line 59 records this as a Sol BLOCKER: "fractional multi-line imports could create commissions from a stale per-line-rounded profit accumulator." The SAME doc, line 11, asserts "The normal quote, order, delivery, invoice, and payment paths reviewed in this section were otherwise aligned with the live lifecycle constraints" — i.e. the baseline affirmatively cleared convert_quote_to_order for the exact defect it blocked publication over on bulk_import_order. KNOWN_ISSUES.md line 1032 lists only the MISSING SWEEP ("order/quote total_profit, net_margin_pct, per-line commissions still unwritten"), not the formula inconsistency. The reviewer characterized the documentation state correctly.

SEVERITY CORRECTED LOW -> MED. Bounded to roughly n/2 cents for an n-line quote (each line contributes at most half a cent of rounding residue), non-escalating, and self-healing on the first order edit — so not HIGH. MED rather than LOW because (a) convert_quote_to_order is the PRIMARY order-creation path, far higher volume than bulk import; (b) the project treated this exact defect as publication-blocking on the low-volume path and shipped a dedicated migration for it, so the risk appetite is already established; (c) it writes a real commission-liability figure (commissions.order_profit) that disagrees with the stored order header from creation, and the same economic order pays out differently depending on whether anyone happened to edit a line first.
