### [MED] Reports quick-pay deterministic idempotency key silently replays a voided payment batch for 24 hours

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** src/pages/Reports.tsx:483-487; supabase/migrations/20260210000000_tier3_idempotency_and_triggers.sql:23; supabase/migrations/20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:31-44

**Detail.** handleMarkPaid builds a content-derived idempotency key from the selected commission ids. The code comment justifies the deterministic key with 'These commission ids transition to paid on success, so the key can't collide with a future payment' — that is wrong: create_commission_payment only batches them (status stays pending); they become paid only when the batch is POSTED, and void_commission_payment resets live-lineage rows back to pending. So within the 24-hour idempotency window (idempotency_keys.expires_at defaults to now()+24h; check_idempotency deletes only expired keys), the sequence quick-pay → void the batch → quick-pay the same commissions again generates the identical key, and the RPC replays the cached ORIGINAL payment id — the id of the now-voided batch. No new batch is created, yet the frontend asserts the id and toasts success ('N commission(s) added to M unposted payment batch(es)').

**Evidence.** Reports.tsx:485-487: `// creating duplicate commission payments. These commission ids transition\n// to 'paid' on success, so the key can't collide with a future payment.\np_idempotency_key: \`reports-commission-pay-${ids.join('-')}\`` — check_idempotency (20260714230000:31-38) deletes only `WHERE ... expires_at < now()` then returns the stored result; idempotency_keys DDL (20260210000000:23): `expires_at timestamptz DEFAULT now() + interval '24 hours'` — void_commission_payment resets non-dead-lineage batch rows to pending (20260707060000:1624-1654).

**Failure scenario.** Admin quick-pays rep's commissions A,B from Reports (batch P-051 created). Ten minutes later realizes the payment method should be a check with a reference number, voids P-051 (A,B reset to pending), re-selects A,B and clicks Create Payment Batch. The RPC replays P-051's id from the idempotency cache; nothing is created; the UI reports success. The rep's commissions sit pending with no batch until someone notices; retrying the same day keeps hitting the same cached key.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every element of the chain verified against current source; no superseding migration or guard exists.

1. The key is deterministic and content-derived. src/pages/Reports.tsx:477-491: `supabase.rpc('create_commission_payment', { ... p_idempotency_key: `reports-commission-pay-${ids.join('-')}` })`, with the comment at :485-487 `// These commission ids transition\n// to 'paid' on success, so the key can't collide with a future payment.`

2. The comment is factually wrong on current code. The latest create_commission_payment (supabase/migrations/20260714180000_harden_commission_payment_creation.sql:69-250 — I confirmed by grepping every migration mentioning the name that no later file redefines it) inserts the batch as `'unposted'` (`VALUES (v_payment_num, v_recipient_id, v_total, 'unposted', ...)`) and never touches `commissions.status`. Commissions become `'paid'` only in post_commission_payment (20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql, `SET status = 'paid' ... AND status = 'pending'`). The archived rationale confirms the assumption is stale drift, not a typo: docs/archive/2026-spring/2026-05-28-codex-p1-p2-remediation-prompt.md:38 records the deliberate removal of `Date.now()`, and docs/archive/2026-spring/2026-05-27-graph-workflow-analysis.md:150 states the then-true model "create_commission_payment ... advances commissions from pending to paid" — a model retired by the 2026-06-20 batch-freeze/post model.

3. The replay returns before every validation. 20260714180000:110-115: `IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL THEN v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key,'create_commission_payment'); IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN (v_existing #>> '{}')::uuid; END IF;` — so the stale-selection, already-in-non-voided-payment, recipient, and period guards never run on a replay.

4. check_idempotency really does replay for 24h. Latest definition is 20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-52 (verified as the last of four definitions; nothing later): `DELETE FROM public.idempotency_keys WHERE idempotency_key = p_key AND expires_at < now();` then `IF FOUND THEN RETURN v_existing.result;`. Same operation string, so the cross-op guard at :39-44 does not fire. Table default is unchanged: 20260210000000_tier3_idempotency_and_triggers.sql:22 `expires_at timestamptz DEFAULT now() + interval '24 hours'`. The 2026-08-03 intent-binding hardening does NOT cover this RPC — supabase/migrations/20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql only wraps save_invoice (:26,:33) and create_quick_delivery (:183,:190) with a request_fingerprint; create_commission_payment is untouched, so no payload/actor fingerprint blocks the replay.

5. The void half of the scenario is real and leaves the key intact. void_commission_payment (latest body: supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:1569-1720) accepts `'posted'` or `'unposted'` (`IF v_payment.status NOT IN ('posted','unposted')`), sets the batch `'voided'`, and resets live-lineage rows: `UPDATE commissions c SET status = 'pending', paid_date = NULL WHERE c.id IN (SELECT commission_id FROM commission_payment_items ...)`. It deletes nothing from idempotency_keys.

6. The re-selection is reachable in the UI. Reports.tsx:213-222 `supabase.from('commissions').select('*').order('order_date',{ascending:false})` — no batch/voided filter, so the reset-to-pending rows reappear and pass the client-side `status !== 'pending'` gate at :458-462, producing the identical id list and identical key. Absent the replay the recreate would legitimately succeed: the duplicate guard at 20260714180000:175-186 excludes voided batches (`WHERE cp.status IS DISTINCT FROM 'voided'`).

7. Failure is silent. `assertRpcResult<string>(data,'create_commission_payment')` (Reports.tsx:490) only rejects null/undefined, so the voided batch's uuid passes; the success toast (:494) and the `commission_payment_batch_created` activity_feed row (:495) both fire with no batch created.

Contrast with the canonical page, which is not affected: src/pages/CommissionPayments.tsx:56 uses `useIdempotencyKey('create_commission_payment', profile?.id)` (src/hooks/useIdempotencyKey.ts — random per-intent key, reset on success), so it is the working recovery path.

Not in the baselines: no mention of `reports-commission-pay`, quick-pay, or this replay in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md or docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md (the only commission content there is bulk-order-import commission creation).

Severity held at MED, not raised: no money corruption, no duplicate payout, no wrong balance; the damage is a silent no-op reported as success plus a false activity_feed entry, self-limiting because the refetched list still shows the commissions pending, recoverable immediately via CommissionPayments.tsx, and bounded to 24h. Two aggravators keep it above LOW: the key omits payment_method/reference/payment_date/notes, so even a legitimately different-intent recreate is deduped, and the stale in-code comment actively misleads future maintainers about the commission lifecycle.

Minor citation correction: the void reset UPDATE is at 20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:1622-1654 (reviewer wrote 1624-1654), and the idempotency_keys `expires_at` default is line 22, not 23.

### [MED] jobs.commission_split is directly writable by any admin/sales_rep with no validation, no audit, and no lock — the job channel's money-routing window is unguarded between scheduling and invoicing

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260430180000_field_app_workflow_phase5.sql:28-32; supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:96-118, 630-673

**Detail.** Unlike the order channel (commissions minted at creation, so later split edits are inert), the job channel mints commissions from jobs.commission_split at transfer_job_to_invoice time. U8 deliberately snapshots the split at job creation so 'a later quote or customer split edit must not re-attribute an already-scheduled job' — but the snapshot is only protected against re-reading live sources, not against direct row edits. jobs_update RLS allows any active admin or sales_rep to UPDATE any jobs row (no ownership scoping), jobs has no BEFORE UPDATE trigger validating or freezing commission_split (only set_jobs_updated_at and the BEFORE INSERT snapshot trigger exist), no financial_audit_log is written for the change, and save_job never touches commission_split so no RPC-side guard applies. Two consequences: (a) a sales rep can, via a direct PostgREST UPDATE, re-attribute a colleague's scheduled job's future commission to themselves before invoicing, with a valid-looking 100% split that passes the mint's validator, leaving zero audit trail; (b) an invalid split written directly (nothing validates at write time) fails closed only at invoicing with COMMISSION_SPLIT_INVALID, blocking billing at the worst moment. Contrast: quotes get a write-time validator trigger (20260719064000) plus save_quote's split-conflict guard and audit; customers get save_customer validation; jobs get nothing. Distinct from the known D3 item (applicator READ visibility of jobs.commission_split) and from KNOWN_ISSUES 1b (name-vs-id identity).

**Evidence.** jobs_update (20260430180000:29-32): `CREATE POLICY jobs_update ON jobs FOR UPDATE USING (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()) WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep());` — only triggers on jobs across all migrations: `set_jobs_updated_at` (20260215200000:51) and `trg_jobs_snapshot_commission_split BEFORE INSERT` (20260707060000:116-118). U8 comment (20260707060000:86-87): 'commission split snapshot ... locked at job creation'; mint reads the row live at invoicing (:630 `v_commission_split := v_job.commission_split;` → :668 `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_job(...)`).

**Failure scenario.** Job J-500 scheduled from rep Alice's quote carries {splits:[{recipient:'Alice',percentage:100}]}. Rep Bob issues `PATCH /rest/v1/jobs?id=eq.<J-500>` with commission_split={splits:[{recipient:'Bob',recipient_user_id:'<bob>',percentage:100}]} using his own JWT — jobs_update permits it, nothing validates or logs it. Weeks later the job completes and is invoiced: _insert_commissions_for_job validates Bob's split as well-formed and mints 100% of the chemical profit to Bob. No audit row exists to show the split ever changed.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — I tried to refute it and could not. The reviewer's supporting trigger inventory is materially wrong, but every load-bearing fact and the failure scenario survive independent verification.

WHAT I CHECKED AND CONFIRMED
1. RLS is exactly as quoted. Final state of jobs_update is `supabase/migrations/20260430180000_field_app_workflow_phase5.sql:28-32`: `CREATE POLICY jobs_update ON jobs FOR UPDATE USING (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()) WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep());`. Only two migrations ever create jobs_update (20260215200000:137, superseded by 20260430180000:28); no later migration re-scopes it. No ownership predicate — any active sales_rep can UPDATE any jobs row.
2. jobs.commission_split is reachable over PostgREST. `src/types/supabase.ts:5626` (Row), `:5673` (Insert), `:5720` (Update) all expose `commission_split: Json | null` on the `jobs` table.
3. The mint reads the row live at invoicing. `20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:630` `v_commission_split := v_job.commission_split;` → `:668` `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_job(...)`, and `_insert_commissions_for_job` (`:150`) only runs `PERFORM public.validate_commission_split_json(p_commission_split)` — a well-formedness check, not an authorization or provenance check. A valid 100%-to-Bob split mints 100% to Bob.
4. No audit on the direct write. Every financial_audit_log row touching jobs is written inside an RPC body (20260622020000:291, 20260625120000:138, 20260707140000:432/723/866, etc.). There is no audit trigger on the jobs table, so a raw PATCH leaves no ledger row.
5. save_job genuinely never touches commission_split. Latest definition is in `20260706080000_customer_supplied_chemicals.sql`; the file contains no `commission_split` reference at all. And `grep commission_split src/` shows no frontend write path to jobs — only quote/customer editors. So there is no legitimate app path that writes this column post-creation, which makes the open RLS write purely an unguarded hole rather than a feature.

WHERE THE REVIEWER IS WRONG (evidence defect, not verdict defect)
The claim "only triggers on jobs are set_jobs_updated_at and trg_jobs_snapshot_commission_split BEFORE INSERT" is false. jobs carries at least nine triggers: set_jobs_updated_at (20260215200000:51), enforce_job_status_transition (20260228300000:491, BEFORE UPDATE OF status), enforce_applicator_license (20260610185714:63), enforce_billed_job_immutability (20260625190000:164, BEFORE UPDATE — no column list), trg_release_job_holds_on_lifecycle (20260702174000:289), trg_sync_job_location_dispatch_on_applicator_change (20260707020000:328), trg_close_job_location_dispatch_on_job_terminal (20260707020000:363), trg_jobs_snapshot_commission_split (20260707060000:116), trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids (20260722174029:639, BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF commission_split).

Two of those are the strongest refutation candidates, and both fail to close the hole:
- enforce_billed_job_immutability is a BEFORE UPDATE guard with no column list, but `_enforce_billed_job_immutability()` opens with `IF OLD.status NOT IN ('invoiced','cancelled') THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;` (20260625190000:88-90). The finding's window is scheduled/in_progress/completed, so the guard early-returns and never fires.
- trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids does fire on UPDATE OF commission_split, but `stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids()` (20260722174029:610-622) only does `NEW.commission_split := public.commission_split_with_recipient_ids(NEW.commission_split)` — it enriches names into ids. It validates nothing and blocks nothing; it would helpfully stamp Bob's own uuid onto Bob's forged split.

THE CONTRAST IS UNDERSTATED, NOT OVERSTATED
The reviewer said quotes get a validator trigger and jobs get nothing. It is worse than that: quotes_update is ownership-scoped for reps — `20260206174345_fix_security_and_performance_issues.sql`: `USING (is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND created_by = (select auth.uid())))`. Bob cannot direct-write Alice's quote split at all. jobs_update has no such scoping, so the identical attack that is structurally impossible on the quote channel is wide open on the job channel.

THE MECHANISM IS LIVE-PROVEN PRECEDENT IN THIS REPO
`20260625190000_guard_billed_job_immutability.sql:5-19` states the defect was "live-proven by the reviewer as a non-admin sales_rep under RLS" who "could soft-delete an `invoiced` job ... AND rewrite its billed-relevant columns (e.g. total_price_cents, customer_id) ... while bypassing the UI." Raw REST UPDATE on jobs by a sales_rep is not hypothetical here — it has been demonstrated against this exact table.

ONE CORRECTION THAT WIDENS THE FINDING
commission_split is absent from the immutability guard's equality allow-list in its latest revision (`20260629120000_guard_billed_job_immutability_invoice_id.sql:102-110` — the chain ends at loader_tank_capacity/invoice_id). The column was added later, by U8 (20260707060000). The guard's header asserts "New columns are protected by default (they are not in the exempt set below)", but the implementation is an explicit AND-chain of named columns, so an unlisted column is simply never compared. Consequence: the window is not even closed after invoicing. Since void_invoice returns a job to completed and re-invoicing re-mints from jobs.commission_split (per KNOWN_ISSUES.md:949 describing exactly that revival path), a split rewritten on an invoiced job is not inert.

NOT ALREADY KNOWN
KNOWN_ISSUES.md:993 and :1020 (Sprint D "D3") concern applicator READ visibility of jobs.commission_split — a different axis. KNOWN_ISSUES.md:941-949 and :1040/:1053 concern name-vs-id recipient identity and the name-reuse guard family, none of which gate who may write a job's split. The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline is entirely bulk_import_order lifecycle; grep for "commission" there returns only order-channel import findings. Neither baseline covers this.

SEVERITY
Keeping MED. Impact is silent internal money re-routing with zero audit trail, and the write window extends past invoicing — which argues up. But exploitation requires an insider sales rep deliberately using raw REST/PostgREST rather than the UI, and the loss is commission misattribution between employees rather than customer-facing money. The repo's own precedent is the tiebreaker: the structurally identical hole fixed by 20260625190000 was itself rated "Medium, pre-existing & app-wide" in that migration's header. MED is the consistent rating.

---

### [MED] void_invoice's order-channel commission cancel is missing every sibling protection: no batch-freeze guard, amount not zeroed, no paid-commission review notification

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260718221505_preserve_voided_payment_allocation_history.sql:250-256 vs 258-281; supabase/migrations/20260620130000_commission_batch_freeze_guard.sql:93-103, 872-883

**Detail.** Every other commission-reversal path carries three protections: (1) hard-block when a pending commission sits in an active payout batch (ORDER_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS in cancel_order/void_order/cancel_delivery; JOB_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS in the job branches of the very same void_invoice function), (2) zero commission_amount on cancel, (3) notify admins when paid commissions exist for the dying entity. The order-channel branch of void_invoice has none of them: it cancels pending rows without checking batch membership (only the later post_commission_payment BATCH_COMMISSION_DRIFT backstop prevents the resurrect-to-paid money loss — the batch fails to post with a confusing error and must be voided and rebuilt instead of the void being refused up front), it leaves the cancelled rows carrying their full dollar amounts (every sibling path writes `commission_amount = 0`; any report or export summing amounts without a status filter over-counts), and paid commissions on the voided invoice's order generate no commission_review notification while the identical situation on a job invoice notifies every active admin.

**Evidence.** Order branch (20260718221505:253): `UPDATE commissions SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE order_id = v_inv.order_id AND status = 'pending';` (no batch EXISTS check, no `commission_amount = 0`, no paid-row notification) — versus the job branch 25 lines later (:259-279): `IF EXISTS (... cpi ... cp.status <> 'voided') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'JOB_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS...'` / paid check → INSERT INTO notifications / `UPDATE commissions SET status = 'cancelled', commission_amount = 0` — and versus cancel_order (20260620130000:93-103): `RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS: ...'`.

**Failure scenario.** Rep's pending commissions for order O-77 are batched into unposted payment P-9. Admin voids O-77's posted invoice: void succeeds (no guard), commissions flip to cancelled while still inside P-9. Days later admin posts P-9 and gets 'BATCH_COMMISSION_DRIFT: 2 of 2 batched commission(s) are no longer pending' with no hint why, and must void and rebuild the batch — where the same flow via cancel_order would have been refused up front with a clear ORDER_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS instruction. Meanwhile the cancelled rows retain non-zero commission_amount values.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — I attempted to refute this on four fronts (superseding migration, trigger-level enforcement, upstream unreachability, already-documented) and all four failed.

1. Currency of the cited body. `20260718221505_preserve_voided_payment_allocation_history.sql:4` is the last `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.void_invoice` on disk (verified by grepping every migration that mentions void_invoice). The later `20260720175946_protect_governed_split_edit_and_void_group.sql:92-134` only `ALTER FUNCTION public.void_invoice(uuid,text,text) RENAME TO _void_invoice_group_guard_impl_20260720` and creates a thin wrapper that raises `SPLIT_INVOICE_GROUP_VOID_REQUIRED` for governed split members then `PERFORM public._void_invoice_group_guard_impl_20260720(...)` — the cited body is still what executes. `20260721014858`, `20260722112835`, `20260803221244`, `20260805151605` reference void_invoice only in md5 pre/postflight assertions or comments; none redefines it.

2. Order-branch text is exactly as quoted. `20260718221505:250-256`:
   `IF v_inv.order_id IS NOT NULL THEN / IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM invoices WHERE order_id = v_inv.order_id AND id != p_invoice_id AND status NOT IN ('voided','cancelled') AND deleted_at IS NULL) THEN / UPDATE commissions SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE order_id = v_inv.order_id AND status = 'pending';`
   No batch-membership EXISTS check, no `commission_amount = 0`, no paid-commission notification. The job branch 3 lines later (`:258-281`) has all three: `RAISE EXCEPTION 'JOB_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS: ...'`, the `INSERT INTO notifications ... 'commission_review'` block, and `UPDATE commissions SET status = 'cancelled', commission_amount = 0`. I read the full function body (lines 1-320): the only other guards are admin role, invoice status, `INVOICE_HAS_APPLIED_PAYMENTS` (`:135-140`), and `check_period_open`. Nothing batch-aware.

3. "The DB does not enforce it" — checked, not merely unfound. There is no trigger on `commissions` protecting batched rows: the only `CREATE TRIGGER` statements touching commissions are `trg_validate_quote_commission_split` (20260719064000:142), `trg_commissions_recipient_resolved` (20260722134252:221), `trg_jobs_snapshot_commission_split` (20260707060000:116), and `trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids` (20260722174029:627-645) — split-validation/identity only. `20260620130000_commission_batch_freeze_guard.sql`'s own header states the fix is two-sided and names only cancel_order / void_order / cancel_delivery + post_commission_payment; void_invoice was never included. The later `ORDER_HAS_BATCHED_COMMISSIONS` at `20260721014858:987` belongs to `_close_undelivered_order_remainder_20260718`, a different function.

4. Reachability. I tried to kill the scenario by showing a batch can only contain commissions on paid invoices — it cannot. `create_commission_payment` (`20260714180000:139-183`) requires only that each selected commission still exist, be `deleted_at IS NULL AND status = 'pending'`, share one recipient, and not already sit in a non-voided payment; nothing ties eligibility to invoice payment state. And order commissions are minted at order creation (`_insert_commissions_for_order` called from convert_quote_to_order / create_direct_order / draw-down, e.g. `20260610145253:303,517`), not at posting. So pending → batched → invoice voided (with `paid_amount_cents = 0`, so the `INVOICE_HAS_APPLIED_PAYMENTS` guard does not fire) is a real sequence. `post_commission_payment` (`20260620130000:862-883`) then raises exactly the quoted `BATCH_COMMISSION_DRIFT: % of % batched commission(s) are no longer pending (cancelled or already paid). Void and rebuild this commission payment.` — money is protected, the operator is not.

5. The non-zeroed amount has a concrete consumer, stronger than the reviewer's hedge. `get_commission_balance_report` (`20260330100000_prelaunch_state_machine_and_security.sql:795`) computes `total_earned` as `ROUND(SUM(cm.commission_amount)::numeric, 2)` with NO status filter (only `total_paid`/`outstanding_balance` are status-filtered), and no later migration redefines it. Because every other cancel path zeroes, cancelled rows normally contribute 0; rows cancelled by void_invoice's order branch inflate an admin money report's total_earned. `src/pages/Reports.tsx:214` also loads `commissions.select('*')` unfiltered into the table/CSV export (`:548`), though its `unpaidTotal` (`:762`) does filter to pending. Cross-check confirms the asymmetry is unique to this branch: of 36 `UPDATE commissions SET status='cancelled'` statements across migrations, the only ones omitting `commission_amount = 0` are successive versions of this same void_invoice order branch (20260529214538:353, 20260706110000:180, 20260707060000:877, 20260707140000:964, 20260711050000:405, 20260712160000:250, 20260718154810:261, 20260718213305:251, 20260718221505:253).

6. Notification asymmetry is real. cancel_order (`20260620130000:225-246`) does `UPDATE commissions SET status='cancelled', commission_amount = 0`, then counts `status='paid'` rows and inserts a `cancellation_review` notification per active admin; the job branch of void_invoice itself inserts `commission_review`. The order branch does neither, and the defense that "the order is still alive so it can be re-invoiced" fails because the job branch is in the identical situation (job returns to `completed`, re-invoiceable) and still notifies + zeroes. Order commissions are also not re-minted on re-invoice (creation is at order creation), so the cancel is terminal for that order's commissions.

7. Not already documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` records the commission-resurrection class as CLOSED via `20260620130000` and `20260622070000` (lines 925, 937, 1072) — both scoped to cancel/void order, cancel_delivery, post_commission_payment, and void_commission_payment, none to void_invoice. The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline discusses commissions only in the bulk-order-import context. This gap is beyond both baselines.

Severity: MED is correct, not inflated. No money can be paid out (the BATCH_COMMISSION_DRIFT backstop holds) so it is not HIGH; but it is more than cosmetic because `get_commission_balance_report.total_earned` returns an inflated figure for the affected recipient and an admin gets an unexplained batch failure days later plus no paid-commission review signal. One nuance worth passing to the fixer, which the finding does not mention: `void_commission_payment` (`20260622070000`) resets batched commissions to `pending` when the ORDER is still live — and after a void_invoice-driven cancel the order IS still live, so voiding the batch silently resurrects commissions that void_invoice deliberately cancelled. Adding a bare `commission_amount = 0` here without also fixing that reset would turn today's over-count into a silent zero-dollar resurrection, so the fix should be the batch-freeze guard first.

### [MED] update_order_items idempotent replay is not payload-bound — edited items silently reported as saved on retry after a lost response

- **Finder:** `idempotency-concurrency`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260714221000_order_and_cycle_count_concurrency_guards.sql:37-40; src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:501-523; supabase/migrations/20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql:1-8

**Detail.** The public update_order_items wrapper replays a cached receipt keyed only by (key, operation) — no request fingerprint, no actor binding, unlike save_invoice/create_quick_delivery which were hardened by 20260803010917 exactly against this class ('avoids… silently treating edited input as saved'). Checked all migrations: no later migration adds binding for update_order_items (grep for ARGUMENT_MISMATCH/request_fingerprint finds none naming it). Frontend keeps the same key on failure (useIdempotencyKey resets only on confirmed success, OrderDetail.tsx:523), which is correct for retries of the SAME payload but combines with the unbound replay to drop CHANGED payloads.

**Evidence.** 20260714221000:37-40: `IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL THEN\n    v_existing := public.check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'update_order_items');\n    IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN v_existing; END IF;\n  END IF;` — vs 20260803010917's stated rationale for save_invoice: "reusing that key after the form changes can replay a result for different data. Persist a PostgreSQL-derived fingerprint and fail closed". OrderDetail.tsx:501-506: `const idemKey = updateOrderIdem.getKey(); … supabase.rpc('update_order_items', { …, p_idempotency_key: idemKey })`; resetKey only at line 523 after success.

**Failure scenario.** Office user saves order-item edits; the RPC commits but the response is lost (network drop), so resetKey() never runs. User re-opens the editor, changes a quantity or price, clicks Save again. The same key is sent; the wrapper returns the first save's cached result before any mutation (20260714221000:39), assertRpcResult passes, and the UI toasts 'Order updated' — the second edit (money- and inventory-affecting: update_order_items adjusts prebooked inventory and totals) is silently discarded while being reported as saved.

**Verifier verdict.** NOT REFUTED as a mechanism, but the stated failure scenario is factually wrong in its main step and the impact is narrower than claimed, so severity drops HIGH → MED.

What holds up (verified myself):
1. Current definition of public.update_order_items is the wrapper in supabase/migrations/20260714221000_order_and_cycle_count_concurrency_guards.sql:11-89. Its replay is key+operation only: ":37-40 `IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL THEN v_existing := public.check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'update_order_items'); IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN v_existing; END IF; END IF;`" and it returns before the FOR UPDATE lock, the ORDER_ITEM_MISMATCH check, and the delegation to _update_order_items_impl at :85-87.
2. The DB genuinely does not enforce payload binding here (checked, not "couldn't find"): grep of every migration defining the function (`grep -n "FUNCTION public.update_order_items"`) shows the last redefinition is 20260714221000; the only later files naming it (20260716120112, 20260721014858, 20260721223817) mention it in comments only. The current shared helper public.check_idempotency (latest definition supabase/migrations/20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-52) compares only operation: ":40-43 `IF FOUND AND v_existing.operation IS DISTINCT FROM p_operation THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'IDEMPOTENCY_CROSS_OP_KEY_REUSE'`" then ":45-47 `IF FOUND THEN RETURN v_existing.result; END IF;`". No request_fingerprint/IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH/IDEMPOTENCY_ARGUMENT_MISMATCH migration names update_order_items. The impl writes its receipt only on commit (20260617123503_refresh_item_profit_on_sameproduct_order_edit.sql:317-318 `PERFORM save_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'update_order_items', v_result);`), so replay implies a real prior commit.
3. Not previously documented: no hit for "update_order_items" in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md or docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md, and the 2026-08-06 section-06 idempotency refresh lists 1 HIGH/3 MED/3 LOW none of which is this RPC.

What is refuted / corrected:
a. The failure scenario's central step — "User re-opens the editor … clicks Save again. The same key is sent" — is wrong. src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1159 resets the key on entering edit mode: `onClick={() => { updateOrderIdem.resetKey(); setEditing(true); }}`. Re-opening the editor always mints a new key, so that path cannot replay.
b. The only reachable path is narrower: the save fails (lost response after commit), runCriticalAction (src/lib/criticalAction.ts:46-49) only toasts and leaves `editing` true, the user edits a value in the still-open editor and clicks Save Changes again (OrderDetail.tsx:1148). Then getKey() (src/hooks/useIdempotencyKey.ts:29-38, ref keyed only by [operation, userId]) returns the same key and the wrapper replays.
c. Impact is smaller than "silently discarded": after the replayed success toast the handler runs `fetchOrder()` (OrderDetail.tsx:527), which repaints items from the DB, so the un-applied edit is visible on screen immediately. Nothing incorrect is written to money or inventory; the defect is a false "Order updated" confirmation plus a lost edit intent — the same class the codebase already fixed for price_order with a payload-hash reset (OrderDetail.tsx:1022-1040, "editing a price and resubmitting would replay the SAME key … silently discarding the edited prices") and for NewOrder's rushOrderIdem. update_order_items has no such payload-change reset (updateOrderIdem appears only at OrderDetail.tsx:61, 501, 523, 1159).

Net: real gap, correct root cause, wrong reproduction step, MED not HIGH.

---

### [MED] Orders and invoices have no row-version stale-write guard — quotes/customers equivalents were never extended; two office users get last-write-wins

- **Finder:** `idempotency-concurrency`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:10-13,63-71; supabase/migrations/20260714221000_order_and_cycle_count_concurrency_guards.sql:42-68; docs/manual/DECISION_LOG.md (no matching entry)

**Detail.** Checked the migrations: 20260730235031 adds row_version columns and bump triggers to exactly public.quotes and public.customers; no migration adds row_version (or any expected-version parameter) to orders, order_items, invoices, or invoice_items (`grep -rn "ADD COLUMN.*row_version" supabase/migrations` returns only the two 20260730235031 lines). update_order_items and save_invoice take row locks (serialization) but perform whole-collection replaces with no conflict detection, so the second writer wins silently. This is the direct answer to the row-version-equivalents question: THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE stale-write protection on orders/invoices (verified in migrations), and I did not find a settled decision excluding them — DECISION_LOG.md has zero row-version/stale/last-write entries about orders or invoices (grep), and KNOWN_ISSUES describes the 20260730 guard as closing the exposure only 'for whole-record save_quote and save_customer updates'.

**Evidence.** 20260730235031:10-13: `ALTER TABLE public.quotes ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS row_version…; ALTER TABLE public.customers ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS row_version…` — the only two tables. 20260714221000:42: `PERFORM 1 FROM public.orders WHERE id = p_order_id FOR UPDATE;` — lock only, no version compare anywhere in the wrapper or its call into `_update_order_items_impl`.

**Failure scenario.** Two office users open the same order's item editor. User A corrects a price and saves; user B (still holding the pre-edit rows) saves a quantity change seconds later. update_order_items locks and rewrites every supplied line from B's stale snapshot — A's price correction is silently reverted, affecting invoice totals and commission profit downstream. Identical exposure for two users editing the same draft invoice via save_invoice (its 20260803 fingerprint binds retries of one client's key, but does nothing across two different users' keys).

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim verified independently; no refutation angle held.

(1) DB-level absence verified two ways, not one. 20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:11-13 adds row_version to exactly public.quotes and public.customers. Beyond the reviewer's migration grep, live state agrees: .claude/schema-registry.json lists row_version under /columns/ only for customers, quotes, and pricing_workbook_export_rows — never orders, order_items, invoices, or invoice_items. So this is genuinely "the DB does not enforce X", not "I couldn't find where X is enforced".

(2) No later migration supersedes either path. Latest update_order_items is the 20260714221000 wrapper; lines 42-68 quoted accurately ("PERFORM 1 FROM public.orders WHERE id = p_order_id FOR UPDATE;" plus "FOR UPDATE OF oi"). Its only content-ish check is ORDER_ITEM_MISMATCH (lines 55-57), which asserts supplied ids belong to p_order_id — it never compares a field value to a client baseline. Latest save_invoice is 20260803010917:33; its fingerprint is gated behind "SELECT * FROM idempotency_keys WHERE idempotency_key = p_idempotency_key", so two users with two different keys never see each other's receipt. The reviewer's parenthetical about the 20260803 fingerprint was exactly right. Also searched for any trigger-level stale/conflict guard on these tables — none exists.

(3) Failure scenario is reachable in current source, not theoretical. src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:190 seeds editItems from the fetch snapshot; handleSaveEdits at lines 477-484 transmits every line including price_per_unit for lines the user never touched. The impl at 20260617123503_refresh_item_profit_on_sameproduct_order_edit.sql:218-222 writes "price_per_unit = (v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric" unconditionally, so B's stale payload silently reverts A's committed price correction, and profit/net_margin/total_price are recomputed from the reverted value. Invoice half is a literal whole-collection replace: 20260721223817_save_invoice_payment_terms.sql:161 "DELETE FROM invoice_items WHERE invoice_id = v_invoice_id;" then reinserts from payload, with header fields such as "purchase_order_ref = p_invoice->>'purchase_order_ref'" and header_notes/footer_notes overwritten with no ?-key presence guard. Neither OrderDetail.tsx nor InvoiceDetail.tsx sends any version/expected-version token (grep returned nothing).

(4) Not already documented in either baseline. KNOWN_ISSUES.md:898 is titled "RESOLVED LIVE — Whole-record lost updates on quote/customer saves" and its body scopes the closure to "whole-record save_quote and save_customer updates" — orders/invoices are neither covered nor excluded. The 2026-08-05 gauntlet section-04 refresh contains zero mentions of update_order_items or save_invoice concurrency. DECISION_LOG.md has zero row-version/stale-write/lost-update entries, and the guard migration's own header comment scopes itself to quote/customer with no deferral note for orders/invoices. So the reviewer's "no settled decision excludes them" is accurate.

Severity: MED confirmed, not raised. I specifically tested whether inventory could drift as a second-order effect — it cannot: the impl recomputes v_qty_diff against a freshly re-read v_old_item inside the locked transaction, so quantity_prebooked stays consistent with whichever value ultimately lands. No database invariant is violated and totals recompute coherently; the damage is bounded to the wrong user's value silently winning, which then propagates into invoice totals and commission profit. Real, money-visible, silent data loss — but concurrency-dependent and non-corrupting, which is squarely MED rather than HIGH.

---

### [MED] allocate_payment allocation_sets version assignment is check-then-act — concurrent payments for one customer abort with a raw unique-constraint error

- **Finder:** `idempotency-concurrency`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260713060300_harden_allocate_payment_idempotency.sql:139-152; supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:107-116

**Detail.** allocate_payment computes the next allocation-set version with `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version), 0) + 1 … WHERE entity_type='payment' AND entity_id = p_customer_id` and then INSERTs. Nothing serializes two concurrent payments for the same customer before this read (the advisory/UNIQUE idempotency claim serializes only same-key retries; different users use different keys; no customer-row lock or per-customer advisory lock is taken before the MAX read — invoice rows are locked later, and two payments to different invoices of one customer share no row lock at all). The UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version) constraint makes the race fail closed rather than corrupt: the loser blocks on the winner's index entry and then raises 23505.

**Evidence.** 20260713060300:139-141: `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version), 0) + 1 INTO v_next_version\n  FROM allocation_sets WHERE entity_type = 'payment' AND entity_id = p_customer_id;` then :142-152 INSERT. 20260213100000:116: `UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version)`.

**Failure scenario.** AR clerk and a sales rep both record payments for the same farm within the same moment (different invoices, different keys). Both read MAX(version)=N; both INSERT version N+1; the second transaction aborts with 'duplicate key value violates unique constraint "allocation_sets_entity_type_entity_id_version_key"' — a raw Postgres error surfaced to the user on the money path with no retry guidance; the entire payment (allocations, prepay) is rolled back and must be re-entered.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Code citations are accurate and no missed enforcement mechanism exists. (1) 20260713060300_harden_allocate_payment_idempotency.sql is the latest on-disk definition of allocate_payment — the three later migrations naming it (20260716120112_gauntlet_money_workflows.sql, 20260716160000_fix_idempotency_committed_replay_guard.sql, 20260718203206_retire_legacy_record_invoice_payment.sql) redefine save_invoice/get_customer_statement/_guard_idempotency_key_insert/record_invoice_payment, not allocate_payment. (2) The check-then-act is verbatim as quoted at :139-152. (3) The DB does not enforce serialization — checked, not merely unfound: the only customers read is `SELECT farm_name INTO v_customer_name FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id;` at :133 with NO `FOR UPDATE`; no pg_advisory lock anywhere in the body; the invoice `FOR UPDATE` at :164 comes after the INSERT and two payments to different invoices of one customer share no row lock. The per-customer lock existed historically (20260222200000_fix_allocate_payment_double_count.sql:59 and 20260223200000_payment_allocation.sql:59: `SELECT * INTO v_cust FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id FOR UPDATE;`) and was dropped when 20260316200001_fix_allocate_payment_missing_columns.sql introduced the MAX(version) scheme (MAX read at :64, no customers select at all); never restored. (4) UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version) at 20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:116 is still live — no migration drops it; 20260316200000 drops only allocation_sets_entity_type_check. 20260316200001's own header records this constraint firing in production before ("allocation_sets version always defaulted to 1, causing unique constraint violation on 2nd payment"). (5) trg_enforce_allocation_not_over_payment (20260616121105:34-37) is a value-only BEFORE-row check and takes no lock. (6) Not documented in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md, the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline, or any docs/audits/gauntlet/*.md (no hit for MAX(version)/next_version/allocate_payment race).

TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE FINDING. (a) The evidence claim "a raw Postgres error surfaced to the user" is factually wrong: both UI callers pass the error through sanitizeError (src/pages/PaymentAllocation.tsx:307, src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:1049) and src/lib/errorSanitizer.ts:8-9 maps /duplicate key value violates unique constraint "[^"]+"/i to 'A record with this information already exists'. This does not reduce severity — it supports MED: on a payment screen that wording reads as "this payment was already recorded", so an AR clerk may believe the payment posted and never re-enter it (fail-closed abort becomes a silently missing payment). Recovery is otherwise one click: resetKey() runs only on success, so a resubmit reuses the same idempotency key, and the winner's claim row rolled back with its transaction, so the retry re-claims and succeeds. (b) The race window is wider than the finding states: the winner holds its uncommitted UNIQUE index entry from the INSERT at :142 through the invoice loop, prepay insert, activity_feed and financial_audit_log writes until COMMIT, so any second payment for the same customer that merely STARTS during that whole body still reads MAX(version)=N (the uncommitted row is invisible), then blocks and raises 23505.

Severity MED confirmed: fails closed with no money corruption, but sits on the cash-application path with a misleading user-facing message.

### [LOW] create_direct_order performs no cent rounding anywhere — fractional-cent money stored in order_items/orders and commissions minted from the unrounded profit accumulator

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:118-131, 158-171, 190-193; src/pages/NewOrder.tsx:243-247, 507-514

**Detail.** The latest on-disk create_direct_order (the path NewOrder.tsx uses for priced orders) accumulates header totals and inserts order_items.total_price / profit / net_margin as raw numeric qty*price with no ROUND(...,2) at any point. A 2-decimal price times a fractional quantity (both ordinary UI inputs — NewOrder parses free-text floats) stores sub-cent dollar amounts in order_items. The header is later rewritten by trg_recalc_order_totals with ROUND(SUM(lines),2), so orders.total_price = round-of-sum while invoicing bills sum-of-round (extended_cents = round(line*100) per line, 20260620190000:104,111 and 20260721014858:488), letting order header vs sum-of-invoice-line-cents drift by cents on multi-line fractional orders. Commissions are minted at line 190-193 from the UNROUNDED v_total_profit accumulator, not the trigger-canonical rounded stored profit — the exact class the Section-4 gauntlet forced bulk_import_order to fix ('whole-cent per line', 'commissions from trigger-canonical stored profit', migrations 20260806004644/20260806012423); the canonical direct-order path itself was never given the same treatment. Quote-converted orders don't have this problem (save_quote rounds each line to 2dp server-side).

**Evidence.** 20260614142939:119-121: `v_total_price := v_total_price + COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0);` (no ROUND); :162: total_price inserted as `COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric, 0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric, 0)`; :190-193: `PERFORM _insert_commissions_for_order(v_order_id, p_customer_id, v_total_profit, v_customer.default_commission_split, p_order_date);` — v_total_profit is the unrounded accumulator. Contrast bulk-import hardening (docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04...md): 'derives one bigint-cent cost snapshot ... keeps every line profit whole-cent' and 'rereads trigger-canonical order totals'.

**Failure scenario.** Direct order: 3 lines of qty 3.33 at $10.10 → each line total_price stored 33.633 (sub-cent). orders.total_price = ROUND(100.899,2) = 100.90; invoice for full delivery bills 3 × round(3363.3) = 10090 — here equal, but with half-cent lines (e.g. qty 2.5 × $10.01 = 25.025 per line, 3 lines: header ROUND(75.075)=75.08 vs invoice 3×round(2502.5)=3×2503=7509 = 75.09) the order total and the invoiced total disagree by a cent, and commission basis differs sub-cent from stored order profit. Repeated across the season this creates unreconcilable penny drift between orders, invoices, and commissions.

**Verifier verdict.** CORE CLAIM SURVIVES, COMMISSION SUB-CLAIM REFUTED.

Verified accurate:
1. The cited body is the latest definition. `grep -rn "CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION.*create_direct_order" supabase/migrations/` returns 20260614142939 as newest; 417 later migration files exist and none redefines it (20260805211951_harden_bulk_order_import_lifecycle.sql only mentions it in a comment at :7, "downstream work as create_direct_order"). No staged/parked or applied-migration-artifact variant exists.
2. No rounding in it, confirmed by reading: `v_total_price := v_total_price + COALESCE((v_item->>'quantity')::numeric,0) * COALESCE((v_item->>'price_per_unit')::numeric,0);` and the order_items INSERT writes total_price / profit / net_margin as raw products with no ROUND.
3. The DB does NOT enforce cent scale (checked migrations, not merely "didn't find"): supabase/migrations/20260206172436_create_full_schema_v2.sql:191 `total_price numeric NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — unqualified numeric; no later ALTER TABLE retypes it to numeric(x,2). The only order_items triggers are `after_order_items_change` (trg_recalc_order_totals, AFTER, writes orders only) and `trg_snapshot_order_item_cost` (20260513050000:53, BEFORE INSERT, only fills cost_at_time_cents). Nothing normalizes line dollars to cents.
4. Round-of-sum vs sum-of-round is real. Header: 20260311200000_invoice_ar_single_source.sql:74-77 `total_price = ROUND(v_total_price, 2)` over `SUM(total_price)`. Invoicing: 20260620190000_invoice_creator_provenance_totals.sql:103 `round(v_item.total_price * 100)::bigint` per line and :111 `v_total_cents := v_total_cents + round(v_item.total_price * 100)::bigint`; delivery path 20260721014858:488 `SUM(ROUND(di.quantity_delivered * oi.price_per_unit * 100)::bigint)`.
5. Reachability confirmed: src/pages/NewOrder.tsx:830 `updateItem(item._key, 'quantity', parseFloat(e.target.value) || 0)` (free-text fractional) and :505-514 sends raw quantity/price_per_unit; the frontend's own `Math.round(totalPrice*100)/100` at :243-245 is display-only and never reaches the DB. Worked example checks out: 3 x (2.5 @ $10.01) stores 25.025/line, orders.total_price = ROUND(75.075,2) = 75.08, invoice = 3 x round(2502.5) = 7509 = $75.09.
6. Scoping holds and it is not a baseline duplicate: save_quote rounds each line server-side (20260302120000_save_quote_bidirectional_calc.sql:246-255 `ROUND(ppu * client_units, 2)`, `ROUND((ppu - cc) * ..., 2)`) and quick-delivery works in cents (20260622090000:106-108,190,228). KNOWN_ISSUES.md has zero hits for "create_direct_order" or "sub-cent"; the 2026-08-05 section-04 doc hardens only bulk_import_order and cites create_direct_order at :38 purely as the reference for effects.

REFUTED PART (drives severity down): the claim that commissions are minted from a basis that disagrees with trigger-canonical stored profit — "the exact class the Section-4 gauntlet forced bulk_import_order to fix" — does not hold. (a) The accumulator is mathematically IDENTICAL to the trigger's pre-round value: the trigger computes SUM(order_items.total_price) - SUM(cost_per_unit * total_units_needed), and each line was inserted as exactly qty*price / qty*cost, so orders.total_profit = ROUND(v_total_profit, 2) of that same number. In bulk import the accumulator was a stale per-line-ROUNDED value that genuinely disagreed; here it does not. (b) The latest `_insert_commissions_for_order` (20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:456-464) reconciles the final split as `GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_order_profit,0), 2), 0) - SUM(prior splits)`, and `compute_commission_amount` rounds each split to 2dp (20260526151856:169-178) — so total commissions = ROUND(exact profit,2) = orders.total_profit exactly. Residual is only a sub-cent value stored in `commissions.order_profit` and a possible 1c difference on a non-final split: cosmetic, not leakage.

SEVERITY MED -> LOW: what remains is sub-cent values in order_items plus a low-frequency penny disagreement between orders.total_price and invoice/AR totals (requires a line total landing on a half-cent). Reporting noise, not mis-billing (the invoice is internally consistent and is the sole AR source) and not commission drift. Worth a forward-only ROUND(...,2) for parity with the hardened import path, but not MED.

### [LOW] QuoteBuilder client totals use a different rounding formula than the server-canonical save_quote recompute — displayed money can differ by cents from what is stored

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:1116-1144, 1323-1341; supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:386-408

**Detail.** Rounding does happen server-side in exactly one place (save_quote's canonical recompute — good), but the client computes and displays a DIFFERENT formula before/while saving: client total_price = pricePerUnit × totalInventoryUnits with the UNROUNDED units, rounded only at the end (Math.round(x*100)/100); server total_price = ROUND(ppu × ROUND(units, 2), 2) — it multiplies by the units value already rounded to 2 decimals. When acres×rate/conversion yields units with >2 decimal places, the number the rep sees in the builder (and quotes to the grower verbally) differs from the stored/PDF/converted-to-order figure by up to ppu/2 × 0.01 dollars (e.g. units 10.005 at $100/unit: client shows $1,000.50, server stores $1,001.00). Same divergence for profit and price_per_acre. Client display also floats through IEEE doubles while the server uses exact numeric.

**Evidence.** QuoteBuilder.tsx:1123-1130: `const totalInventoryUnits = ... (acres * rateInOz) / inventoryUnitFactorOz ...; const totalPrice = pricePerUnit * totalInventoryUnits;` then :1140-1141 `total_units_needed: Math.round(totalInventoryUnits * 100) / 100, total_price: Math.round(totalPrice * 100) / 100`. Server 20260730235031:388: `WHEN inv_oz > 0 THEN ROUND((ac * ar * rate_oz) / inv_oz, 2) ... AS total_units` and :408: `ROUND(ppu * total_units, 2) AS total_price` — price computed from the rounded units.

**Failure scenario.** Rep builds a quote where a line computes 10.005 units at $100: builder shows line total $1,000.50 and quotes it to the customer; on save the server stores $1,001.00, which is what the PDF, the converted order, and the invoice all carry. The rep's spoken/screenshotted number is 50 cents off the contract of record.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED (with two sub-claims corrected). Core mechanism verified end-to-end.

1) Citations are accurate. `supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:388` = `WHEN inv_oz > 0 THEN ROUND((ac * ar * rate_oz) / inv_oz, 2) ... END AS total_units` and `:408` = `ROUND(ppu * total_units, 2) AS total_price` — the server multiplies price by the ALREADY-ROUNDED unit count. `src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:1123-1130` = `const totalInventoryUnits = ... (acres * rateInOz) / inventoryUnitFactorOz; ... const totalPrice = pricePerUnit * totalInventoryUnits;` with `:1140-1141` `total_units_needed: Math.round(totalInventoryUnits * 100) / 100, total_price: Math.round(totalPrice * 100) / 100` — the client multiplies by the UNROUNDED units and rounds once at the end. Two different formulas, confirmed.

2) No superseding migration. `20260730235031` is the LAST file defining `save_quote` (`:86 CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.save_quote(...)`); the only other late definition is `20260722202622_commission_split_lost_update_guard.sql:38`, which it supersedes. Every migration after it (`20260731001654` through `20260807220323`) touches AP period close, bulk order import, statements, and profile roles — none redefine `save_quote` or the `quote_items` recompute. KNOWN_ISSUES.md:214 confirms `20260730235031` is applied live.

3) Server wins, and the client never learns. `:467-502` recomputes the quote header from the recomputed item rows (`COALESCE(SUM(total_price), 0)`) and overwrites the client-supplied `total_price` sent at `QuoteBuilder.tsx:1429`. The RPC returns the stored totals (`:534-537 'total_price', (SELECT total_price FROM quotes ...)`), but the client discards them — the save handler at `QuoteBuilder.tsx:1500-1523` reads only `quote_id`, `commission_split`, `row_version` from `assertRpcResult<{ quote_id: string; commission_split?: ...; row_version?: unknown }>`, and there is NO post-save refetch (the only `fetchQuote(savedId)` at `:2648` is an error-recovery branch after a convert whose order could not be found). So the builder keeps displaying the client number after the save commits. Confirmed by reading the load path: `fetchQuote` at `:775-894` installs DB values verbatim into `setSections` with no client recalc, so the divergence only surfaces on a later reload.

4) The scenario is reachable with ordinary agronomy inputs, not an exotic edge case. Simulated 40.5 acres × 32 oz/ac ÷ 128 oz/gal = 10.125 units at $100/gal: client displays $1,012.50, server stores $1,013.00 — a $0.50 delta on one line. Any acres×rate/conversion producing >2 decimal units triggers it, which is routine.

5) Not previously documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` mentions `save_quote` only for row-version/commission-split lost-update work (`:214-220`, `:960`); grep for rounding/floating-point/client-vs-server divergence in quote totals returns nothing. `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md` is entirely about the Bulk Order Import lifecycle bypass; its rounding items (`:56, :59, :83-84`) concern `bulk_import_orders`, not `save_quote`.

CORRECTIONS to the finding:
- "Same divergence for profit and price_per_acre" is HALF WRONG. `profit` does diverge (client `:1131` `(pricePerUnit - cost) * totalInventoryUnits` unrounded vs server `:409` `ROUND((ppu - cc) * total_units, 2)` on rounded units). `price_per_acre` does NOT diverge: client `:1128-1129` `pricePerUnit * (rateInOz / inventoryUnitFactorOz)` and server `ROUND(ppu * (ar * rate_oz / inv_oz), 2)` both use the UNROUNDED rate ratio. `oz_per_acre` also agrees. Only `total_units_needed`-derived values (`total_price`, `profit`, and hence `net_margin` and the header totals) diverge.
- The stated example (units 10.005) is a float artifact: in JS `Math.round(10.005*100)/100` is 10.00, so the client would show 10.00 units next to a $1,000.50 total — internally inconsistent, but not the clean case. The mechanism holds regardless; use the 10.125 case above.
- "the PDF ... carry [the server figure]" is WRONG for the in-session PDF. `QuoteBuilder.tsx:1667-1672` and `:2052-2057` build the PDF from in-memory `i.total_price` / `totals.totalPrice`, i.e. the CLIENT number. So a PDF emailed in the same session and the stored quote of record disagree — arguably a slightly worse framing than the reviewer's, since the divergence escapes to the customer document rather than being corrected by it. The converted order and invoice do carry the server figure.

SEVERITY: LOW is correct and should stand. Bounded at ppu × 0.005 per line; the database remains internally canonical and self-consistent (items, header, converted order, and invoice all agree on the server number); nothing double-counts or leaks money; and any page reload shows the stored value. It is a display/document-vs-record inconsistency, not a money-integrity defect.

### [LOW] allocate_payment computes allocation_sets version with unlocked MAX(version)+1 — concurrent payments for the same customer collide on the UNIQUE constraint with a raw error

- **Finder:** `money-math`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260713060300_harden_allocate_payment_idempotency.sql:139-152; supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:107-117

**Detail.** allocate_payment derives the new allocation_sets.version as `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version), 0) + 1 ... WHERE entity_type = 'payment' AND entity_id = p_customer_id` with no lock serializing same-customer callers. allocation_sets carries UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version), so two office users entering different checks for the same customer simultaneously both read the same MAX, and the second INSERT fails with a raw 'duplicate key value violates unique constraint' AFTER the first caller commits — the loser's whole transaction (including its invoice updates) rolls back correctly, so no money is lost, but the user gets an opaque database error instead of a retry or a clean serialization, and with an idempotency key the failed attempt's claimed key row may force a fresh key for the retry.

**Evidence.** 20260713060300:139-141: `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version), 0) + 1 INTO v_next_version FROM allocation_sets WHERE entity_type = 'payment' AND entity_id = p_customer_id;` (no FOR UPDATE / advisory lock). 20260213100000 CREATE TABLE allocation_sets: `UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version)`.

**Failure scenario.** Two clerks post two different checks for the same farm within the same second. Both compute version N+1; the second INSERT hits allocation_sets_entity_type_entity_id_version_key, the RPC aborts with a duplicate-key error string surfaced in the toast, and the clerk must guess that simply retrying fixes it. Fail-safe (no double-move of money), but an un-handled race on a money entry path.

**Verifier verdict.** CORE MECHANISM CONFIRMED, TWO SUB-CLAIMS REFUTED. Verified facts: (1) `supabase/migrations/20260713060300_harden_allocate_payment_idempotency.sql:139-140` reads exactly `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(version), 0) + 1 INTO v_next_version FROM allocation_sets WHERE entity_type = 'payment' AND entity_id = p_customer_id;` followed at :142-152 by the `INSERT INTO allocation_sets (entity_type, entity_id, version, ...) VALUES ('payment', p_customer_id, v_next_version, ...)`. Citation and line numbers are accurate. (2) The only lock token in the entire 283-line file is a single `FOR UPDATE` at line 164 — on `invoices`, inside the allocation loop, i.e. AFTER the allocation_sets INSERT. `grep -n "advisory\|FOR UPDATE\|LOCK TABLE\|FOR NO KEY"` on that file returns only `164: FOR UPDATE;`. So there is genuinely no serialization before the version read — this is "the DB does not enforce it", not "I couldn't find where it's enforced". (3) The UNIQUE constraint is live: `supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:116` `UNIQUE (entity_type, entity_id, version)`, and grepping every allocation_sets DDL across all migrations shows no DROP CONSTRAINT touching it (only `allocation_sets_entity_type_check` was dropped/recreated at 20260316200000, and a `payment_method` check added at 20260702152000). (4) NOT SUPERSEDED: 20260713060300 is the last file defining allocate_payment (`grep -rn "FUNCTION.*allocate_payment"` over all migrations), and `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_migration_history.sql:783` confirms it applied live as ledger version 20260713001107 with nothing later touching the function. The later advisory-lock hardening migrations (20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql, 20260806004644) contain zero references to allocate_payment or allocation_sets. No BEFORE INSERT trigger assigns version — the only allocation_sets trigger in the tree is `trg_enforce_allocation_not_over_payment` (20260616121105), an over-payment guard. (5) allocate_payment is effectively the sole live writer of `entity_type='payment'` sets; the other historical INSERT sites are superseded prior versions of the same function plus the retired record_invoice_payment (retired by 20260718203206). (6) NOT DOCUMENTED: grepping docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md for allocate_payment/allocation_sets/MAX(version) returns only an unrelated vendor-bill period-lock row; the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet file mentions allocate_payment only as a call-site citation at line 26. Baseline does not cover this. REFUTED SUB-CLAIMS, which materially deflate the stated harm: (a) "a raw 'duplicate key value violates unique constraint' error string surfaced in the toast" is FALSE. Both call sites — `src/pages/PaymentAllocation.tsx:307-309` and `src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:1047-1049` — end with `toast('error', sanitizeError(err));`, and `src/lib/errorSanitizer.ts:8-9` maps `/duplicate key value violates unique constraint "[^"]+"/i` to the literal string 'A record with this information already exists'. The clerk sees a generic (confusing) message, not a leaked constraint name; there is no schema disclosure. (b) "the failed attempt's claimed key row may force a fresh key for the retry" is FALSE. The idempotency claim is an `INSERT INTO idempotency_keys ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` inside the same function invocation (20260713060300:70-79), so it rolls back with the aborted transaction; and the callers only call `resetKey()` on the success path (PaymentAllocation.tsx:298, InvoiceDetail.tsx:1036), so a retry reuses the same key against a table with no surviving claim row and succeeds normally. What actually remains: a genuinely unserialized MAX+1 version generator on a money-entry path. Two concurrent allocate_payment calls for the SAME customer both compute version N+1; the second INSERT blocks on the btree unique index and, once the winner commits, aborts with 23505. Fail-safe (the loser's invoice UPDATEs roll back atomically, no double-application of money, no torn allocation), but the operator gets a misleading 'A record with this information already exists' toast for what is really a retryable serialization conflict, with no automatic retry anywhere in the path. Reachability is narrow: it requires two authenticated admin/sales_rep sessions posting payments for the same customer within the same transaction window, or two offline-queue devices draining `offlineSync.ts:400-404` for the same customer simultaneously — single-client offline drain is sequential and cannot self-collide. Severity LOW is correct as stated; I would not raise it (no money or data-integrity consequence, error is fail-closed) and would not lower it to nothing (the race is real, undocumented, and the repo demonstrably knows this pattern — 30+ pg_advisory_xact_lock sites including 20260726190515 which serializes an entire PO subsystem on a single lock key — so allocate_payment is a real gap, not an accepted design). Historical corroboration that this constraint has bitten before: live ledger entry `20260302040409 fix_allocate_payment_version_conflict` (baseline line 213) and the comment at 20260316200001_fix_allocate_payment_missing_columns.sql:4, "allocation_sets version always defaulted to 1, causing unique constraint violation on 2nd payment."

---

### [LOW] Mint-time last-row penny reconciliation can compute a negative commission for tiny-profit multi-way splits, violating chk_commission_amount and aborting the whole order conversion / invoice save

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:452-461; supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:167-175, 1376-1381; supabase/migrations/20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:45

**Detail.** _insert_commissions_for_order, _insert_commissions_for_job, and save_invoice's U8 recompute all give the last split row `GREATEST(ROUND(profit,2),0) − SUM(previous compute_commission_amount rows)`. compute_commission_amount rounds each earlier row half-away-from-zero, so with a near-zero positive profit and 3+ recipients the earlier rows can over-round past the total: e.g. profit $0.02 with a valid 4×25% split gives three rows of ROUND(0.005)=0.01 (sum 0.03) and a last row of 0.02−0.03 = −$0.01. commissions carries `chk_commission_amount CHECK (commission_amount >= 0)` (20260209200000:45), so the INSERT/UPDATE fails and the entire convert_quote_to_order / create_direct_order / transfer_job_to_invoice / save_invoice transaction aborts with a raw check-constraint error — a fail-closed availability edge with a cryptic message, contradicting the helper's own stated invariant ('clamp so losing-trade orders produce $0 commission claims not negative payables'). The remainder-close recompute (20260721014858:1183-1215) is immune because it uses FLOOR-based largest-remainder distribution; the mint paths do not.

**Evidence.** 20260722174029:453-460: `WHEN sr.rn = sr.split_count THEN GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_order_profit, 0), 2), 0) - COALESCE(SUM(public.compute_commission_amount(p_order_profit, sr.percentage)) OVER (ORDER BY sr.rn ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND 1 PRECEDING), 0)` — compute_commission_amount (20260513020000:57): `SELECT GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_profit, 0) * COALESCE(p_percentage, 0) / 100, 2), 0);` — constraint (20260209200000:45): `ALTER TABLE commissions ADD CONSTRAINT chk_commission_amount CHECK (commission_amount >= 0);`

**Failure scenario.** A quote for a near-cost deal (total_profit $0.02) with a 4-way 25/25/25/25 split (valid: sums to 100) is accepted; convert_quote_to_order computes the fourth commission as −$0.01, the INSERT violates chk_commission_amount, and the whole conversion aborts with 'new row for relation "commissions" violates check constraint "chk_commission_amount"' — the office cannot convert the quote and gets no actionable message.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — I could not refute it; every element checked out against current source, the live catalog, and the baselines.

1) Current-source accuracy. supabase/migrations/20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql is the LATEST definition of BOTH mint helpers (grep over all 28 commission migrations: _insert_commissions_for_order last redefined at :404, _insert_commissions_for_job at :486; nothing later touches either). Both carry the unclamped last-row expression exactly as quoted — order path :452-461 `WHEN sr.rn = sr.split_count THEN GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_order_profit, 0), 2), 0) - COALESCE(SUM(public.compute_commission_amount(p_order_profit, sr.percentage)) OVER (ORDER BY sr.rn ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND 1 PRECEDING), 0)`, job path :532-535 identical with p_profit. The GREATEST clamps the TOTAL, not the difference — no outer clamp exists.

2) No missed enforcement. compute_commission_amount's live definition is the later 20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql:169-178 (`SELECT GREATEST(ROUND(COALESCE(p_profit,0)*COALESCE(p_percentage,0)/100, 2), 0)`) — same rounding as cited, so that supersession does not help. chk_commission_amount (20260209200000:45) is never dropped by any later migration (only commissions_status_check and chk_commission_source are dropped) and is confirmed LIVE in .claude/schema-registry.json:2735-2736 and by catalog read: `CHECK ((commission_amount >= (0)::numeric))`. The only non-internal trigger on public.commissions live is trg_commissions_recipient_resolved (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF recipient_user_id, WHEN new.recipient_user_id IS NULL) — it rejects unresolved recipients, it does not clamp amounts. validate_commission_split_json (20260526151856:130-163) only checks recipient presence, uniqueness, 0 < pct <= 100, and abs(total-100) <= 0.01 — it does not bound recipient count or profit, so a 4x25% split is fully valid input.

3) Live drift check. pg_get_functiondef for both live helpers returns the unclamped expression verbatim (read-only catalog query), so the deployed database matches the migration file — not a disk-only artifact.

4) Failure scenario is real, verified arithmetically against the live database (read-only SELECT, no mutation): compute_commission_amount(0.02,25) = 0.01, three prior rows = 0.03, GREATEST(ROUND(0.02,2),0) - 0.03 = -0.01, against a live CHECK requiring >= 0. Callers pass profit in DOLLARS, not cents, which is what makes sub-cent shares possible: convert_quote_to_order passes `v_quote.total_profit` (20260702172500_layer2_convert_quote_to_order_job_aware.sql:207-210) and create_quick_delivery passes `v_order_profit := (v_total_cents - v_total_cost_cents) / 100.0` (20260706130000_stock_policy_warn_not_block.sql:248-252). Neither gates on profit magnitude, and the exception is not handled by the idempotency wrapper, so the whole conversion transaction aborts.

5) Not already known. KNOWN_ISSUES.md and docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-*.md contain no entry for negative mint-time commission amounts or chk_commission_amount violations (greps on chk_commission_amount / "negative commission" / penny / reconcil / rounding return only unrelated bulk-import rounding items and a CHANGELOG note describing the intended >= 0 clamp). The nearest prior art is the save_invoice comment at 20260707060000:1367-1370, which anticipated only the 33.33/33.33/33.34 x $0.02 case — that case does NOT go negative (0.01+0.01 = 0.02, last row 0.00); the equal 4-way case the reviewer found does.

One citation correction that does not affect the claim: the save_invoice instance is still live but reached through a renamed impl — 20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql:25-26 renames save_invoice to _save_invoice_intent_impl_20260802 and wraps it, so the body carrying the reconciliation descends from 20260721223817_save_invoice_payment_terms.sql (identical expression), not literally 20260707060000:1376-1381. The defect survives that supersession.

Severity: LOW is correct and should not be raised. The window is narrow — a negative last row requires the final recipient's exact share to be smaller than the accumulated over-rounding of the earlier rows, i.e. roughly total profit < $0.005 x (n-1) for equal splits, so ~2-4 cents of profit with 3+ recipients. Zero and negative profit are safe (all rows clamp to 0, last row = 0). It is fail-closed — no incorrect money is written; the transaction aborts — so the impact is an availability/UX edge with a raw Postgres check-constraint message, not money corruption.

### [LOW] customers.default_commission_split (internal pay attribution) is readable by drivers, applicators, and location-dispatchees through the row-level customers_select policies

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260510070000_tighten_customer_profile_rls.sql:52-79; supabase/migrations/20260626120000_job_location_dispatches.sql:188-196; src/types/supabase.ts:2082

**Detail.** customers_select grants drivers full customer rows for customers tied to their recent deliveries, applicators for customers on their assigned jobs (7-day window), and any per-location dispatchee via customers_select_location_dispatchee. RLS is row-scoped, so the entire row is exposed — including default_commission_split, which names internal recipients and their percentage shares. A field employee's own JWT against PostgREST (`GET /rest/v1/customers?select=farm_name,default_commission_split`) returns who earns commission on that account and at what split. This is the same disclosure class as the known D3 item (jobs.commission_split visible to assigned applicators, pending Mason's owner decision) but on a different table and roles, and it is not covered by that entry; the 2026-07-27/28 office-only pricing lockdowns addressed quote_items/rates/SECDEF readers, not the customers row. Nothing in the CRX UI shows this to field roles — it is API-reachable only. Fix direction would mirror D3's options: a column-restricted view for field roles or moving the split to an office-only side table.

**Evidence.** 20260510070000:52-79: `CREATE POLICY customers_select ON customers FOR SELECT USING (is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND assigned_sales_rep = (SELECT auth.uid())) OR (is_driver() AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM deliveries d WHERE d.customer_id = customers.id AND d.assigned_driver = (SELECT auth.uid()) ...)) OR (is_applicator() AND EXISTS (...)))` — no column restriction; customers row includes `default_commission_split: Json | null` (supabase.ts:2082).

**Failure scenario.** A driver assigned today's delivery for Smith Farms queries /rest/v1/customers with their own token and reads {splits:[{recipient:'Clayton Wells',percentage:60},{recipient:'Mason Wells',percentage:40}]} — internal compensation-structure data for the account, plus credit_limit_cents and prepay_balance_cents from the same row.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — the citations check out verbatim and no enforcement mechanism exists elsewhere.

1. Policy text is exact. `supabase/migrations/20260510070000_tighten_customer_profile_rls.sql:52-77` is `CREATE POLICY customers_select ON customers FOR SELECT USING (is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND assigned_sales_rep = (SELECT auth.uid())) OR (is_driver() AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM deliveries d WHERE d.customer_id = customers.id AND d.assigned_driver = (SELECT auth.uid()) AND d.scheduled_date >= CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '1 day')) OR (is_applicator() AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM jobs j WHERE j.customer_id = customers.id AND j.applicator_id = (SELECT auth.uid()) AND j.job_date >= CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '7 days')))`. Row-scoped, no column restriction. The reviewer's line range (52-79) is off by two lines only because 78-79 are blank/comment; 52-77 is the policy.

2. `supabase/migrations/20260626120000_job_location_dispatches.sql:187-198` adds a second, additive whole-row policy: `CREATE POLICY "customers_select_location_dispatchee" ON public.customers FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.jobs j WHERE j.customer_id = customers.id AND public._is_dispatched_to_me(j.id)))` — confirmed, and 20260709210000 explicitly widens the same helper ("customers_select_location_dispatchee -- inherit the same widening").

3. No superseding migration. I grepped every migration for `customers_select` / `ON customers` / `ON public.customers`: the only later files touching `public.customers` are triggers (20260716124223 contact sync, 20260722174029 recipient-id stamp, 20260730235031 row_version bump) — none drops or replaces either SELECT policy. So this is "the DB does not enforce column scoping," checked in the migrations, not "I couldn't find it."

4. No column-level grant exists as a fallback. The only `GRANT SELECT (` column-grant in the whole migration tree is `supabase/migrations/20260729015706_application_service_cost_admin_only.sql:111` (application_services, not customers). The 2026-07-27/28 pricing lockdowns therefore do not cover the customers row, as the reviewer states.

5. The exposed column is real and sensitive: `src/types/supabase.ts:2082` `default_commission_split: Json | null` on the customers Row, alongside `credit_limit_cents` and `prepay_balance_cents` in the same row block (2069, 2094). Contents are recipient-name + percentage objects (`supabase/migrations/20260722202622_commission_split_lost_update_guard.sql:509` validates `{splits:[{recipient, percentage}]}`).

6. Not already documented. KNOWN_ISSUES.md's Sprint-D D3(b) is scoped to `jobs.commission_split` via `jobs_select` ("The jobs_select policy already exposes the split JSONB (names+percentages) to assigned applicators", docs/loops/workflow-waves-ledger.md:105) — a different table and a different policy. The 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet refresh contains no `commission_split` reference at all (grep: zero hits). Nothing in docs/audits/gauntlet/ or KNOWN_ISSUES.md mentions `customers_select` column breadth.

Severity stays LOW rather than rising: for applicators and location-dispatchees the same split values are already reachable through `jobs.commission_split` (trg_jobs_snapshot_commission_split copies the customer default onto direct jobs — docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:372), which is exactly the documented D3 exposure. The genuinely new delta is the driver branch, where drivers have no jobs_select path — a real but narrow, employee-only, API-only disclosure with no UI surface.
