### [LOW] CommissionPayments payout picker and payment list are hard-capped (500/200 rows) with no pagination or truncation warning

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** src/pages/CommissionPayments.tsx:91, 164-165

**Detail.** fetchUnpaid loads pending commissions ordered by order_date desc with `.limit(500)`, and fetchPayments loads `.limit(200)` payments, with no count check, pagination, or truncation indicator. Once more than 500 pending commissions exist, the OLDEST pending commissions (beyond the newest 500 by order_date) silently disappear from the payout picker — precisely the rows most at risk of being forgotten — and the per-recipient '(N unpaid)' counts and selected totals understate. Same for payments beyond 200: the Unposted/Posted summary card totals (computed client-side from the fetched page, lines 425-430) silently under-report.

**Evidence.** CommissionPayments.tsx:163-165: `.eq('status', 'pending').order('order_date', { ascending: false }).limit(500);` and :90-91: `.order('created_at', { ascending: false }).limit(200);` — no `count:'exact'` or overflow handling on either.

**Failure scenario.** After two busy seasons, 620 commissions are pending. The 120 oldest (last year's) never appear in the New Payment modal for any recipient; nobody is told they were truncated; those reps' old commissions sit unpaid indefinitely while the screen looks complete.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every element of the claim checks out against current source, and I could not find any mechanism elsewhere that mitigates it.

Code verification (src/pages/CommissionPayments.tsx, 839 lines total):
- :83-91 `fetchPayments`: `supabase.from('commission_payments').select('*').order('created_at', { ascending: false }).limit(200);` — no `count:'exact'`, no range/pagination, no overflow branch. Quoted exactly as the reviewer states.
- :154-165 `fetchUnpaid`: `.from('commissions').select(...).eq('status','pending').order('order_date', { ascending: false }).limit(500);` — same, no count or overflow handling. Sort is DESC on `order_date`, so the rows dropped are indeed the OLDEST pending commissions, exactly as claimed.
- Downstream dependence on the truncated arrays is real and client-side only: :249-252 `recipients` is built from `unpaidCommissions`; :254-256 `recipientCommissions = unpaidCommissions.filter(c => c.recipient_user_id === selectedRecipient)` (client-side filter over the global top-500 — there is no per-recipient server query, so one recipient's list is capped by other recipients' newer rows); :258-260 `selectedTotal` reduces over that filtered set; :726-728 renders `{r.name} ({unpaidCommissions.filter(...).length} unpaid)`; :739 `Unpaid Commissions ({recipientCommissions.length})`; :774 "No unpaid commissions for this recipient" is shown whenever the recipient has zero rows in the fetched page, which is indistinguishable from truncation.
- :424-430 confirms the payments-side claim: `const unpostedTotal = payments.filter(p => p.status === 'unposted').reduce(...)` and the same for `postedTotal` — both computed over the 200-row page, so the summary cards under-report once payments exceed 200. Note the tab filter at :423 (`payments.filter(p => p.status === tab)`) means the Voided tab is also fed from the same shared 200-row page ordered by `created_at`, so a heavily-voided history can starve the Unposted tab of rows — a slight aggravation the reviewer did not mention.

No missed enforcement mechanism:
- No other read path substitutes. `grep "from('commissions')"` across src returns only four callers: CommissionPayments.tsx:155, Reports.tsx:214, notificationTriggers.ts:576, reconciliation.ts:780. Reports.tsx:213-218 is a date-ranged report view (`select('*').order('order_date',...)`, no explicit limit) and does not create payouts. Payment creation is only reachable from the modal's `selectedCommissions` set, which is sourced entirely from the capped `fetchUnpaid` result — so a truncated commission is genuinely unpayable through the UI until the newer rows ahead of it are paid off.
- No server-side guard exists or could exist: this is a pure client query shape, so no migration, trigger, or RPC can compensate. I checked the commission-creation path (`_insert_commissions_for_order`, supabase/migrations/20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids.sql:404-506) only to confirm `order_date` is always populated on both the order and job channels (`p_order_date date DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE`, written at :475 and :504) — so the DESC sort is well-defined and NULL-ordering does not accidentally rescue old rows.

Not already documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` contains no entry about UI row caps, `.limit()`, pagination, or truncation on any list screen (the only "cap" entry is the unrelated Phase 3C git-ancestry `MAX_HISTORY_COMMITS` item at :1029). The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline contains no `limit`/pagination/truncation material at all. Every prior CommissionPayments finding across docs/ is a different defect: the cancelled-commission leak (LEDGER.json:347 `lifecycle:CommissionPayments.fetchUnpaid:cancelled-leak`, the `.eq('status','pending')` fix that is now the line immediately above the cap), the blank order#/customer display fix (LEDGER.json:842, "RESOLVED" in the 2026-08-05 §4 doc line 934), the NULL-recipient "Other" HIGH (gauntlet 2026-07-22 sections-05-08:45), and the void dead-order count (codex-driven LEDGER.json:89). None touches row caps.

Severity: LOW is correct and should not be raised. The failure requires >500 simultaneously-pending commissions; the overnight-bug-hunt live evidence in docs/audits/overnight-bug-hunt/LEDGER.json:639 records "32 pending, 0 paid" on live, so this is latent and volume-gated, not currently firing. It is nonetheless a real silent-money-loss shape rather than a cosmetic nit — the rows that vanish are the oldest unpaid liabilities, the screen gives no indication of truncation, and the fix is small (`count:'exact'` plus a banner or range pagination, and a per-recipient server-side filter for the picker).

### [LOW] Void-payment modal copy omits the job-channel close-out rule — admin is told dead-ORDER rows close out, not dead-minting-INVOICE rows

- **Finder:** `commissions`
- **Location:** src/pages/CommissionPayments.tsx:666-670; supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:1656-1681

**Detail.** The void confirmation modal says linked commissions 'will be reset to pending ... except any whose order was since cancelled or voided, which will be closed out'. Since U8, void_commission_payment ALSO permanently closes out (cancelled, amount 0) any job-sourced commission whose minting invoice is no longer live — a condition the modal never mentions. An admin voiding a batch containing job commissions whose invoice was cancelled expects them back as pending per the modal, but they are closed out. The post-void toast does report the aggregate counts, but attributes them only to 'order cancelled/voided' (line 401), which is wrong for job rows.

**Evidence.** CommissionPayments.tsx:668-669: 'except any whose order was since cancelled or voided, which will be closed out (not re-payable)' — DB (20260707060000:1670-1680): cancel branch also fires on `c.job_id IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM invoices i WHERE i.id = c.invoice_id AND i.status NOT IN ('voided','cancelled') AND i.deleted_at IS NULL)`.

**Failure scenario.** A batch holds job J-88's pending commissions; J-88's invoice was cancelled (rows already cancelled) or the batch's job rows reference a since-voided invoice. Admin reads the modal, expects reset-to-pending, voids, and gets 'closed out (order cancelled/voided)' messaging for rows whose ORDER concept doesn't even apply — misdiagnosing what happened and possibly re-invoicing the job expecting old rows to revive.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — the claim survives every refutation angle I tried.\n\n1) Source text is quoted accurately. src/pages/CommissionPayments.tsx:668-669: \"Its {voidTarget?.item_count ?? 'unverified number of'} linked commission(s) will be reset to <strong>pending</strong> and can be re-paid later &mdash; / except any whose order was since cancelled or voided, which will be <strong>closed out</strong> (not re-payable).\" The toast at :401 is likewise `${resetCount} commission(s) reset to pending, ${cancelledCount} closed out (order cancelled/voided)` — the ONLY reason surfaced is order deadness.\n\n2) DB behavior is as claimed, and I verified it in the CURRENT definition, not a superseded one. supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:1655-1681 — the cancel branch fires on `EXISTS (orders o ... cancelled/voided/deleted_at)` OR `(c.job_id IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM invoices i WHERE i.id = c.invoice_id AND i.status NOT IN ('voided','cancelled') AND i.deleted_at IS NULL))`, and the reset branch excludes the same job-lineage condition plus `AND c.status <> 'cancelled'`. So a job row is closed out (status 'cancelled', commission_amount 0) for a reason the modal never names.\n\n3) Supersession check (migrations apply in timestamp order): `grep -rln void_commission_payment supabase/migrations/` returns 14 files; the only two later than 20260707060000 (20260720233000_per_line_split_billing_save_rpc.sql:1556 and 20260721180000_fix_split_impl_unassigned_app_service_record.sql:1239) mention it only inside a comment about `commission_payment_items.commission_id` FK behavior — neither redefines the function. The earlier 20260622070000_void_commission_payment_dead_order_guard.sql is the order-only ancestor that the U8 file replaced. 20260707060000 is the live body.\n\n4) The scenario can occur. Job-sourced commissions really do enter these batches: fetchUnpaid selects `job_id` (CommissionPayments.tsx:157) and labels rows `Job ${...}` (:227-228), so a batch can contain rows with NO order at all — for which the modal's \"whose order was since cancelled or voided\" is not merely incomplete but categorically inapplicable. The dead-invoice condition is reachable post-batch (invoice voided/cancelled after the batch was created or posted); a row already flipped to 'cancelled' while sitting in the batch is likewise excluded from reset and counted in v_cancelled_count.\n\n5) Not already documented. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md mentions void_commission_payment only at line 937 (the 2026-06-22 order-deadness guard, marked fixed) — nothing about the U8 job/invoice branch or the modal copy. docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md (101 lines) is entirely about bulk_import_order and never touches commission-payment voiding.\n\nSeverity: LOW is correct and I would not raise it. The DB does the right thing deliberately (the Codex R1 P1 comment at :1644-1647 shows generation-precise `c.invoice_id` testing is intentional — re-invoicing mints a NEW commission generation), so there is no money or data error. The harm is confined to operator misdiagnosis, and the finding's tail speculation (\"possibly re-invoicing the job expecting old rows to revive\") overstates it, since re-invoicing produces correct fresh commissions regardless. Real, but purely a user-facing copy/messaging accuracy defect in both the modal (:668-669) and the toast reason string (:401).

### [LOW] tests/e2e/concurrent-operations.spec.ts contains no concurrent execution and none of its claimed idempotency/race coverage; test 1's premise contradicts create_direct_order's warnings-only design

- **Finder:** `idempotency-concurrency`
- **Location:** tests/e2e/concurrent-operations.spec.ts:7-14,92-146,232,263-352; supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql (warnings-only inventory)

**Detail.** The file header claims coverage of 'Concurrent payment recording on same invoice', 'Order cancellation during delivery creation', and 'Duplicate idempotency key handling' (lines 8-13). Verified against the file body: every test issues strictly sequential awaited calls (no Promise.all of competing mutations anywhere); no test reuses an idempotency key, replays a receipt, or tests key-argument mismatch; there is no cancellation-during-delivery test at all; nothing exercises convert double-click, QUOTE_STALE_WRITE/row_version, hold-release vs conversion, or two-editor conflicts — i.e., none of this dimension's hunt list is covered. Several create_direct_order calls omit p_idempotency_key entirely (e.g., lines 97-102). Additionally test 1 ('second should fail if inventory exhausted', line 92) asserts an 'insufficient' rejection, but create_direct_order never raises on shortfall — it returns warnings (verified in 20260614142939: only v_warnings appends, no inventory RAISE) — so the second 5-unit order succeeds against 2 free units and the fallback assertion `expect(avail - prebooked).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0)` (line 143) evaluates 10−13=−3 and fails: the test is either red or never run, and either way documents behavior the RPC does not have. (Context: KNOWN_ISSUES line 1041 already records that E2E is blocked from CI for credential reasons; the content-vs-claim mismatch itself is not recorded anywhere.)

**Evidence.** Lines 8-13: `* - Concurrent payment recording on same invoice\n * - Order cancellation during delivery creation\n * … - Duplicate idempotency key handling` — vs. body: 10 tests, all sequential; the only key usage is unique fresh keys (e.g. line 320 `p_idempotency_key: 'PAY1-${RUN}'`), never a reuse. Lines 118-131: second order expected to 'fail — only 2 free' though the RPC design is warn-only.

**Failure scenario.** The team treats the file name/header as evidence that double-submit and race classes are regression-tested; a future refactor that breaks same-key replay or double-click convert ships with green tests because no test ever exercised those paths. If the suite is ever wired into CI as-is, test 1 fails for design reasons unrelated to any regression.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on the facts, but severity should drop MED -> LOW because the stated failure scenario ("ships with green tests") cannot occur: the entire Playwright suite is currently unloadable, so nothing in this file ever reports green.

WHAT I VERIFIED (independently, not taking the reviewer's word):

1. No concurrency in the file. `grep -n "Promise.all\|Promise.allSettled\|Promise.race" tests/e2e/concurrent-operations.spec.ts` returns ZERO matches across all 699 lines. Every one of the 10 tests is strictly sequential awaited calls. The header at tests/e2e/concurrent-operations.spec.ts:7-13 claims "Two simultaneous orders consuming the same inventory / Concurrent payment recording on same invoice / Order cancellation during delivery creation / Duplicate idempotency key handling". Reading all 10 tests: (1) two sequential orders, (2) exact-inventory order, (3) over-delivery, (4) two sequential payments, (5) draft-invoice payment, (6) zero qty, (7) negative payment, (8) inventory non-negative, (9) double complete_delivery, (10) PO receive ledger. There is NO cancellation-during-delivery test at all, and no test simulates simultaneity.

2. Test 1's premise is factually wrong against the DB. I read the CURRENT definition, not an older one. Migration order check: `grep -rn "FUNCTION public.create_direct_order\|FUNCTION create_direct_order" supabase/migrations/` shows the LAST definition is supabase/migrations/20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:49 (which DROPs the 7-arg overload at :47 and asserts exactly-one-overload at :238-242). No later migration redefines it. Its inventory block, lines 99-116, contains only `v_warnings := array_append(...)` in both branches — there is NO `RAISE` anywhere on shortfall. The design is deliberate: supabase/migrations/20260322100000_inventory_warn_not_block_net_position.sql is literally named "warn not block". So the assertion at :130 `expect(String(order2.error).toLowerCase()).toContain('insufficient')` tests behavior the RPC does not have and never had since 2026-03-22. This is a "the DB does not enforce X, checked the migrations" claim, not a "couldn't find it" claim.

3. The fallback assertion is arithmetically red. create_direct_order unconditionally prebooks at 20260614142939:172-175 (`quantity_prebooked = quantity_prebooked + qty`). Seed is quantity_available:10, quantity_prebooked:0 (spec :57-62). After order1 (8) and order2 (5), prebooked=13, available=10, so line 143 `expect(avail - prebooked).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0)` evaluates expect(-3) and fails. The only CHECK constraint on the column is `chk_inventory_qty_prebooked CHECK (quantity_prebooked >= 0)` (20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:20) — nothing caps prebooked at available, so order2 commits and the negative math is reached. Test 1 is red on both branches.

4. Idempotency keys: confirmed every `create_direct_order` call in the file omits `p_idempotency_key` (:97-102, :155-159, :190-194, :269-273, :361-365, :409-413, :437-441, :488-492), and the only keys used are fresh per-run values (`PAY1-${RUN}` :320, `PAY2-${RUN}` :331, `DRAFT-PAY-${RUN}` :394, `NEG-PAY-${RUN}` :469) — never a reuse or a mismatch replay.

5. Not in the baselines. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md records only that E2E is blocked (`E2E stays if: false`, "five E2E files still contain direct production endpoint literals"); it does not record the header-vs-body coverage mismatch or the wrong test-1 premise. docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-... is about bulk order import lifecycle hardening and does not touch this file. So it is genuinely new.

WHY I DOWNGRADE TO LOW (the part the reviewer got wrong):
The stated failure scenario — "a future refactor ships with green tests" — is impossible today. tests/e2e/golive/utils/supabase-helpers.ts:12 hardcodes `https://rhyzpcqhnizqbxphqdkr.supabase.co`, and playwright.config.ts:44 calls `assertNoProductionEndpointReferences(e2eSources)` at CONFIG LOAD time, which throws on that literal (tests/e2e/utils/safety-guards.ts:58-73). playwright.config.ts:31 also calls `resolveSafeE2EConfig()` which throws unless `E2E_TARGET_ENV=staging` with a non-prod URL, and no staging project exists. .github/workflows/ci.yml:227 is `if: false`. So the whole suite cannot load, let alone go green — there is no false-green signal, and the red test-1 costs nothing because it is never executed. The residual harm is purely inventory/documentation drift: the filename and header overstate what would be covered if the suite were ever re-enabled.

Two minor overstatements in the finding worth noting: test 9 (:609-626) DOES replay `complete_delivery` and asserts `already_completed`, which is duplicate-submission coverage (keyless, but not nothing), and tests 2/8 do exercise prebook math sequentially — so "none of this dimension's hunt list is covered" is too strong. The uncovered header bullets are specifically "Concurrent payment recording on same invoice" and "Order cancellation during delivery creation" (zero coverage), plus true idempotency-key reuse.

### [LOW] QuoteBuilder failed-convert recovery writes quotes.status directly; the DB transition enforcer allows accepted→sent unconditionally, so a convert race can flip a converted quote back to 'sent'

- **Finder:** `idempotency-concurrency`
- **Location:** src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:2625-2701 (write at 2688); supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:103

**Detail.** After a failed convert RPC, the error path re-reads status and — when it is not 'accepted' — writes `supabase.from('quotes').update({ status: revertTo })` directly (bypassing revert_quote_status, which is the RPC that refuses reverting an accepted quote that has an order). Checked the migrations: the trigger _enforce_quote_status_transition permits `accepted → sent` with no orders-exist check (the orders-exist refusal exists only inside revert_quote_status), so the DB does not enforce the converted-quote invariant for direct writes. The window is the gap between the recovery read (~line 2625) and the update (2688): a convert committed from another tab in that gap makes this tab overwrite 'accepted' with 'sent' on a quote that now has an order and a fully-drawn ledger. Downstream damage is bounded: re-convert returns already_converted (draws guard), and the row_version bump invalidates other editors — but the quote shows 'sent' while its order exists, which QuoteBuilder's own comment at 2696-2700 treats as a manual-fix state.

**Evidence.** QuoteBuilder.tsx:2688: `const revertResult = await supabase.from('quotes').update({ status: revertTo }).eq('id', savedId).select('*');` — 20260706030000:103 in _enforce_quote_status_transition: `OR (OLD.status = 'accepted' AND NEW.status = 'sent')` with no accompanying EXISTS(orders) condition (the enforcer body contains no reference to the orders table).

**Failure scenario.** User A's convert fails on a transient error; simultaneously user B (or A's second tab) converts the same quote successfully. A's recovery read raced ahead of B's commit (returned 'sent'), then A's direct update lands after B's commit, flipping the converted quote from accepted back to sent. Staff now see an open 'sent' booking that is actually fully drawn with a live order; conversion attempts return 'already_converted' info toasts, and the status must be fixed by hand.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim verified against current source and the full migration chain; no superseding enforcement found.

1) The direct write exists as quoted. src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:2688 — `const revertResult = await supabase.from('quotes').update({ status: revertTo }).eq('id', savedId).select('*');`. It is preceded by the recovery read at :2613-2619 (`.from('quotes').select('status').eq('id', savedId).maybeSingle()`), the accepted-branch early return at :2625-2655, and the null-status early return at :2657-2664. `revertTo` is computed at :2685 from React state, not from `liveQuoteStatus`. The update carries NO `row_version`/status predicate — only `.eq('id', savedId)` — so it is a blind last-writer-wins write. The read→write window is real and unguarded.

2) The DB does NOT enforce the invariant (checked the migrations, not merely "couldn't find it"). `_enforce_quote_status_transition` is last redefined in supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:74-121 — I confirmed by grep that this is the newest of the 11 migrations touching that function (20260228300000, 20260311200000, 20260319000000, 20260416100000, 20260511120000, 20260608154245, 20260610185806, 20260616204400, 20260702131000, 20260703200000, 20260706030000), and no later migration drops or replaces trigger `enforce_quote_status_transition` on quotes. Its body contains `OR (OLD.status = 'accepted' AND NEW.status = 'sent')` with no accompanying condition; the only `EXISTS` in the function targets `jobs` and is gated on `NEW.status = 'closed_short'`. There is no reference to `orders` anywhere in the function.

3) No other trigger closes the gap. The only other status trigger on quotes is `trg_enforce_quote_terminal_not_drawn` (20260614151956:50, body updated 20260616115308), which fires only when `OLD.status IN ('sent','revised') AND NEW.status IN ('declined','cancelled','expired')` — OLD='accepted' misses it entirely. `enforce_quote_accepted_fully_drawn` was DROPPED (20260610181612:292). `release_holds_on_quote_status_change` only acts when NEW is terminal, so it no-ops on accepted→sent.

4) The RPC the write bypasses does enforce it. `revert_quote_status` (20260718232157_harden_quote_reopen_history_guards.sql) refuses with `'Cannot revert accepted quote % — an active (non-cancelled) order exists from it'` and `QUOTE_REOPEN_HAS_ORDER_HISTORY`, and additionally zeroes `quote_product_draws` and rebuilds planned holds via `create_planned_holds` — none of which the direct write does.

5) The write is permitted at the RLS layer: `CREATE POLICY "quotes_update" ON quotes FOR UPDATE TO authenticated USING (is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND created_by = (select auth.uid())))` (20260206174345_fix_security_and_performance_issues.sql:369-377); quotes was NOT included in the direct-DML revoke of 20260730235031 (which revokes only on quote_sections/quote_items/customer_addresses).

6) Not in the baseline. docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04… contains one finding (HIGH — Bulk Order Import bypassing the order lifecycle); it never mentions revert/accepted→sent or this recovery path. KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no entry for it — its nearest text (line 216) concerns client-side row-version token *adoption* on direct lifecycle writes, not a missing predicate on this write nor the DB's permissive accepted→sent edge.

Severity stays LOW, and two facts argue against raising it: (a) reachability is genuinely narrow — it needs A's convert to fail transiently (a lost-response-after-commit lands in the accepted branch at :2625 instead) AND B's convert to commit inside a single round trip between :2619 and :2688; (b) there is a post-hoc detector the reviewer did not credit — `applyDirectQuoteMutationRowVersion` (QuoteBuilder.tsx:697-718) sees the jumped token and raises "another edit may have completed at the same time… reload before saving, revising, or converting it", so the operator is warned even though the bad row is already committed. Two corrections/additions to the writeup: the read is at :2613-2619 (":2625" is the accepted-status check, not the read), and the unmentioned extra damage is that a planned booking flipped back to 'sent' this way keeps the holds released at accept time with no `create_planned_holds` rebuild — the direct write skips that half of `revert_quote_status` too.

---

## Phase 3: Security & Frontend (29)

### [HIGH] Assigned driver can complete a delivery by direct table UPDATE, bypassing complete_delivery's inventory, order-fulfillment, and invoice effects

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:83-93 (del_update policy); supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:174 (authenticated UPDATE grant on deliveries); baseline public_schema.sql line 3871 (_enforce_delivery_status_transition); baseline public_schema.sql line 2081 (_complete_delivery_authorized_impl)

**Detail.** The `del_update` RLS policy lets the assigned driver UPDATE a delivery whose OLD status is 'in_progress' or 'completed', and its WITH CHECK permits a NEW status in the same set. `authenticated` holds a table-level UPDATE grant on `public.deliveries`. The only BEFORE UPDATE OF status trigger, `_enforce_delivery_status_transition`, explicitly allows `in_progress -> completed`. `guard_completed_delivery_signature` only requires a non-blank `signed_by`, which the caller supplies. I checked every trigger on `public.deliveries` in the DR baseline (enforce_delivery_status_transition, trg_enforce_delivery_accounting_period, trg_guard_completed_delivery_signature, trg_delivery_status_change, guard_delivery_delete) — none performs the work the RPC does. Therefore: THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE that a delivery reaches 'completed' only through `complete_delivery()`. All of the completion side-effects live inside `_complete_delivery_authorized_impl`: `UPDATE delivery_items SET quantity_delivered`, `UPDATE order_items SET ...`, `INSERT INTO inventory_transactions`, `UPDATE inventory SET`, `UPDATE orders SET` (fulfilment status), `INSERT INTO invoices` + `invoice_items`, and `INSERT INTO financial_audit_log`. A direct PostgREST `PATCH /rest/v1/deliveries?id=eq.<id>` with `{"status":"completed","signed_by":"x"}` from the assigned driver's own JWT produces a completed delivery with no inventory decrement, no order progression, no invoice, and no financial audit row. The driver write path to this table is live and used today (src/pages/FieldStop.tsx:351 updates `deliveries.signature_url` directly), so nothing about the driver holding UPDATE here is theoretical.

**Evidence.** 20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:83-93 —
CREATE POLICY "del_update" ON public.deliveries
  AS PERMISSIVE FOR UPDATE TO authenticated
  USING ((
    is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()
    OR ((assigned_driver = (SELECT auth.uid())) AND (status = ANY (ARRAY['in_progress'::text, 'completed'::text])))
  ))
  WITH CHECK (( ... same predicate ... ));

acl_lockdown.sql:174 — GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.deliveries TO authenticated;

_enforce_delivery_status_transition() —
  IF (OLD.status = 'scheduled' AND NEW.status IN ('in_progress', 'cancelled'))
  OR (OLD.status = 'in_progress' AND NEW.status IN ('completed', 'cancelled'))
  THEN
    RETURN NEW;

guard_completed_delivery_signature() —
  IF NEW.status = 'completed' AND NULLIF(btrim(COALESCE(NEW.signed_by, '')), '') IS NULL THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'SIGNATURE_REQUIRED';

src/pages/FieldStop.tsx:351 — await supabase.from('deliveries').update({ signature_url: filePath }).eq('id', id).select();

**Failure scenario.** A driver assigned to delivery D (status 'in_progress') sends PATCH /rest/v1/deliveries?id=eq.D with {"status":"completed","signed_by":"J. Doe"} using their own session token. RLS passes (assigned_driver = auth.uid(), old and new status both in the allowed set), the transition trigger passes, the signature guard passes. Result: delivery shows completed; on-hand inventory is never decremented; order_items.quantity_delivered stays 0 so the order never advances to partially_fulfilled/fulfilled; no invoice is generated so the customer is never billed; no financial_audit_log row exists. Net Position reports stock that has physically left the yard as available, and the load is delivered free.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on the facts; severity corrected BLOCKER -> HIGH.

Independent verification (I decompressed the DR baseline `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br` and re-derived every object rather than trusting the reviewer's citations):

1. Policy is current, not superseded. `del_update` in `supabase/migrations/20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:83-93` is reproduced verbatim in the baseline at `public_schema.sql:65825`: `USING ((is_admin() OR is_sales_rep() OR ((assigned_driver = (SELECT auth.uid())) AND (status = ANY (ARRAY['in_progress','completed'])))))` with an identical WITH CHECK. I grepped every migration timestamped after the baseline (20260727231652 through 20260807220323) for policy/grant/trigger changes on `deliveries` — zero hits. Nothing supersedes it.

2. Grant is table-level, not column-level. `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:174` — `GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.deliveries TO authenticated;` and baseline `public_schema.sql:73758` `GRANT ALL ON TABLE "public"."deliveries" TO "authenticated"`. No column list restricts the driver to signature columns — the old policy name `del_driver_signature_only` (dropped at 20260511060000:81) was aspirational; RLS cannot restrict columns.

3. Trigger inventory is complete and none does the RPC's work. Baseline `CREATE TRIGGER` lines on `public.deliveries`: `enforce_delivery_status_transition` (60343), `guard_delivery_delete` (60413), `trg_delivery_status_change` (60784), `trg_enforce_delivery_accounting_period` (60798), `trg_guard_completed_delivery_signature` (60847). I read all four relevant bodies:
   - `_enforce_delivery_status_transition` (public_schema.sql:3871) explicitly permits `OLD.status = 'in_progress' AND NEW.status IN ('completed','cancelled') THEN RETURN NEW;`
   - `guard_completed_delivery_signature` (32359) only raises `SIGNATURE_REQUIRED` when `signed_by` is blank — caller-supplied.
   - `enforce_delivery_accounting_period` (24456) only calls `check_period_open()`; passes for an open period.
   - `trg_delivery_status_change` (47400) only inserts an `activity_feed` row.
   No trigger touches inventory, order_items, invoices, or financial_audit_log, and none requires a "completed via RPC" marker (no `current_setting`/session-flag gate exists anywhere on this path).

4. No table constraint blocks it. `CREATE TABLE public.deliveries` has only `deliveries_issue_type_check`, `deliveries_priority_check`, `deliveries_status_check` (which lists 'completed' as legal); `completed_at` is nullable with no status-conditional CHECK. So `{"status":"completed","signed_by":"x"}` alone satisfies every DB check.

Conclusion on mechanism: this is genuinely "THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE that completion routes through complete_delivery" — not "I could not find the enforcement." I looked for it and it is absent.

Not previously documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` has no entry for `del_update`/`assigned_driver`/delivery-status direct writes (grep returned nothing). The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline contains a single HIGH about Bulk Order Import and nothing about deliveries. The nearest prior coverage actively *misses* this: `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-07-26-section-04-...:87` records "Skipping delivery confirm -> complete: refuted" reasoning only from the RPC path, and `docs/audits/2026-07-15-graph-workflow-analysis.md:111` says "inspected UI paths call confirm_delivery before complete_delivery; no bypass was confirmed" — both are UI/RPC-path statements, neither examines the table-write surface. The project has closed this exact class elsewhere (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:216 removes direct INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `quote_sections`/`quote_items`/`customer_addresses` from browser roles), which confirms it is treated as a real defect rather than accepted design — deliveries was simply not covered.

Severity correction to HIGH, on three grounds the finding overstates:
(a) Not reachable from the app. I grepped every frontend write to the table: only `src/pages/FieldStop.tsx:351` `.update({ signature_url: filePath })`. No code path in `src/` sets `deliveries.status` directly, so this cannot fire accidentally — it requires a deliberate hand-crafted PostgREST call.
(b) Not a privilege escalation. `supabase/migrations/20260716173342_authorize_delivery_before_replay.sql:80-85` authorizes `v_actor_role = 'driver' AND v_actor = v_delivery.assigned_driver` to call `complete_delivery`. The assigned driver may already legitimately complete that delivery; the bypass buys them a *worse-formed* completion, not access they lacked. The exposure is insider abuse/self-dealing, not an outsider or a lower-privileged actor.
(c) A detective control exists for part of the damage: `supabase/migrations/20260709230000_u18_safety_nets.sql:329-338` surfaces `unbilled_deliveries` ("Completed deliveries not covered by an active delivery- or order-level invoice"), so the missing-invoice half of the failure is dashboard-visible, and `IntegrityCleanupPanel.tsx:142` reads the same class (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:918 shows five such historical rows were in fact caught this way). The inventory-decrement and order-progression halves remain silently wrong, which is why this is HIGH and not MED.

One correction that strengthens, not weakens, the finding: the reviewer scoped it to drivers, but the first two disjuncts `is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()` are unconditional — an office user can drive any delivery through any transition the trigger allows on any row, so the bypassable surface is wider than "the assigned driver."

---

### [HIGH] get_customer_year_end_summary is an ungated SECURITY DEFINER RPC granted to authenticated — any role reads any customer's full financial history

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260228200000_season_calendar_oct_sep.sql:843-873 (definition); baseline public_schema.sql line 27329 (live body, 2026-07-27); src/pages/CustomerDetail.tsx:896 (caller)

**Detail.** `get_customer_year_end_summary(p_customer_id uuid, p_season integer)` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, granted `EXECUTE` to `authenticated`, and its body contains no `auth.uid()` check, no `is_admin()`/`is_sales_rep()` check, and no profile/active check of any kind — the first executable statement is `SELECT * INTO v_cust FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id`. Being SECURITY DEFINER it bypasses RLS on `customers`, `invoices`, and `invoice_items`. It returns: customer PII (email, phone, billing address, assigned_tier, payment_terms), `total_invoiced_cents`, `total_paid_cents`, `prepay_applied_cents`, `outstanding_balance_cents`, per-product spend and acreage, and the full invoice list with `balance_cents`. This crosses two deliberate boundaries at once: `customers_select` restricts a sales_rep to `assigned_sales_rep = auth.uid()`, and `invoices_select` restricts to `created_by`/`salesman_id`. This is the identical shape as `compute_application_service_fee` (KNOWN_ISSUES section 0, fixed 2026-07-28 by 20260728182141_secdef_pricing_reads_office_only) but on a different function that migration never touched — it is a gap in that remediation, not a re-report of it. I confirmed by scanning all 527 function bodies in the DR baseline against the `authenticated` EXECUTE grants in acl_lockdown.sql; no later migration re-emits or revokes this function.

**Evidence.** 20260228200000_season_calendar_oct_sep.sql:843-862 —
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.get_customer_year_end_summary(
  p_customer_id uuid,
  p_season      integer
)
RETURNS jsonb
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY DEFINER
SET search_path = public
AS $$
DECLARE ...
BEGIN
  -- ── Customer info ──
  SELECT * INTO v_cust FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id;
  IF NOT FOUND THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Customer not found: %', p_customer_id;

(no auth.uid(), is_admin(), is_sales_rep(), or profiles lookup appears anywhere in the body)

returned payload includes:
    'total_invoiced_cents', COALESCE(SUM(total_amount_cents), 0),
    'total_paid_cents', COALESCE(SUM(paid_amount_cents), 0),
    'outstanding_balance_cents', COALESCE(SUM(balance_cents), 0),

Boundary it bypasses — customers_select (baseline line 65607):
  USING ((is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND (assigned_sales_rep = (SELECT auth.uid()))) OR (is_driver() AND (EXISTS ...))))

**Failure scenario.** An active driver (or any sales_rep) obtains a customer uuid — trivially available to a driver from their own delivery rows, or to a sales_rep from `global_search` — and POSTs /rest/v1/rpc/get_customer_year_end_summary {"p_customer_id":"<uuid>","p_season":2026}. They receive that customer's email, phone, billing address, payment terms, total invoiced/paid, outstanding AR balance, complete invoice list with per-invoice balances, and per-product spend, for a customer they are not assigned to and whose invoices `invoices_select` would never return to them.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — could not refute; the finding is accurate and, if anything, understated.

VERIFIED AGAINST SOURCE + LIVE DR BASELINE:

1. Definition is current and ungated. `supabase/migrations/20260228200000_season_calendar_oct_sep.sql:843-849` — `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.get_customer_year_end_summary(p_customer_id uuid, p_season integer) RETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public`. First executable statement at :862 is `SELECT * INTO v_cust FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id;`.

2. LIVE body confirms it (this is the decisive check, not just the migration). Decompressed `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br`; function body spans lines 27329-27525 (`$$;` terminator at 27525). I grepped the entire 197-line body for `auth.uid`, `is_admin`, `is_sales_rep`, `is_driver`, `is_active_profile`, `AUTH_REQUIRED`, `INSUFFICIENT_ROLE` — ZERO matches. Live definition is `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path TO 'public','pg_temp'`. (Minor correction to the finding: live pins `pg_temp`, the migration text does not; immaterial to the vulnerability.)

3. Grant confirmed twice. Baseline ACL line 71083: `GRANT ALL ON FUNCTION "public"."get_customer_year_end_summary"(...) TO "authenticated";`. Also `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:1092`. And `20260529214355_revoke_anon_execute_on_report_dashboard_secdef.sql:61` explicitly re-grants `TO authenticated, service_role` after revoking anon — so the `authenticated` grant is deliberate and standing.

4. NO later migration supersedes it. I enumerated every migration file with a timestamp greater than the 2026-07-27 baseline and grepped each for `year_end_summary`: zero hits. Across the whole `supabase/migrations/` tree only two files ever emit this function (`20260224200000_year_end_summary.sql`, `20260228200000_season_calendar_oct_sep.sql`); every other reference is a comment or a caller. `20260728182141_secdef_pricing_reads_office_only` does not touch it — the reviewer's "gap in that remediation" framing is correct, and KNOWN_ISSUES section 0 scopes that remediation to the 20 SECDEF readers of `quote_items`/`quote_versions`/`customer_application_rates`/`rebate_programs`, a different table set than this function's `customers`/`invoices`/`invoice_items`.

5. NOT already documented. `grep -n "get_customer_year_end_summary\|year_end"` over `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` and `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md` returns zero hits in both. No predicate under `scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/` covers it either.

6. Boundaries it bypasses are exactly as quoted. `customers_select` (baseline 65607): `is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND assigned_sales_rep = auth.uid()) OR (is_driver() AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM deliveries d WHERE d.customer_id = customers.id AND d.assigned_driver = auth.uid() AND d.scheduled_date >= CURRENT_DATE - '1 day'))` — so a driver's legitimate customer visibility is scoped to their own recent deliveries. `invoices_select` (baseline 66652): `is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid()`. SECURITY DEFINER owned by postgres bypasses both. Payload confirmed at baseline 27498-27512: `email`, `phone`, `billing_address`, `city/state/zip`, `assigned_tier`, `payment_terms`, plus `financial` (`total_invoiced_cents`, `total_paid_cents`, `prepay_applied_cents`, `outstanding_balance_cents`), the full per-invoice list with `balance_cents`, per-product spend, acreage, grower `shares` with `amount_cents`/`price_per_acre_cents`, and prior-season totals.

MATERIAL ADDITION THE REVIEWER MISSED (amplifies, does not refute): the batch wrapper `get_batch_year_end_summaries(uuid[], integer)` — baseline lines 25855-25872, ACL line 70912 `GRANT ALL ... TO "authenticated"` — is likewise `SECURITY DEFINER`, has no gate of its own, and loops `SELECT public.get_customer_year_end_summary(v_cid, p_season)` over an arbitrary caller-supplied uuid array. One POST with N uuids exfiltrates N customers' full financial history and PII in a single call. Any fix must gate both functions.

FEASIBILITY: the failure scenario is realistic and has direct precedent — KNOWN_ISSUES section 0 records a real live impersonation of an active driver (`5bfbf33a-…`) successfully executing the structurally identical ungated SECDEF `compute_application_service_fee` via PostgREST with the field user's own JWT, and states exposure is 5 active non-office accounts (2 driver, 1 applicator, 2 customer-facing `entity_recipient`). The `entity_recipient` accounts make this customer-facing, not merely internal.

SCOPE CAVEAT ON MY OWN VERIFICATION: this is a source-and-live-catalog confirmation (migrations + the 2026-07-27 DR baseline), not a live behavioral proof — I did not execute the RPC or impersonate a role, per read-only constraints. The 2026-07-27 baseline is ~13 days stale, but I closed that gap by confirming no migration after it touches the function.

SEVERITY: HIGH stands. Same class as the `compute_application_service_fee` HIGH, with a broader payload (AR balances + PII vs. pricing) and a bulk-enumeration wrapper, but not a BLOCKER because it is a confidentiality read with no write/money-mutation path.

---

### [HIGH] Sales reps can create orders and order lines by direct PostgREST insert, bypassing every canonical order-creation effect and the confirmed-only status rule

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:12-24 (orders_insert / oitems_insert); supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:400,415 (authenticated INSERT grants); baseline public_schema.sql line 53587-53614 (orders DDL, orders_status_check)

**Detail.** `orders_insert` and `oitems_insert` are `WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())` with no `TO` clause, and `authenticated` holds table-level INSERT on both `public.orders` and `public.order_items`. `orders.status` has a DEFAULT of 'confirmed' but its CHECK permits 'confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided', and I found no BEFORE INSERT trigger on `orders` constraining status — the only status trigger, `enforce_order_status_transition`, is `BEFORE UPDATE OF status`. So THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE confirmed-only order creation on the direct-table path. The canonical path (`create_direct_order`, migration 20260614142939:172-205) performs inventory prebooking, a 'booked' inventory_transactions row, `_insert_commissions_for_order`, and an activity_feed row; none of these are triggers, so a direct insert produces an order with zero of them. This is the same defect class the 2026-08-05 Section 4 HIGH closed — but that remediation hardened `bulk_import_order` (the RPC), leaving the raw table path, which is available to the same role, untouched. `oitems_insert` additionally carries no parent-order ownership or terminal-status predicate, so a sales_rep may insert lines into any order including one already fulfilled and invoiced; `after_order_items_change` then fires `trg_recalc_order_totals`, rewriting `total_price`/`total_cost`/`total_profit`/`total_margin_pct` on someone else's order.

**Evidence.** 20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:12-24 —
CREATE POLICY "orders_insert" ON orders
  FOR INSERT
  WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep());
...
CREATE POLICY "oitems_insert" ON order_items
  FOR INSERT
  WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep());

acl_lockdown.sql:400,415 —
GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.order_items TO authenticated;
GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.orders TO authenticated;

orders DDL —
    "status" "text" DEFAULT 'confirmed'::"text" NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT "orders_status_check" CHECK (("status" = ANY (ARRAY['confirmed'::"text", 'partially_fulfilled'::"text", 'fulfilled'::"text", 'cancelled'::"text", 'voided'::"text"]))),

Triggers present on orders (DR baseline): enforce_order_status_transition (BEFORE UPDATE OF status), guard_order_customer_source_lineage (BEFORE UPDATE OF customer_id), guard_order_delivered_activity_cancel (BEFORE UPDATE OF status, deleted_at), _guard_order_delete (BEFORE DELETE), trg_order_status_change (AFTER UPDATE), trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF commission_split). None constrains status on INSERT.

Canonical effects skipped — 20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:172-205 (inventory prebook + booked ledger row, _insert_commissions_for_order, activity_feed insert).

**Failure scenario.** An active sales_rep POSTs /rest/v1/orders with {"order_number":"ORD-2026-9999","customer_id":"...","status":"fulfilled","salesman_id":"<their id>"} then POSTs order_items lines. The order exists as 'fulfilled' with every line at quantity_delivered = 0, no inventory reserved or consumed, no commission row, and no activity_feed record — the exact impossible fulfilment state the Section 4 remediation eliminated from bulk_import_order. Separately, a sales_rep POSTs a new order_items row against another rep's already-invoiced order; trg_recalc_order_totals rewrites that order's total_price/total_profit/total_margin_pct while the posted invoice (built from invoice_items) stays unchanged, silently desynchronising order profit from billed revenue.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim verified against current source, the 2026-07-27 live dump, and all post-baseline migrations; no missed enforcement mechanism exists.\n\n(1) POLICIES AS QUOTED, NEVER SUPERSEDED. supabase/migrations/20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:12-24 matches verbatim. Only two migrations in the entire 859-file tree mention orders_insert/oitems_insert (20260206174345 and 20260210); nothing later replaces them. The live baseline still carries them: baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql:67466 `CREATE POLICY \"orders_insert\" ON \"public\".\"orders\" FOR INSERT WITH CHECK ((\"public\".\"is_admin\"() OR \"public\".\"is_sales_rep\"()));` and :67364 the identical oitems_insert. No TO clause, as claimed.\n\n(2) GRANTS CONFIRMED. baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql: `GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.orders TO authenticated;` and the identical line for public.order_items. No REVOKE of these in any later migration (grep of all REVOKE statements returns nothing touching orders/order_items tables).\n\n(3) THE DB GENUINELY DOES NOT ENFORCE CONFIRMED-ONLY ON INSERT — this is a checked-the-migrations claim, not a could-not-find claim. Complete trigger set on orders in the live dump: enforce_order_status_transition (:60371, BEFORE UPDATE OF status), guard_order_customer_source_lineage (BEFORE UPDATE OF customer_id), guard_order_delete (:60455, BEFORE DELETE), guard_order_delivered_activity_cancel (BEFORE UPDATE OF status, deleted_at), trg_order_status_change (AFTER UPDATE), trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF commission_split). Not one constrains status on INSERT. orders_status_check at public_schema.sql:53614 permits confirmed/partially_fulfilled/fulfilled/cancelled/voided. Only THREE CREATE TRIGGER statements exist across every post-baseline migration (20260807215532 trg_guard_profile_role_lock; 20260730235031 quotes_bump_row_version and customers_bump_row_version) — none on orders or order_items.\n\n(4) THE SECTION-4 REMEDIATION DOES NOT REACH THIS PATH. 20260805211951_harden_bulk_order_import_lifecycle.sql contains zero CREATE TRIGGER, zero CREATE POLICY, and zero table-level GRANT/REVOKE — its only ACL statements are REVOKE/GRANT EXECUTE on the RPC at :289 and :293. The 2026-08-05 gauntlet doc scopes its HIGH strictly to bulk_import_order and even corroborates the underlying gap: \"Live `orders_status_check` permits partial and terminal statuses, so the database CHECK did not reject the structurally inconsistent rows.\" The raw-table path is a distinct, unremediated instance of the same defect class.\n\n(5) NOT PREVIOUSLY DOCUMENTED. Zero hits for orders_insert/oitems_insert across all of docs/ and .claude/. No KNOWN_ISSUES entry, no accepted-residual note, no DECISION_LOG entry covering direct-table order writes.\n\n(6) FAILURE SCENARIO IS REACHABLE. orders requires only order_number (text NOT NULL) and customer_id; status defaults to confirmed but is freely settable within the CHECK. is_sales_rep() (public_schema.sql) requires only role='sales_rep' AND is_active=true, so any active rep's own JWT satisfies the WITH CHECK. The canonical effects the reviewer lists live only inside create_direct_order (20260614142939:172-205) as function body, not as triggers, so a direct insert produces none of them.\n\nTWO POINTS THAT STRENGTHEN THE FINDING BEYOND WHAT THE REVIEWER CLAIMED:\n(a) Genuine RLS boundary crossing, not merely a lifecycle gap. orders_update (public_schema.sql:67480) and oitems_update (:67378) are ADMIN-ONLY — `FOR UPDATE TO \"authenticated\" USING (\"public\".\"is_admin\"()) WITH CHECK (\"public\".\"is_admin\"())`. Yet trg_recalc_order_totals is SECURITY DEFINER and executes `UPDATE orders SET total_price = ..., total_cost = ..., total_profit = ..., total_margin_pct = ...` on the parent. A sales rep therefore drives a privileged rewrite of an orders row they are explicitly denied UPDATE on, by inserting an order_items row — and oitems_insert has no parent-ownership or terminal-status predicate, while orders_select/oitems_select let any rep see every order, so target order_ids are trivially discoverable.\n(b) The path is entirely UI-unreachable, so revocation is zero-cost. Zero .insert() calls against orders or order_items anywhere in src/ or supabase/functions/; NewOrder.tsx:516 uses supabase.rpc('create_direct_order', ...). The repo already set this exact precedent in 20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard, which stripped direct INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on quote_sections/quote_items/customer_addresses from normal browser roles for the same reason (KNOWN_ISSUES:216).\n\nMINOR EVIDENCE ERRORS, NON-LOAD-BEARING: the reviewer's trigger list omits trg_snapshot_order_item_cost (public_schema.sql:61099, BEFORE INSERT ON order_items — only fills cost_at_time_cents from products.current_cost, constrains nothing) and guard_order_item_delivery_lineage (BEFORE UPDATE OF order_id, product_id), and names the delete trigger _guard_order_delete when the trigger is guard_order_delete (the function is _guard_order_delete). Neither changes the conclusion.\n\nSEVERITY: HIGH sustained. It requires an authenticated active sales rep deliberately calling PostgREST outside the app rather than an ordinary UI action, which argues for MED under a strict honest-mistake threat model. But this repo has consistently rated direct-PostgREST-reachable defects as serious (KNOWN_ISSUES 0d profile_public_view RLS bypass; the SECDEF pricing reads reachable via direct /rpc/ at KNOWN_ISSUES:682), and the admin-only-UPDATE bypass in (a) is a real privilege-boundary violation on top of the lifecycle gap. Consequences are money- and inventory-bearing: unreserved stock feeding oversell through Net Position, missing commission liability, no activity_feed audit record, impossible fulfilled-with-zero-delivered state, and cross-rep desynchronisation of order profit from billed invoice revenue.

---

### [MED] check_customer_credit_limit and get_customer_summary are ungated SECURITY DEFINER readers of AR — any authenticated role reads any customer's outstanding balance and credit limit

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260712130000_credit_limit_count_unposted.sql:4-48 (check_customer_credit_limit); supabase/migrations/20260404040200_get_customer_summary_rpc.sql:4 and baseline public_schema.sql line 27165 (get_customer_summary)

**Detail.** Both are `SECURITY DEFINER`, both are explicitly granted `EXECUTE TO authenticated`, and neither body contains `auth.uid()`, `is_admin()`, `is_sales_rep()`, a `profiles` lookup, or any other authorization test. `check_customer_credit_limit` returns `credit_limit`, `outstanding_ar`, `available_credit` and `farm_name` for an arbitrary `p_customer_id`, reading `invoices` under definer rights. `get_customer_summary` returns `ar_balance_cents` (sum of `balance_cents` over posted/overdue invoices), season order count, completed delivery count, credit tier, and last activity. Both bypass `customers_select` (which scopes a sales_rep to `assigned_sales_rep = auth.uid()`) and `invoices_select`. These are the same ungated-SECDEF-reader class as the two functions fixed by 20260728182141, and neither was in that migration's scope.

**Evidence.** 20260712130000_credit_limit_count_unposted.sql:4-13,47-48 —
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.check_customer_credit_limit(p_customer_id uuid)
 RETURNS jsonb
 LANGUAGE plpgsql
 SECURITY DEFINER
 SET search_path TO 'public', 'pg_temp'
AS $function$
...
BEGIN
  -- Get customer info
  SELECT credit_limit_cents, farm_name
  INTO v_credit_limit_cents, v_farm_name
  FROM customers
  WHERE id = p_customer_id;
...
REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.check_customer_credit_limit(uuid) FROM anon, public;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.check_customer_credit_limit(uuid) TO authenticated;

(returns) 'exceeded', 'credit_limit', 'outstanding_ar', 'available_credit', 'farm_name'

get_customer_summary (baseline line 27165) —
CREATE FUNCTION "public"."get_customer_summary"("p_customer_id" "uuid") RETURNS "jsonb"
    LANGUAGE "plpgsql" SECURITY DEFINER
    SET "search_path" TO 'public', 'pg_temp'
...
  SELECT COALESCE(sum(balance_cents), 0)
  INTO v_ar_balance
  FROM invoices
  WHERE customer_id = p_customer_id
    AND status IN ('posted', 'overdue');

(no auth or role test anywhere in either body)

**Failure scenario.** A sales_rep assigned to zero of Farm X's accounts, or an applicator, POSTs /rest/v1/rpc/check_customer_credit_limit {"p_customer_id":"<Farm X>"} and receives Farm X's credit limit and exact outstanding AR; a follow-up call to get_customer_summary yields the same customer's AR balance, order and delivery counts, and credit tier. Competitive/commercially sensitive account standing for customers outside the caller's assignment is exposed to every signed-in account in the company.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — the factual core survives every refutation attempt, but severity should be MED, not HIGH.

WHAT I VERIFIED (not "did not find"; I read the migrations and the live-state baseline).

1. Bodies are ungated — confirmed on disk AND in live capture. `supabase/migrations/20260712130000_credit_limit_count_unposted.sql:4-46` is the last migration in the repo to define `check_customer_credit_limit`; its body goes `BEGIN` → `SELECT credit_limit_cents, farm_name ... FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id;` with no `auth.uid()`, no `is_admin()`/`is_sales_rep()`, no `profiles` read. Same for `get_customer_summary` at `supabase/migrations/20260404040200_get_customer_summary_rpc.sql:4-79`. Independently confirmed against the live-state capture: `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br:15495-15533` and `:27165+` contain the identical ungated bodies. The reviewer's exact citation "baseline public_schema.sql line 27165" is correct.

2. No superseding migration exists. A name grep over all of `supabase/migrations/` returns exactly six files mentioning either function (`20260303200000`, `20260315200000`, `20260404040200`, `20260429140635`, `20260529214355`, `20260712130000`); the two most recent that alter anything are `20260712130000` (body, no gate) and `20260529214355_revoke_anon_execute_on_report_dashboard_secdef.sql:56-57`, which does the opposite of a fix — it *re-asserts* the grant: `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.get_customer_summary(p_customer_id uuid) TO authenticated, service_role;`. Nothing after `20260728233459` (current live high-water per KNOWN_ISSUES) touches either.

3. The grant is live, not merely declared. `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:844-845` and `:1088-1089` show `authenticated` holding EXECUTE on both as captured from the live database.

4. The bypassed RLS is real. `supabase/migrations/20260510070000_tighten_customer_profile_rls.sql:52-77` — `customers_select` is `is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND assigned_sales_rep = (SELECT auth.uid()))` plus narrow driver/applicator windows. `supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:256-262` — `invoices_select` is `is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid()`. SECDEF bypasses both, so a non-assigned sales_rep, a driver, an applicator, or a customer-facing `entity_recipient` gets data RLS would deny.

5. Not covered by any existing guard. `scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/auth-bound-role-ungated.sql` requires `prosrc ~* '(insert into|update ... set|delete from)'` AND `prosrc ~* 'auth\.uid'` — these are pure readers with no `auth.uid()`, so it cannot fire. `office-only-pricing-secdef-gates.sql` is explicitly "exact-name, not heuristic" and pins only `compute_application_service_fee` and `get_program_completion`.

6. Not in either exclusion document. Grep for both function names across `docs/manual/` and `docs/audits/` (excluding unrelated hits) returns zero security mentions; `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` has no occurrence of either name. The 2026-08-05 gauntlet section-04 file mentions neither. KNOWN_ISSUES open item (b) around line 681 is scoped strictly to the four office-only *pricing* tables (`quote_items`, `quote_versions`, `customer_application_rates`, `rebate_programs`) — neither of these functions reads any of them, so the finding is outside that documented item.

7. The route guard is not a control on this path. `src/App.tsx:209-210` gates `/customers/:id` to `['admin','sales_rep']`, but the KNOWN_ISSUES §0 precedent settles that this does not protect the PostgREST `/rpc/` endpoint — `get_program_completion` was still treated as a live finding despite having only route-gated callers. The project has also demonstrably recognized this exact class for AR data: `20260716190000_harden_sales_financial_scope.sql:29-61` wraps `get_customer_statement` in a public wrapper doing `AUTH_REQUIRED` → `SELECT role FROM profiles WHERE id = v_actor AND is_active = true` → `INSUFFICIENT_ROLE` for non-admin/sales_rep, and `docs/loops/crm-relationship-intelligence-ledger.md:80` records Sol's instruction to "match get_customer_statement, NOT get_customer_summary". Both functions were left out of that hardening.

SEVERITY CORRECTION HIGH → MED. Three mitigators the finding does not account for:
(a) `check_customer_credit_limit` is nearly inert on live data. `docs/audits/2026-08-04-crm-functional-and-coverage-audit.md:52` states 149 of 150 customers have `credit_limit_cents = 0`, and the body's `IF v_credit_limit_cents IS NULL OR v_credit_limit_cents <= 0 THEN` short-circuit returns `credit_limit: 0, outstanding_ar: 0` plus only `farm_name`. So for ~99% of the book that function today leaks nothing beyond a farm name. The stated failure scenario ("receives Farm X's credit limit and exact outstanding AR") is currently reachable for one customer, not all.
(b) Read-only. No mutation, no money movement, no lifecycle effect — this is a confidentiality issue only.
(c) The blast radius is intra-company. Exposure is signed-in CRX accounts; per KNOWN_ISSUES §0 that is 5 active non-office accounts (2 driver, 1 applicator, 2 customer-facing `entity_recipient`) plus cross-rep leakage among sales reps.

`get_customer_summary` carries no such mitigator — it returns `ar_balance_cents` unconditionally over posted/overdue invoices, plus order/delivery counts and credit tier, for any `p_customer_id`. That is what keeps this at MED rather than LOW, and it matches the repo's own MED rating for the structurally identical `get_program_completion`. The fix is the house pattern already used twice: the `AUTH_REQUIRED`/`INSUFFICIENT_ROLE` in-body gate plus, for `get_customer_summary`, an assigned-rep scope check mirroring `customers_select`.
