### [MED] create_invoice_from_order / create_split_invoices_from_order accept an unvalidated caller-supplied p_salesman_id that is itself an RLS read-grant key

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** baseline public_schema.sql lines 20575 (create_invoice_from_order), 2886 (_create_invoice_from_order_idem_impl_20260721), 2957 (_create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718), 22403 (create_split_invoices_from_order); src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:864,880; supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:256-262 (invoices_select); scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/actor-forgery.sql:29

**Detail.** `p_salesman_id` is written straight into `invoices.salesman_id` as `COALESCE(p_salesman_id, v_order.salesman_id)`. The function derives `v_actor := auth.uid()` and gates on `is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()`, but never requires `p_salesman_id = v_actor` and never requires the supplied uuid to be an active sales_rep. `invoices.salesman_id` is not merely attribution: `invoices_select` grants full row read on `salesman_id = auth.uid()`, and `invoice_items_select` inherits it — and `invoice_items.cost_cents` / `invoices.total_cost_cents` are internal cost. So an unvalidated caller-supplied parameter directly controls who can read internal cost on that invoice, with no role predicate on that arm. The frontend always passes `profile.id` (OrderDetail.tsx:864,880), which is also a second, non-adversarial problem: any sales_rep pressing "Create Invoice" on another rep's order silently reassigns the invoice's salesman to themselves, moving `get_sales_detail_report`/`get_sales_summary_report` attribution. The standing `actor-forgery` predicate cannot catch this: it only considers argument names matching `^p_\w*by$|^p_actor|^p_user`, and `p_salesman_id` matches none of them.

**Evidence.** _create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718 —
  INSERT INTO invoices (
    order_id, customer_id, invoice_type, status, season,
    salesman_id, created_by, total_amount_cents, invoice_date
  ) VALUES (
    p_order_id, v_order.customer_id, p_invoice_type, 'draft',
    COALESCE(v_order.season, (SELECT current_season())),
    COALESCE(p_salesman_id, v_order.salesman_id),
    v_actor, 0, CURRENT_DATE
  )

(the only checks above it are:)
  IF v_actor IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Not authenticated'; END IF;
  IF NOT (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Not authorized: admin or sales role required to create invoices from orders';

20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:256-262 —
CREATE POLICY invoices_select ON invoices FOR SELECT
  USING (
    is_admin()
    OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid())
    OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid())
  );

scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/actor-forgery.sql:29 —
    AND a.argname ~* '^p_\w*by$|^p_actor|^p_user'

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:878-881 —
        const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc('create_invoice_from_order', {
          p_order_id: id,
          p_salesman_id: profile.id,
          p_idempotency_key: invoiceKey,
        });

**Failure scenario.** A sales_rep calls /rest/v1/rpc/create_invoice_from_order with p_salesman_id set to a driver's (or an entity_recipient's) uuid. The invoice is created with that uuid in salesman_id; `invoices_select` and `invoice_items_select` now return the whole invoice and every line — including `cost_cents` and `total_cost_cents` — to a field user who has no office role, defeating the office-only cost containment. In the everyday non-adversarial case, rep B invoices rep A's order from the UI and the invoice's salesman_id silently becomes B, shifting the sales reports and the read grant away from A.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on the DB facts, with one sub-claim refuted and severity corrected down to MED.

WHAT HOLDS (baseline decompressed from supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br via `node scripts/decompress-schema-baseline.mjs`; line numbers are that decompressed file):

1. The insert is exactly as quoted. `_create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718` lines 3011-3019: `INSERT INTO invoices (... salesman_id, created_by ...) VALUES (... COALESCE(p_salesman_id, v_order.salesman_id), v_actor, ...)`. The only guards above it are line 2972 `IF v_actor IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Not authenticated'` and 2973-2975 `IF NOT (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())`. No `p_salesman_id = v_actor` check, no role check on the supplied uuid. Same in the split path: `_create_split_invoices_from_order_provenance_impl_20260719` lines 3279-3283 `... COALESCE(p_salesman_id, v_order.salesman_id), auth.uid(), ...` with only the identical gate at 3118-3121.

2. THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE IT ELSEWHERE — checked, not merely "did not find". `invoices` has 11 triggers (lines 60357-61169: enforce_invoice_status_transition, guard_invoice_delete, guard_mono_invoice_split_billing_post, guard_split_invoice_provenance_identity, require_split_invoice_creation_claim, set_invoices_updated_at, trg_enforce_field_application_type_lock, trg_guard_invoice_terminal_order, trg_invoice_draft_insert, trg_snapshot_line_shares_on_post, trg_sync_blend_ticket_payment) and none validates salesman_id on INSERT. The only constraint on the column is `invoices_salesman_id_fkey FOREIGN KEY (salesman_id) REFERENCES profiles(id)` (62813-62817) — a driver's or entity_recipient's profile satisfies it. No CHECK constraint exists.

3. No later migration supersedes this. Baseline is 20260727174805; grepping supabase/migrations/ for files newer than that mentioning create_invoice_from_order returns ZERO. The only post-baseline salesman_id hits are read-side filters in 20260805151605 and 20260805171334. The baseline body is current.

4. The read-grant coupling is real. Line 66652: `CREATE POLICY invoices_select ON invoices FOR SELECT USING (is_admin() OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))` — no role predicate on that arm. Lines 66529-66531: `invoice_items_select` is an EXISTS over invoices repeating the same three-arm predicate, so it inherits. `authenticated` holds table SELECT on both (74098-74099, 74039-74040), and `invoice_items.cost_cents` / `invoices.total_cost_cents` sit on those rows. Both RPCs are `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO authenticated` (acl_lockdown lines 928, 953).

5. The sweep genuinely cannot catch it. scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/actor-forgery.sql:29 `AND a.argname ~* '^p_\w*by$|^p_actor|^p_user'` — `p_salesman_id` matches no alternative.

6. Corroboration the reviewer did not cite: the house pattern for this exact control exists and these two RPCs are the outliers. `save_field_app_invoice` (baseline 7043-7051): `v_req_salesman := (p_invoice->>'salesman_id')::uuid; IF is_admin() THEN v_salesman_id := v_req_salesman; ELSE IF v_req_salesman IS NOT NULL AND v_req_salesman IS DISTINCT FROM v_actor THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Not authorized: cannot attribute this invoice to another user (salesman_id)'`. `_save_invoice_scoped` wrapper (9411-9413) raises `SALESMAN_SCOPE_DENIED` on the same condition. The guard string appears in 9 migrations (20260623140000:97, 20260624010000:96, 20260624211000:100, 20260624212000:120, 20260625140000:210, 20260625141000:409, 20260630180000:138, 20260720233000:474, 20260721180000:155). So this is a recognized required control the order-invoice RPCs never received, not a deliberate choice.

7. Not previously documented. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no mention of p_salesman_id or attribution forgery; its section 0e (line 380) is a different, narrower open owner decision ("Sales reps can still recover internal application-service cost for invoices assigned to them"). docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md has zero hits for salesman/create_invoice_from_order — its single Finding is the Bulk Order Import lifecycle HIGH.

8. Frontend citations accurate: src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:864 and :880 both pass `p_salesman_id: profile.id` unconditionally, and orders_select (67473) is `USING (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())`, so any rep can open any other rep's order and press Create Invoice.

REFUTED SUB-CLAIM (strike from the finding): "moving get_sales_detail_report/get_sales_summary_report attribution" is FALSE. Both reports attribute and filter off ORDERS.salesman_id, never invoices.salesman_id — get_sales_summary_report lines 31369 (`o.salesman_id`), 31382 (`LEFT JOIN profiles rep ON rep.id = o.salesman_id`), 31389 (`p_sales_rep_id IS NULL OR o.salesman_id = p_sales_rep_id`), 31405/31431 (`WHEN 'sales_rep' THEN f.salesman_id`, f being the orders-derived CTE); get_sales_detail_report lines 31311, 31328 identically. Rewriting invoices.salesman_id shifts neither report. The real everyday consequence is narrower but still real — READ ACCESS moves: when rep B invoices rep A's order, both created_by and salesman_id become B, so rep A loses SELECT on the invoice for their own order, and invoices.salesman_id is also the visibility key in the field-application profitability report (28543 `OR i.salesman_id = auth.uid()`) and the AR/customer-balance RPCs (20260805151605:288,323,351; 20260805171334:55,90,118).

SEVERITY HIGH -> MED. The scenario is reachable exactly as described, but the caller must ALREADY be admin or sales_rep (`is_sales_rep()` = `role = 'sales_rep' AND is_active`), so this is not escalation by an unprivileged user. It is an insider-controlled disclosure channel: a rep who can already read that invoice's cost (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:380 records exactly that) deliberately hands one invoice's cost_cents/total_cost_cents to one non-office account. A genuine bypass of office-only cost containment and a genuine unvalidated-attribution integrity gap that contradicts the codebase's own guard — but the adversary must already be trusted and could otherwise simply read out the numbers, so it lacks HIGH's immediate-production-risk weight.

---

### [MED] release_expired_quote_holds() is an ungated authenticated-callable SECURITY DEFINER mutator with no idempotency key, and the standing ungated-secdef-mutators predicate cannot see it

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260302200000_business_logic_enhancements.sql:1515; baseline public_schema.sql line 39438; scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/ungated-secdef-mutators.sql:29; src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:329

**Detail.** The function is `SECURITY DEFINER`, granted EXECUTE to `authenticated`, performs `UPDATE inventory_holds ... SET is_active = false`, and contains no `auth.uid()`, no role helper, and no `p_idempotency_key` — violating the CRX rule that mutating RPCs accept and enforce one. Separately, and more importantly for the guard system: the standing invariant predicate that exists precisely to catch this class matches DML with `p.prosrc ~* '(insert\s+into|update\s+\S+\s+set|delete\s+from)'`. The body writes `UPDATE inventory_holds ih SET` — the table alias sits between the relation name and `SET`, so `update\s+\S+\s+set` does not match and the row is never emitted. It is therefore missing from the sweep results and from the allowlist, which is why it reads as clean. I ran the same comparison across all 527 baseline function bodies: `release_expired_quote_holds` and `settle_applied_record_acres` (`UPDATE job_applied_records r SET`) are both ungated authenticated SECDEF mutators invisible to the predicate for the same aliasing reason. The predicate's blind spot is the durable finding; any future ungated mutator written with an alias will pass too.

**Evidence.** baseline public_schema.sql:39438 —
CREATE FUNCTION "public"."release_expired_quote_holds"() RETURNS "jsonb"
    LANGUAGE "plpgsql" SECURITY DEFINER
    SET "search_path" TO 'public', 'pg_temp'
    AS $$
DECLARE
  v_released integer;
BEGIN
  UPDATE inventory_holds ih SET
    is_active = false, updated_at = now()
  FROM quotes q
  WHERE ih.source_id = q.id
    AND ih.is_active = true
    AND q.status IN ('declined', 'expired');

scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/ungated-secdef-mutators.sql:29-33 —
  AND p.prosrc ~* '(insert\s+into|update\s+\S+\s+set|delete\s+from)'
  AND p.prosrc !~* 'auth\.uid'
  AND p.prosrc !~* 'require_admin'
  AND p.prosrc !~* 'is_admin'
  AND p.prosrc !~* 'is_sales_rep'

allowlist.json entries for this predicate: only log_failed_notification(...) and save_invoice(...) — release_expired_quote_holds and settle_applied_record_acres appear nowhere.

src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:329 — const { data: holdsData, error: holdsErr } = await supabase.rpc('release_expired_quote_holds');

**Failure scenario.** Direct impact is bounded — the UPDATE only touches holds whose quote is already declined or expired, so a hostile call converges to the same state a legitimate Dashboard load produces. The durable failure is the guard: someone adds a new authenticated-callable SECDEF RPC that writes `UPDATE orders o SET status = ...` with no role gate; every one of the 21 standing invariant predicates returns zero unallowlisted rows, the sweep is reported CLEAN, and the ungated mutator ships — exactly the 2026-06-09 round-2 class this predicate was built as a standing gate against.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on its durable core (the predicate blind spot), with two sub-claims corrected. I could not refute the regex gap and found no compensating mechanism.

VERIFIED — the regex gap is real and wider than reported. `scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/ungated-secdef-mutators.sql:29` is `AND p.prosrc ~* '(insert\s+into|update\s+\S+\s+set|delete\s+from)'`. Executed against the exact body text: `update\s+\S+\s+set` cannot match `UPDATE inventory_holds ih SET` — after `UPDATE `, `\S+` consumes `inventory_holds`, `\s+` consumes the space, and the next token is `ih `, not `set`; backtracking `\S+` shorter always lands on a non-whitespace char so `\s+` fails. Node check: `/(insert\s+into|update\s+\S+\s+set|delete\s+from)/i` returns false on the real body and true on `UPDATE inventory_holds SET x=1`. The body contains no other DML (`GET DIAGNOSTICS`, `RETURN jsonb_build_object`) and `updated_at` does not satisfy `update\s+`. The reviewer UNDERSTATED the blast radius: the identical regex is also line 24 of `scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/auth-bound-role-ungated.sql` — the W1/create_direct_order predicate — so the alias blind spot punches through BOTH standing role-gate predicates, not one.

VERIFIED — no compensating control. (a) Allowlist confirmed: `scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/allowlist.json` has exactly two `ungated-secdef-mutators` entries, `log_failed_notification(...)` (dated 2026-06-10) and `save_invoice(...)` (2026-07-20); `release_expired_quote_holds` appears nowhere, matching the reviewer. (b) `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md:261` and `:285` record the live sweep output as ungated-secdef-mutators = 1 allowlisted row — i.e. the function is genuinely absent from live sweep results, corroborating the miss against the real catalog, not just against a dump. (c) The predicate header documents only ONE known approximation (helper-gated functions); the alias gap is undocumented. (d) Authoring-time hooks do not cover it: `grep -n "SECURITY DEFINER|secdef|auth\.uid|require_admin|GRANT" .claude/hooks/sql-safety.mjs` returns nothing, and `.claude/hooks/idempotency-body-check.mjs:394` short-circuits with `if (!/p_idempotency_key\s+text/i.test(params)) continue;` — a new mutating RPC that simply omits the parameter is never inspected. Its own mutation test at :396 uses `\b(INSERT\s+INTO|UPDATE\s+|DELETE\s+FROM)\b`, which WOULD match an aliased UPDATE — direct proof the sweep predicate's `\S+\s+set` is an avoidable authoring defect, since the sibling guard in the same repo gets it right. (e) Neither the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline (line 77: "All 21 live invariant predicates: zero unallowlisted violations") nor KNOWN_ISSUES mentions predicate coverage limits — that "clean" is exactly the signal the blind spot produces.

CORRECTION 1 — the "ungated SECDEF mutator" half is a documented, deliberate disposition, not an undetected hole. `supabase/migrations/20260609203541_harden_maintenance_secdef_revoke_authenticated.sql:15-19` states verbatim: "NOT touched (explicitly justified, Codex #2/#3): log_failed_notification + release_expired_quote_holds are frontend-called (Dashboard.tsx / notificationTriggers.ts), already authenticated-only (anon revoked), and benign (write a failure-log row / release already-expired inventory holds). A hard auth.uid() gate would break their cron / service_role / Edge-function callers (auth.uid() is NULL there)". Two callers confirm the rationale: `src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:329` (`supabase.rpc('release_expired_quote_holds')`) and the pg_cron schedule at `supabase/migrations/20260501140000_field_app_workflow_phase19.sql:47-51` (`$$SELECT public.release_expired_quote_holds()$$`, 6:15 AM UTC). The grant at `20260302200000_business_logic_enhancements.sql:1537` is still live — no later `REVOKE`/`CREATE OR REPLACE` touches it (grep over all migrations returns only line 1515). So the function's ungated state is a settled accepted risk; the REAL process defect is that this disposition lives in a migration comment and was never written into `allowlist.json`, so the sweep reads clean by accident rather than by decision. That is a narrower but still valid finding.

CORRECTION 2 — the second example is weaker than claimed. `settle_applied_record_acres` (`supabase/migrations/20260624181000_fix_applied_acres_integrity.sql:97-121`, body `UPDATE job_applied_records r SET applied_acres = ...`) is REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon at :122-123 and is never explicitly GRANTed to `authenticated` anywhere in the migrations. Whether `has_function_privilege('authenticated', ...)` is true depends solely on Supabase's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` (referenced at `20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql:11-13`). I did not verify live ACL, so this instance is unproven from source. It is also trigger-invoked only (`PERFORM settle_applied_record_acres(...)` at :151/:155), so even if callable it is a convergent recompute.

CORRECTION 3 — the generalization is overstated. "any future ungated mutator written with an alias will pass too" is false as written: `insert\s+into` and `delete\s+from` match regardless of aliasing, so only a function whose ENTIRE DML set is aliased UPDATEs is invisible. The reviewer's own hypothetical (`UPDATE orders o SET status = ...`) slips through only if that function performs no INSERT or DELETE anywhere in its body — real, but a narrower window than stated.

NET: the failure scenario can occur, the fix is a one-token regex change (`update\s+\S+\s+set` → `update\s+` or an alias-tolerant `update\s+\S+(\s+(as\s+)?\S+)?\s+set`) in two predicate files, plus an allowlist entry recording the 20260609203541 disposition. Severity MED sustained — no live exploit (the UPDATE converges to the same state a Dashboard load or the 6:15 UTC cron produces), but it is a verified hole in the standing detection gate that section-04 and the gauntlet report as clean.

### [MED] Direct UPDATE of quotes.is_planned is unguarded — planned inventory holds desynchronise because holds are only reconciled on a status change

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260206174345_fix_security_and_performance_issues.sql:369 (quotes_update); supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:497 (authenticated UPDATE grant on quotes); baseline public_schema.sql line 39463 (release_holds_on_quote_status_change); supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:82-84

**Detail.** The 2026-07-30 row-version work deliberately revoked direct INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE from browser roles on `quote_sections`, `quote_items` and `customer_addresses`, routing those writes through `save_quote`. It did not do the same for the parent `quotes` table: `authenticated` still holds full DML there, and `quotes_update` lets an admin or the owning sales_rep write any column directly. Planned inventory holds are created and unwound inside `save_quote` / `_sync_planned_holds`; the only trigger that touches `inventory_holds` from the quotes table is `trg_release_holds_on_quote_status`, which fires `AFTER UPDATE OF status` only. I enumerated every trigger on `public.quotes` in the DR baseline (enforce_quote_accepted_fully_drawn, _enforce_quote_status_transition, trg_enforce_quote_terminal_not_drawn, trg_release_holds_on_quote_status, trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids, trg_validate_quote_commission_split, plus the post-baseline quotes_bump_row_version) — none is on `is_planned`. THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE hold/flag coherence for a direct `is_planned` write.

**Evidence.** 20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:82-84 (what was locked down) —
REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLE public.quote_sections FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;
REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLE public.quote_items FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;
REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLE public.customer_addresses FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;
(public.quotes is absent from this list)

acl_lockdown.sql:497 —
GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.quotes TO authenticated;

release_holds_on_quote_status_change() — the only hold-touching trigger, and it keys on status:
  IF NEW.status IN ('accepted', 'declined', 'expired', 'cancelled', 'closed_by_application', 'closed_short')
     AND OLD.status NOT IN (...) THEN
    UPDATE public.inventory_holds
    SET is_active = false, updated_at = now()
    WHERE source_id = NEW.id AND is_active = true;

CREATE TRIGGER trg_release_holds_on_quote_status AFTER UPDATE OF "status" ON "public"."quotes" ...

**Failure scenario.** A sales_rep sends PATCH /rest/v1/quotes?id=eq.<their quote> with {"is_planned": false}. RLS passes (they are created_by), no trigger fires on that column, and the quote's active `inventory_holds` rows survive with `is_planned = false` — product stays reserved against a quote that is no longer a plan, understating Net Position indefinitely. The inverse write ({"is_planned": true}) marks the quote as planned with no holds created at all, so the same units are offered for sale twice.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on mechanism; two impact claims are wrong and are corrected below. Severity stays MED.

VERIFIED AGAINST SOURCE
1. Grant is real and never revoked. supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:497 — "GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.quotes TO authenticated;". I searched every file under supabase/migrations/ for a later "REVOKE ... ON [TABLE] public.quotes": zero hits. The 2026-07-30 lockdown (supabase/migrations/20260730235031_quote_customer_row_version_guard.sql:80-82) revokes only quote_sections, quote_items, customer_addresses — public.quotes is absent, as claimed.
2. Policy is real. supabase/migrations/20260206174345_fix_security_and_performance_issues.sql:369-377 defines quotes_update with USING/WITH CHECK = is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND created_by = auth.uid()); the live shape matches the DR baseline (public_schema.sql:68045). No column-level restriction anywhere.
3. Trigger enumeration is correct and complete. Grepping the decompressed baseline for triggers ON "public"."quotes" returns exactly six (public_schema.sql:60385, 60392, 60812, 61057, 61127, 61225): four are "BEFORE/AFTER UPDATE OF status", two are "OF commission_split". The only post-baseline CREATE TRIGGER on quotes across all migrations is quotes_bump_row_version (20260730235031:64), "BEFORE UPDATE ... EXECUTE FUNCTION public.bump_record_row_version()", whose body is only "NEW.row_version := OLD.row_version + 1" — it does not block or reconcile anything. THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE is_planned/hold coherence on a direct write; this is a migration-verified negative, not a failure to find.
4. Reconciliation really is RPC-only. public._sync_planned_holds (public_schema.sql:11003) self-gates on "IF NOT v_quote.is_planned OR v_quote.status NOT IN ('draft','sent','revised') THEN ... release" and its own comment says it "additionally covers is_planned switched off without a status change" (11026-11027) — but every call site is a function body (save_quote 11243, restore_quote_version 21096, cancel/order paths 910/1631/12114), never a trigger. release_holds_on_quote_status_change (39463) keys on status exactly as quoted.
5. Not previously documented. Zero occurrences of "is_planned" in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md or in any file under docs/audits/gauntlet/. Note the cited baseline docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md DOES NOT EXIST in this checkout (latest section-04 refreshes are 2026-07-22 and 2026-07-26); neither mentions is_planned.

IMPACT CORRECTIONS (the reviewer's failure_scenario is partly wrong)
A. "understating Net Position" is inaccurate as written. get_inventory_position computes "(b.quantity_available - b.quantity_prebooked + COALESCE(oo.qty, 0)) AS net_position" (public_schema.sql:29429) — holds are NOT subtracted from net_position; holds_qty is a separate reported column. The real capacity harm is elsewhere and is genuine: create_inventory_hold derives "v_todays_free := quantity_available - quantity_prebooked - v_active_holds" from all is_active holds with no quote join (public_schema.sql:19985-19998, raises INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY), and the job shortfall check subtracts "SUM(h.quantity) FROM inventory_holds h WHERE ... is_active" the same way (10826-10829). A stale hold does consume real capacity there.
B. "the same units are offered for sale twice" on an is_planned=false→true flip is REFUTED for the main surface. get_inventory_position's planned_quotes CTE reads the booking straight from quote_items — "WHERE q.is_planned = true AND q.status IN ('draft','sent','revised')" (public_schema.sql:29341) — and reports "booked − linked_hold − order_drawn − job_drawn" as planned_qty (29332-29366). With no holds created, linked_hold = 0, so the FULL booking still surfaces as planned demand; that is the explicit "no double count" design. The gap is narrower: get_inventory_forecast is hold-only (29236-29252) and the hold-aware free checks in (A) under-reserve.
C. "indefinitely" is overstated. Planned holds carry expires_at = needed_by_date + 14 days (_sync_planned_holds, public_schema.sql:11094), and every consumer filters "expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at >= CURRENT_DATE", so the stale hold ages out; it also self-heals on the next save_quote, which re-runs _sync_planned_holds.

HARM THE REVIEWER MISSED (argues MED, not LOW)
A direct PATCH of is_planned=false bypasses save_quote's explicit BOOKING_HAS_JOB_RESERVATION guard (public_schema.sql:43992-44005: "block unplanning a booking a scheduled job is still drawing from (Codex final push-gate P1 #1) ... reopening the full booking balance while the job still consumes the stock (double-count)"). Once is_planned is false, _sync_quote_job_reservations sets "v_planned_open := FOUND AND v_quote.is_planned AND ..." (11126-11128) and then unconditionally deletes non-consumed job draws (11144-11148) — i.e. the exact double-count that guard was written to prevent, reachable by a write path the guard does not cover. No detective control catches it either: the integrity sweep's stale_quote_hold alert keys on terminal STATUS only ("q.status IN ('declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short')", public_schema.sql:40690-40694), and there is no is_planned predicate under scripts/db-invariant-sweeps/predicates/.

WHY NOT HIGHER THAN MED
Not reachable from the app: the only UI write of quotes.is_planned is the save_quote payload (src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:1439); the direct .update() calls on quotes are status (QuoteBuilder.tsx:1731, 2688) and deleted_at (Quotes.tsx:409). Exploiting requires a hand-crafted PostgREST call by an authenticated admin/owning rep, the drift is bounded by hold expiry, and it self-heals on the next save_quote.

### [MED] global_search is an ungated SECURITY DEFINER reader that enumerates order, invoice and delivery numbers with customer names to every authenticated role

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260404080000_fix_global_search_ilike_escape.sql:4; baseline public_schema.sql line 32158; src/components/ui/CommandPalette.tsx:249

**Detail.** `global_search(p_query text, p_limit integer)` is `SECURITY DEFINER` with `EXECUTE` to `authenticated` and no auth or role test in the body. It reads `customers`, `orders`, `invoices`, `deliveries` and `products` under definer rights, so it bypasses `customers_select` (sales_rep scoped to `assigned_sales_rep`), `orders_select` (admin/sales_rep only), `invoices_select` and `del_select`. It returns identifiers and names, not amounts, so the direct disclosure is modest — but it hands a driver or applicator a working enumeration oracle for the customer uuids and invoice/order ids needed to exploit the ungated SECDEF readers above.

**Evidence.** baseline public_schema.sql:32158 —
CREATE FUNCTION "public"."global_search"("p_query" "text", "p_limit" integer DEFAULT 5) RETURNS TABLE("entity_type" "text", "id" "uuid", "primary_text" "text", "secondary_text" "text")
    LANGUAGE "plpgsql" SECURITY DEFINER
    SET "search_path" TO 'public', 'pg_temp'
    AS $$
...
BEGIN
  IF length(trim(p_query)) < 2 THEN
    RETURN;
  END IF;

(first statement is the length check — no auth.uid(), is_admin(), is_sales_rep(), or profiles lookup)

    SELECT 'invoice'::text AS entity_type, i.id, i.invoice_number AS primary_text, COALESCE(c3.farm_name, '')::text
    FROM invoices i LEFT JOIN customers c3 ON c3.id = i.customer_id
    WHERE i.invoice_number ILIKE v_pattern

**Failure scenario.** An applicator calls /rest/v1/rpc/global_search {"p_query":"a","p_limit":1000} and receives customer uuids and farm names for accounts they are assigned to none of, plus invoice and order ids. Those uuids are the input the ungated get_customer_year_end_summary / check_customer_credit_limit calls need, converting a name-only leak into a full financial read.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Every load-bearing claim checks out against current source, and I found no enforcement mechanism the reviewer missed.

1) Function shape is as claimed. supabase/migrations/20260404080000_fix_global_search_ilike_escape.sql:12-22 defines it `LANGUAGE plpgsql / SECURITY DEFINER / SET search_path = public, pg_temp`, and the first statement in the body is `IF length(trim(p_query)) < 2 THEN RETURN; END IF;` — no `auth.uid()`, `is_admin()`, `is_sales_rep()`, `is_active_profile()`, or profiles lookup anywhere in the body. The post-baseline dump agrees: decompressed supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br line 32158 carries the identical definition, so the baseline (2026-07-27) is the authoritative current shape.

2) No later migration supersedes it. `grep -rl global_search supabase/` returns only 20260404040100_global_search_rpc.sql, 20260404080000_fix_global_search_ilike_escape.sql, 20260529214355_revoke_anon_execute_on_report_dashboard_secdef.sql, 20260702170000 (a comment only), and the two baselines. Nothing after the 2026-07-27 baseline redefines the function or narrows its ACL. The one migration that touched its grants moved in the opposite direction of a fix: 20260529214355:100-101 `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM anon, PUBLIC;` then `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO authenticated, service_role;` — that migration deliberately closed only the anon hole and preserved authenticated access ("so the logged-in app is unaffected", lines 15-18). The role-hardening wave of 2026-07-27/28 (broad_reads_require_active_profile, quote_and_rate_reads_office_only, secdef_pricing_reads_office_only, revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions) does not touch global_search. Baseline ACL confirms the live grant: supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:1216 `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.global_search(p_query text, p_limit integer) TO authenticated;` and public_schema.sql:71577.

3) The RLS bypass is real, not assumed — I checked the policies rather than assuming them. public_schema.sql:65607 `customers_select ... USING ((is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND assigned_sales_rep = auth.uid()) OR (is_driver() AND EXISTS (...))))`; :67473 `orders_select ... USING ((is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()))`; :66652 `invoices_select ... USING ((is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid()))`; :65818 `del_select ... USING ((is_admin() OR is_sales_rep() OR assigned_driver = auth.uid()))`. `grep -n "FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY" schema.sql` returns zero hits, so the definer (table owner) is exempt from all four — the bypass actually occurs, it is not merely theoretical.

4) The affected roles exist. .claude/schema-registry.json `profiles.role`: `["admin","sales_rep","driver","applicator","entity_recipient"]`. An applicator holds none of the branches in customers_select/orders_select, so global_search returns rows they can read by no other path. The exposure is live in the product, not just at the REST layer: src/components/layout/AppLayout.tsx:69 mounts `<CommandPalette open={paletteOpen} .../>` with no role gate.

5) The chained-escalation premise also checks out. Both named follow-on readers are still ungated SECDEF with authenticated EXECUTE: public_schema.sql:15495 `check_customer_credit_limit(p_customer_id uuid) ... SECURITY DEFINER` whose body opens with a bare `SELECT credit_limit_cents, farm_name ... FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id` (no auth test), granted at :70055; and :27329 `get_customer_year_end_summary(p_customer_id uuid, p_season integer) ... SECURITY DEFINER`, body opens `SELECT * INTO v_cust FROM customers WHERE id = p_customer_id`, granted at :71083. No migration after 2026-07-13 touches either. So the name-to-uuid oracle does feed a genuine financial read.

6) Not previously documented. `grep -i global_search` over docs/manual/*.md and docs/audits/gauntlet/*.md returns zero hits — it is absent from KNOWN_ISSUES.md and from the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline. This is beyond the stated baseline.

Two minor corrections to the failure_scenario that do not change the substance: (a) `{"p_query":"a"}` returns nothing — the finding's own quoted `length(trim(p_query)) < 2` guard rejects it; the attack needs a 2+ character seed such as "aa" or "jo". (b) A single `%` wildcard dump is no longer possible — escaping metacharacters is exactly what the cited migration fixed (line 26, `v_escaped := regexp_replace(trim(p_query), '([%_\\])', ...)`), so this is two-character substring enumeration rather than a one-shot dump. `p_limit` is uncapped in the function (each branch is `LIMIT p_limit`) while the UI passes only 5 (src/components/ui/CommandPalette.tsx:250-251), so a direct REST call with p_limit=1000 works as described, and a few hundred bigram queries enumerate the active customer book.

Severity raised LOW -> MED. The reviewer's "identifiers and names, not amounts" framing understates it: farm_name plus contact_name is customer PII, and the function bypasses four separate SELECT policies — including customers_select, which was deliberately written narrow — for a role class (applicator, entity_recipient) that has no customer read grant at all. Combined with the confirmed-still-ungated check_customer_credit_limit and get_customer_year_end_summary, this is the working first stage of a full financial read, and the palette is mounted for every role today. Still short of HIGH because the function itself returns no money values and the audience is authenticated staff rather than the public.

---

### [MED] Sales reps can insert deliveries directly, including in a terminal status, bypassing create_delivery_with_items' order-status, quantity and address-ownership validation

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:27-30 (del_insert); supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:174 (authenticated INSERT grant); baseline public_schema.sql line 19431 (create_delivery_with_items)

**Detail.** `del_insert` is `WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())` and `authenticated` holds INSERT on `public.deliveries`. The canonical creator `create_delivery_with_items` enforces that the parent order is `confirmed` or `partially_fulfilled` (`ORDER_NOT_SCHEDULABLE`), that the customer matches the order (`CUSTOMER_ORDER_MISMATCH`), that no order_item is duplicated, that scheduled quantities do not exceed the remaining order quantity, and that the delivery address belongs to that customer. None of those are triggers. The triggers on `deliveries` that do run on INSERT are `trg_enforce_delivery_accounting_period` (period-open only) and `trg_guard_completed_delivery_signature` (non-blank signed_by when status='completed'); `_enforce_delivery_status_transition` is BEFORE UPDATE OF status, so it never sees an INSERT. I found no BEFORE INSERT trigger restricting `deliveries.status` to 'scheduled'. THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE the RPC's preconditions on the direct-insert path.

**Evidence.** 20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:27-30 —
DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "del_insert" ON deliveries;
CREATE POLICY "del_insert" ON deliveries
  FOR INSERT
  ...

acl_lockdown.sql:174 — GRANT DELETE, INSERT, ... ON TABLE public.deliveries TO authenticated;

create_delivery_with_items (checks that exist only inside the RPC) —
  IF NOT (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'FORBIDDEN'; END IF;
  IF v_order.customer_id IS DISTINCT FROM p_customer_id THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CUSTOMER_ORDER_MISMATCH: ...';
  IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'partially_fulfilled') THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_NOT_SCHEDULABLE: order % is in status %, cannot create delivery ...';

deliveries DDL — "status" "text" DEFAULT 'scheduled'::"text" NOT NULL  (no CHECK pinning INSERT to 'scheduled', and no BEFORE INSERT status trigger in the DR baseline)

**Failure scenario.** A sales_rep POSTs /rest/v1/deliveries with {"delivery_number":"D-9999","order_id":"<a cancelled order>","customer_id":"<a different customer>","status":"completed","signed_by":"x","scheduled_date":"2026-08-09"}. The row is accepted: the order-status gate, the customer/order coherence gate and the quantity gate all live in the RPC and are skipped. A 'completed' delivery now exists against a cancelled order for the wrong customer, with no inventory movement, no invoice, and no delivery_items — and the AFTER UPDATE completion machinery will never run for it because it was never updated into that state.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED as a real DB-enforcement gap, with two citation corrections. Verified against the decompressed live baseline (supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br), not merely against the migration the reviewer cited.

WHAT HOLDS (all verified, DB-does-not-enforce, not merely not-found):
1. Current effective policy: public_schema.sql:65717 — CREATE POLICY "del_insert" ON "public"."deliveries" FOR INSERT TO "authenticated" WITH CHECK (("public"."is_admin"() OR "public"."is_sales_rep"())). No created_by binding, no order/customer/status predicate.
2. Grant: acl_lockdown.sql:174 — GRANT DELETE, INSERT, MAINTAIN, REFERENCES, SELECT, TRIGGER, TRUNCATE, UPDATE ON TABLE public.deliveries TO authenticated. Verbatim as cited. (anon gets only MAINTAIN, SELECT at :173.)
3. No CHECK pins INSERT status: public_schema.sql:51677 — CONSTRAINT "deliveries_status_check" CHECK (("status" = ANY (ARRAY['scheduled','in_progress','completed','cancelled','voided']))). Column default 'scheduled' (:51653) is a default, not a constraint.
4. Trigger inventory on deliveries is exactly five (public_schema.sql:60343, 60413, 60784, 60798, 60847). Only two fire on INSERT and I read both bodies: guard_completed_delivery_signature only raises SIGNATURE_REQUIRED when status='completed' AND signed_by is blank; enforce_delivery_accounting_period's TG_OP='INSERT' branch only calls check_period_open() for status completed/voided. Neither checks order status, customer coherence, or quantity.
5. Reviewer is right that the transition guard cannot fire: :60343 — CREATE TRIGGER "enforce_delivery_status_transition" BEFORE UPDATE OF "status" ON "public"."deliveries". There is NO BEFORE INSERT status trigger.
6. All five gates are genuinely RPC-only (create_delivery_with_items, public_schema.sql:19431-19600): FORBIDDEN role check, CUSTOMER_ORDER_MISMATCH, ORDER_NOT_SCHEDULABLE (status NOT IN confirmed/partially_fulfilled), ADDRESS_CUSTOMER_MISMATCH, ITEM_DUPLICATE_IN_REQUEST, ITEM_ORDER_MISMATCH, and ITEM_OVER_REMAINING_INCL_ACTIVE. The RPC hardcodes status 'scheduled' on its own INSERT.

REFUTATION ATTEMPTS THAT FAILED TO OVERTURN IT:
- Later-migration supersession: newest migration touching deliveries policies is 20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql, which LOOSENS (drops the created_by binding). Nothing after it re-tightens.
- Post-baseline scan: every migration with timestamp > 20260727174805 mentioning deliveries (20260727231652, 20260730114102, 20260730124308, 20260730140808, 20260730235031, 20260803010917) uses deliveries only in read-only SELECT count(*)/NOT EXISTS. No new guard, policy, or trigger.
- Duplicate check: the 2026-08-05 gauntlet section-04 doc contains exactly one finding, 'HIGH — Bulk Order Import bypassed the canonical order lifecycle' (line 31) — unrelated. KNOWN_ISSUES.md does not cover deliveries direct-insert; the nearest entry (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:216) proposes revoking direct browser-role INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on quote_sections, quote_items, customer_addresses — deliveries is NOT in that list, and it is a candidate remediation, not applied. So the project's own stated fix direction matches this finding but has never reached this table.
- Threat-model exemption: the 'honest-mistake threat model' accepted residuals (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:1031, 1036, 1039) scope to agent proof-file self-attestation and shell/npm guard regexes — not to table grants or RLS, which this project actively hardens (20260727145843, 20260727174657, 20260727174805, 20260729125227).

CORRECTIONS TO THE FINDING (do not change the verdict):
(a) The location citation is WRONG. 20260210_fix_rls_critical_issues.sql:28-33 actually reads: CREATE POLICY "del_insert" ON deliveries FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND created_by = (select auth.uid()))). The reviewer's '...' elision hides this. The quoted policy body is really from supabase/migrations/20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:94-98, which DROPs the 20260210 version and removes the created_by binding. Correct location: 20260511060000_perf_consolidate_permissive_policies.sql:94-98 (+ baseline public_schema.sql:65717). The reviewer reached the right current-state conclusion via the wrong file.
(b) The failure-scenario payload is incomplete: deliveries.created_by is NOT NULL with no default (public_schema.sql:51659), so the literal POST shown fails. The attacker must add their own uid — trivial, non-material.

SEVERITY: keeping MED, neither raised nor lowered. Mitigations against raising: no frontend path direct-inserts into deliveries (grep of src/ for from('deliveries') + .insert returns zero; create_delivery_with_items and create_quick_delivery are the only creation paths), anon cannot INSERT, exploitation needs an authenticated admin/sales_rep deliberately hand-crafting a PostgREST call, and enforce_delivery_accounting_period DOES block a directly-inserted 'completed' row in a CLOSED period. Reasons against lowering to LOW: an open-period insert is fully unguarded, the resulting row corrupts inventory/AR reporting with no inventory movement and no invoice, the AFTER UPDATE completion machinery (trg_delivery_status_change) never runs for it, and a plausible non-terminal variant (direct insert at status='scheduled' plus delivery_items, which del_items_insert also permits to sales_rep) silently defeats the ITEM_OVER_REMAINING_INCL_ACTIVE quantity gate on a money/inventory-adjacent lifecycle table.

### [MED] Quotes list bulk-delete leaks inventory holds: soft-delete changes no status, so the hold-release trigger never fires and the reserved units stay counted forever

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:376-421 (write at 407-412); guard at 387-406; supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:124-150; supabase/migrations/20260613191120_planned_holds_drawn_sync.sql:142-155; supabase/migrations/20260701203000_inventory_position_planned_no_double_count.sql:38-45

**Detail.** `handleDelete` soft-deletes quotes with a raw `.update({ deleted_at })`. Its only guard is 'skip quotes with order/job draw-downs' (lines 387-406). A `sent`/`revised` planned booking with ZERO draws is deleted freely — and deletion changes no status, so nothing releases its `inventory_holds` rows.

This is a DB-enforcement gap I verified in the migrations, not a 'couldn't find it' claim:
- The release trigger is `AFTER UPDATE OF status ON quotes` and its body only fires `IF NEW.status IN ('accepted','declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short') AND OLD.status NOT IN (...)`. A `deleted_at`-only UPDATE does not fire the `UPDATE OF status` trigger at all.
- `_sync_planned_holds` (the only other release path) starts with `SELECT * INTO v_quote FROM quotes WHERE id = p_quote_id AND deleted_at IS NULL FOR UPDATE; IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN 0;` — a deleted quote is skipped, so it can never release the holds either.
- No trigger on `quotes` keys on `deleted_at` (only `enforce_quote_status_transition`, `trg_release_holds_on_quote_status`, `enforce_quote_accepted_fully_drawn`, `trg_enforce_quote_terminal_not_drawn`, `trg_validate_quote_commission_split`, `quotes_bump_row_version`).
- Every free-stock reader sums `inventory_holds` with no join back to `quotes` and no deleted-quote filter, so the orphaned holds keep subtracting from Net Free forever.

Note `DELETABLE = ['draft','sent','revised']` (Quotes.tsx:32) is exactly the open-planned set that holds inventory, so this is the normal case, not an edge case.

**Evidence.** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:407-412
      const result = await supabase
        .from('quotes')
        .update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() })
        .in('id', ids)
        .select();
      checkMutationResult(result, 'Delete quotes');

supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:133-138
  IF NEW.status IN ('accepted', 'declined', 'expired', 'cancelled', 'closed_by_application', 'closed_short')
     AND OLD.status NOT IN ('accepted', 'declined', 'expired', 'cancelled', 'closed_by_application', 'closed_short') THEN
    UPDATE public.inventory_holds
    SET is_active = false, updated_at = now()
    WHERE source_id = NEW.id AND is_active = true;

supabase/migrations/20260312200000_business_logic_audit_fixes.sql:63-67
DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_release_holds_on_quote_status ON quotes;
CREATE TRIGGER trg_release_holds_on_quote_status
  AFTER UPDATE OF status ON quotes

supabase/migrations/20260613191120_planned_holds_drawn_sync.sql:142-146
  SELECT * INTO v_quote FROM quotes
  WHERE id = p_quote_id AND deleted_at IS NULL
  FOR UPDATE;
  IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN 0; END IF;

supabase/migrations/20260701203000_inventory_position_planned_no_double_count.sql:38-45
    holds AS (
      SELECT ih.product_id, SUM(ih.quantity) AS qty
      FROM inventory_holds ih
      WHERE ih.is_active = true
        AND (ih.expires_at IS NULL OR ih.expires_at >= CURRENT_DATE)
      GROUP BY ih.product_id
    ),

**Failure scenario.** Sales creates planned quote Q-1042 (is_planned, status 'sent') for 500 gal of a product; `create_planned_holds`/`save_quote` writes a 500-unit `crop_program` hold. The customer walks away and an admin selects Q-1042 in the Quotes list and clicks Delete Quotes. The quote disappears from every screen, but the 500-unit `inventory_holds` row is still `is_active = true` with `source_id` = the deleted quote id. `get_inventory_position()` keeps subtracting 500 units from Net Free, `_reserve_job_inventory` keeps reporting a phantom shortage, and nothing in the UI can ever release it because the quote is hidden and `_sync_planned_holds` refuses deleted rows. The correct flow (Cancel/Decline, which DOES fire the release trigger) is one click away in QuoteBuilder, so the two paths silently disagree.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED as a real DB-enforcement gap, with two of the reviewer's supporting claims corrected (they overstate permanence and irrecoverability). Severity corrected HIGH -> MED.

WHAT I VERIFIED HOLDS UP (checked the migrations, not "couldn't find it"):

1. Write path is as quoted. src/pages/Quotes.tsx:407-412 `supabase.from('quotes').update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() }).in('id', ids)` — a raw table UPDATE that names only `deleted_at`. The only guard (Quotes.tsx:385-402) queries `quote_product_draws` / `job_product_draws` for `quantity_drawn > 0`; there is no `is_planned` check, no hold check. `DELETABLE = ['draft','sent','revised']` (Quotes.tsx:32) is enforced only as row selectability (Quotes.tsx:323, 327) and is exactly the open-planned set that holds stock.

2. The release trigger genuinely cannot fire. Latest CREATE TRIGGER is supabase/migrations/20260312200000_business_logic_audit_fixes.sql:63-67 — `AFTER UPDATE OF status ON quotes`; I grepped every `CREATE TRIGGER ... ON quotes` in all 859 migrations and no later file recreates it (only 20260302200000 and 20260312200000 create it). A `deleted_at`-only UPDATE does not list `status`, so the trigger does not fire at all — the body's status sets (20260706030000:133-138, current definition) are never reached.

3. No `deleted_at`-keyed trigger on quotes exists. `grep -rn "NEW.deleted_at"` returns 16 hits — jobs, blend tickets, returns, invoices, customer documents, commission splits — none on `quotes`. The contrast is decisive: supabase/migrations/20260702174000_layer2_reserve_job_inventory.sql:281 fires the job-hold engine on `NEW.deleted_at IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.deleted_at`, added precisely because docs/archive/2026-summer-closeout/roadmap/2026-07-01-inventory-aware-scheduling-layer-2-handoff.md:83 says "job soft-delete bypasses status transitions and would orphan holds forever". The same fix was never applied to quotes.

4. The other release paths are closed. `_sync_planned_holds` (latest definition supabase/migrations/20260702171000_layer2_sync_planned_holds_job_aware.sql:41-44) does `WHERE id = p_quote_id AND deleted_at IS NULL FOR UPDATE; IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN 0;`. The cron cleanup `release_expired_quote_holds` (20260302200000_business_logic_enhancements.sql:1515-1535) releases only `WHERE q.status IN ('declined','expired')` — status-based, so it misses a deleted `sent` quote. `auto_expire_quotes` (20260614143649:36) filters `AND q.deleted_at IS NULL`, so the deleted quote never even reaches a terminal status.

5. Readers do not filter deleted quotes. Latest `get_inventory_position` is supabase/migrations/20260716120120_gauntlet_inventory_accuracy.sql:53-62 (supersedes the 20260701203000 version the reviewer cited; the substance is unchanged): `FROM inventory_holds ih WHERE ih.is_active = true AND (ih.expires_at IS NULL OR ih.expires_at >= CURRENT_DATE)` — no join to quotes. Its `planned_quotes` CTE (same file, :80-85) is worse: `JOIN quotes q ON q.id = qi.quote_id WHERE q.is_planned = true AND q.status IN ('draft','sent','revised')` with NO `q.deleted_at IS NULL`, so a soft-deleted planned quote keeps contributing `planned_qty` too. Job shortfall math has the same shape: supabase/migrations/20260710120000_d2_reserve_side_unit_normalization.sql:347-356 `active_holds` sums holds with no quote filter, and :327-331 credits coverage from `ih.source_id = wj.quote_id` regardless of the quote's deleted state.

6. Reachable in production. RLS `quotes_update` (20260206174345:369-377) allows any admin or the owning sales rep to UPDATE quotes directly, and the 20260730235031 hardening revoked direct INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `quote_sections`/`quote_items` only — `quotes` was deliberately left writable. No detection either: the sentinel's `stale_quote_hold` check (20260705150000_data_integrity_sentinel.sql:212-222) keys on `q.status IN ('declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application')`, so a deleted-but-'sent' quote's hold is invisible to it.

7. Not in the named baselines. Neither `hold`, `deleted_at`, nor `soft` appears anywhere in docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md, and docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no entry for orphaned holds or Quotes bulk delete. The closest prior art is docs/audits/2026-06-10-error-prevention-execution-log.md:16 — "NEW (found during work): bulk soft-delete hides open bookings with live holds | Quotes.tsx bulk delete skips quotes with draws > 0" — i.e. the draws>0 guard the reviewer quotes IS the historical remedy, and the zero-draw planned-booking residual was never closed. That strengthens the finding rather than refuting it, but the reviewer should cite it.

WHAT IS OVERSTATED (why I cut HIGH to MED):

A. "stay counted forever" is wrong in the common case. `_sync_planned_holds` stamps `expires_at = v_item.needed_by_date + INTERVAL '14 days'` (20260702171000:99), and every reader filters `expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at >= CURRENT_DATE`. The orphan self-heals 14 days past the section's needed-by date. It is indefinite only when `quote_sections.needed_by_date` is NULL — which is possible (nullable: added as `ADD COLUMN ... needed_by_date date` in 20260316500000_planned_programs.sql:6, and absent from the registry's `not_null_columns.quote_sections`, which lists only quote_id/id/section_name/sort_order) — so the unbounded case is a sub-case, not the norm.

B. "nothing in the UI can ever release it" is factually false. src/pages/InventoryPage.tsx:226-237 lists EVERY active, non-expired hold (`.eq('is_active', true).or('expires_at.is.null,expires_at.gte....')`) with no join to quotes — so the orphan is fully visible — and InventoryPage.tsx:447-457 releases it via `release_inventory_hold`, which rejects only `hold_type = 'job'` (supabase/migrations/20260702182000_layer2_release_hold_reject_job.sql:52-55). A crop_program orphan is a one-click admin fix from the Inventory page.

NET: real, unenforced at the DB, reachable by a normal admin action, with no automated detection — but time-bounded in the usual case and admin-recoverable, and it corrupts a planning number rather than money or AR. MED, not HIGH. The correct fix mirrors the jobs pattern: an `AFTER UPDATE OF deleted_at ON quotes` trigger releasing `inventory_holds WHERE source_id = NEW.id`, plus adding `q.deleted_at IS NULL` to the `planned_quotes` CTE in get_inventory_position and extending the sentinel's stale_quote_hold predicate to deleted parents.

### [MED] QuoteBuilder reuses one page-scoped idempotency key across different quote sections (Schedule Job) and across draw attempts (Partial Order), both on key-only RPCs

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:253-254 (hook declarations), 2276+2285 (scheduleJobIdem), 2315-2324 (openDrawDownModal — no reset), 2392+2395 (drawDownIdem); supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql (create_job_from_quote_section idempotency lookup); supabase/migrations/20260702172000_layer2_draw_down_quote_job_aware.sql (draw_down_quote idempotency)

**Detail.** Two handlers on the same page mint one key per page mount and reset it only on success:

(a) `scheduleJobIdem` is used by `handleScheduleJob(sectionKey)`, which is rendered per quote section. `create_job_from_quote_section` looks the key up with a bare `SELECT result ... WHERE idempotency_key = ... AND operation = 'create_job_from_quote_section'` — no payload binding — so a stale key from section A replays A's `job_id` when the user clicks Schedule Job on section B.

(b) `drawDownIdem` is used by `handleDrawDown`. `openDrawDownModal` (line 2315) does not reset the key, so a second draw attempt with different quantities reuses the first attempt's key. `draw_down_quote` calls the key-only `check_idempotency`, so it replays the first draw's result rather than executing the new quantities.

Both are payload-blind by construction; I verified the RPC bodies rather than assuming. `plannedHoldsIdem` (1545/1554) and `rolloverIdem` (1610/1625) share the same reset-only-on-success shape but are single-quote-scoped, so the blast radius is smaller.

**Evidence.** src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:253-254
  const scheduleJobIdem = useIdempotencyKey('create_job_from_quote_section', profile?.id || '');
  const drawDownIdem = useIdempotencyKey('draw_down_quote', profile?.id || '');

src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:2276-2290
      const idemKey = scheduleJobIdem.getKey();
      const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc('create_job_from_quote_section', {
        p_quote_id: quoteId,
        p_section_id: sec.id, ...
      const result = assertRpcResult<{ job_id: string }>(data, 'create_job_from_quote_section');
      scheduleJobIdem.resetKey();
      setSectionJobs((prev) => ({ ...prev, [sec.id as string]: { id: result.job_id, ... } }));

create_job_from_quote_section (20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql)
SELECT result INTO v_existing FROM idempotency_keys WHERE idempotency_key = p_idempotency_key AND operation = 'create_job_from_quote_section';

src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:2388-2395
      const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc('draw_down_quote', {
        p_quote_id: id,
        p_draws: draws.map((d) => ({ product_id: d.product_id, quantity: d.quantity })),
        p_performed_by: profile!.id,
        p_idempotency_key: drawDownIdem.getKey(),
      });
      if (error) throw error;
      drawDownIdem.resetKey();

draw_down_quote (20260702172000_layer2_draw_down_quote_job_aware.sql)
IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL THEN
  v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'draw_down_quote');

**Failure scenario.** (b) is the money case. A rep opens Partial Order on booking Q-1200 and draws 300 gal. The RPC commits (order ORD-877 created, 300 gal drawn) but the response is lost; the toast shows a failure and the key is retained. The rep reopens the modal, sees the remaining balance is now 300 lower, and draws the remaining 200 gal. `draw_down_quote` replays the cached result for the 300-gal draw, the UI toasts 'Order ORD-877 created — booking stays open', sends a second Order Confirmed email for ORD-877 to the customer, and navigates to ORD-877. The 200-gal draw never happened; the booking still shows 200 gal open and the rep believes it was drawn.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Every load-bearing claim verified against current source and the full migration chain. (1) src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:253-254 declares both hooks with scope `profile?.id` only — no quote/section/payload in scope — unlike the sibling create_quote_version/restore_quote_version hooks at 242-252 which use `${profile?.id}:${id}`. (2) src/hooks/useIdempotencyKey.ts:29-36 regenerates only on scope change, so the key persists until resetKey(); the only reset sites in QuoteBuilder.tsx are 2285 and 2395, both post-success, and openDrawDownModal (2314-2370) contains no reset — the reviewer's read is exact. (3) create_job_from_quote_section: I enumerated all 10 CREATE sites; the latest is indeed supabase/migrations/20260707060000_u8_application_channel_commissions.sql:201 (no later migration redefines it), and its lookup at 218-221 is `SELECT result INTO v_existing FROM idempotency_keys WHERE idempotency_key = p_idempotency_key AND operation = 'create_job_from_quote_section'; IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN v_existing; END IF;` — returns before the section-ownership check at line 245, with no quote_id/section_id binding. (4) draw_down_quote: latest of 4 definitions is 20260702172000_layer2_draw_down_quote_job_aware.sql:27, calling check_idempotency at line 94. The current check_idempotency (latest override 20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-52) is deliberately key-only — it raises IDEMPOTENCY_CROSS_OP_KEY_REUSE only when the stored *operation* differs and otherwise does `RETURN v_existing.result`, so same-operation different-payload replay is unguarded. (5) The enforcement mechanism that could have refuted this exists but does NOT cover these two RPCs: 20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql adds request_fingerprint/request_actor_id payload binding, but wraps only save_invoice (line 33) and create_quick_delivery (line 190). No migration extends binding to draw_down_quote or create_job_from_quote_section. The client-side counterpart legacyIntentChanged (src/lib/idempotency.ts:39) is wired only into src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:769 and src/components/deliveries/QuickDeliveryModal.tsx:371 — the codebase explicitly recognizes this bug class and simply has not applied either mitigation to the QuoteBuilder pair. (6) Not in the baseline: docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md has zero mentions of draw-down or job scheduling, and the only draw_down_quote hit in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md (line 688) concerns role gating, not idempotency binding. Failure scenario reachability: on a lost response supabase-js returns an error, `if (error) throw error` fires before resetKey(), so the key is retained (QuoteBuilder.tsx:2390-2395), and openDrawDownModal refetches quote_product_draws + job_product_draws so the rep genuinely sees the reduced remaining and enters a different quantity — the replay then returns the prior order, toasts success, fires sendOrderConfirmedEmail(result.order_id!) at line 2406 for a duplicate customer email, and navigates away. Severity MED is correct and I am not adjusting it: the trigger requires commit-plus-lost-response (narrow), and the damage is a silent no-op draw, an undrawn booking balance the rep believes is drawn, and a duplicate customer-facing confirmation email — not corruption of existing money rows. Part (a) is the weaker half (requires the user to move to a different section after a failed schedule) but is real by the same mechanism.

### [MED] Deliveries page reuses page-scoped idempotency keys across different deliveries (driver Take) and across changed batch-cancel selections

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:113 + 593-624 (batchCancelIdem — no reset on selection change), 115 + 833-857 (reassignIdem — shared across all driver cards); compare the correct pattern at src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:155-163; supabase/migrations/20260511010000_actor_spoof_cleanup.sql (batch_cancel_deliveries / reassign_delivery bodies)

**Detail.** The same file already contains the fix for this exact class: `batchRescheduleIdem` is reset by a `useEffect` keyed on `${selection}|${rescheduleDate}` with a comment naming the 'response-lost replay bug pattern'. Two siblings were not given that treatment.

(a) `batchCancelIdem` is minted once and reset only on success (line 613). The selection can change freely between a failed attempt and the next one.
(b) `reassignIdem` backs the driver dashboard's `handleTakeDelivery(deliveryId)`, which is rendered per unassigned delivery card; it is reset only on success (line 850).

Both RPCs use the key-only `check_idempotency`, verified in the shipped bodies — they cannot distinguish a different `p_delivery_ids` array or a different `p_delivery_id`.

**Evidence.** src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:155-163 (the fix that was applied to the sibling)
  // Codex P2 fix (PR #59, 2026-05-16): reset batchRescheduleIdem when the
  // selection or target date changes. Same response-lost replay bug pattern ...
  const rescheduleIntentKey = `${Array.from(selected).sort().join(',')}|${rescheduleDate}`;
  useEffect(() => {
    batchRescheduleIdem.resetKey();
  }, [rescheduleIntentKey]);

src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:605-613

          p_delivery_ids: ids,

          p_performed_by: profile.id,
          p_idempotency_key: cancelKey,
        });
        if (error) throw error;
        batchCancelIdem.resetKey();

src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:842-850
          const reassignKey = reassignIdem.getKey();
          const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc('reassign_delivery', {
            p_delivery_id: deliveryId,
            p_new_driver: profile.id, ...
          assertRpcResult(data, 'reassign_delivery');
          reassignIdem.resetKey();

supabase/migrations/20260511010000_actor_spoof_cleanup.sql (both RPCs)
  v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'batch_cancel_deliveries');
  v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'reassign_delivery');

**Failure scenario.** Driver flow (the likeliest, since it runs on a phone with poor coverage): a driver taps 'Take' on unassigned delivery DEL-310. `reassign_delivery` commits but the response is lost in a dead zone; the card shows an error and the key stays. The driver taps 'Take' on DEL-455 instead. `reassign_delivery` replays DEL-310's cached result, the toast says 'Delivery assigned to you', and `fetchDeliveries()` shows DEL-310 on the driver's list. DEL-455 remains unassigned but the driver believes he owns it — the load sits at the warehouse while the driver's route says it is covered.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Every cited fact checks out against current source and the full migration chain, and I found no enforcement mechanism the reviewer missed.\n\n(1) Source accurate: src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:113 batchCancelIdem and :115 reassignIdem are page-scoped hook keys; a full grep of resetKey/getKey in the file shows resets ONLY at :613 (post-success batch cancel) and :850 (post-success reassign). The intent-keyed reset useEffect exists only for batchRescheduleIdem (:156-163). src/hooks/useIdempotencyKey.ts:25 scopes the ref to JSON.stringify([operation, userId]) with no payload component, so the key survives an ambiguous failure unchanged. src/lib/criticalAction.ts:44-52 catches, toasts, and returns without resetting anything. src/pages/Deliveries.tsx:997-1003 renders 'Take This Delivery' -> handleTakeDelivery(d.id) per unassigned card, all sharing the one reassignIdem.\n\n(2) No DB-side enforcement exists, and I checked for a superseding migration specifically. grep -rl over supabase/migrations shows the LAST file defining either RPC is 20260511010000_actor_spoof_cleanup.sql (reassign_delivery body :46-137, using check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key,'reassign_delivery') at :83; batch_cancel_deliveries likewise). The latest check_idempotency definition is 20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-45 -- key+operation only, comment states 'The lock is intentionally key-only'. The payload-fingerprint intent binding (request_fingerprint / IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH) added by 20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql wraps ONLY save_invoice (:25-33) and create_quick_delivery (:182-190); no later migration extends it to these two, and the frontend mismatch helpers in src/lib/idempotency.ts are imported only by InvoiceDetail.tsx:16 and QuickDeliveryModal.tsx:16. This is 'the DB does not enforce it' (migrations read), not 'I could not find where it is enforced'.\n\n(3) Failure scenario is reachable, and the RPC's own ordering makes it worse than assumed: in reassign_delivery the driver authorization check 'SELECT 1 FROM deliveries WHERE id = p_delivery_id AND assigned_driver IS NULL' (:76) runs BEFORE the idempotency lookup (:83), so a second, genuinely-unassigned delivery passes authorization and then returns the cached receipt jsonb_build_object('status','reassigned','delivery_id', <FIRST delivery>) (:133) without touching the new one. assertRpcResult does not compare the returned delivery_id to the requested one. A rolled-back error writes no receipt, so the trigger window is specifically commit-then-lost-response -- exactly the class this file already fixed for batchRescheduleIdem.\n\n(4) Not previously documented: absent from docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md, absent from the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline, and absent from the 2026-08-06 section-06 idempotency/double-submit refresh (which scored Bulk Field Import, Blend Duplicate, reconcile_negative_inventory, notification triggers -- not these). The repo's own lint rule names this exact class in prose at eslint-local-rules/rules/idempotency-key-from-hook.cjs:181-187 ('one cached key per mount collides across rows') while its LEGACY_VIOLATION_FILES list does not include Deliveries.tsx, and section-06's own LOW finding admits the rule 'cannot distinguish a key generated per attempt from a key retained per business intent'.\n\nOne narrative correction (does not refute): runCriticalAction's onSuccess calls fetchDeliveries(), so DEL-455 still renders in the unassigned list after the replay -- the driver's route is not durably falsified. The durable artifacts are the false success toast and the false activity_feed row written client-side by logActivity at Deliveries.tsx:851 ('Took delivery DEL-455'), plus the un-cancelled deliveries in the batch-cancel variant.\n\nSeverity MED is correct and consistent with the repo's own calibration: section-06 scored 'reconcile_negative_inventory appends a second false audit event on retry' as MED, and this is a strictly stronger consequence (wrong-entity replay reporting a business action that never happened) on a mobile driver path where lost responses are the expected condition. Not HIGH: no money, no inventory, no data corruption, self-evident on the immediate refresh, and it requires a commit-then-lost response.

### [MED] Orders list fetches EVERY invoice in the database for the Invoiced % column — unscoped by order_id and unfiltered by deleted_at

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/Orders.tsx:106-109 (query), 146-152 (aggregation), 180-181 (use); compare the three sibling queries at 102-105, 110-115, 116-120 which all scope by `orderIds`

**Detail.** Three of the four parallel enrichment queries scope to `orderIds`. The invoices query does not: it has no `.in('order_id', orderIds)`, no `.is('deleted_at', null)`, and no `.limit()`. Two consequences:

1. Correctness: soft-deleted invoices retain their `draft`/`unposted`/`voided` status (`delete_invoices` only stamps `deleted_at`, verified in the migration), so a deleted draft invoice's `total_amount_cents` is still summed into `invoicedByOrder` and inflates the order's Invoiced % badge. `Invoices.tsx:135` correctly filters `.is('deleted_at', null)`, so the two screens disagree about the same invoice.
2. Scale/silent truncation: the query returns every non-voided/cancelled invoice in the system on every Orders page load, while the orders themselves are capped at 500 with an explicit over-limit warning (lines 70, 87-89). If PostgREST ever applies a max-rows cap, the tail is dropped with no warning and the Invoiced % silently reads low for the oldest orders.

**Evidence.** src/pages/Orders.tsx:106-109
      supabase
        .from('invoices')
        .select('order_id, total_amount_cents')
        .not('status', 'in', '("voided","cancelled")'),

src/pages/Orders.tsx:102-105 (the sibling that IS scoped)
      supabase
        .from('order_items')
        .select('order_id, total_units_needed, quantity_delivered, price_per_unit, product_name')
        .in('order_id', orderIds.length > 0 ? orderIds : ['__none__']),

src/pages/Orders.tsx:148-152
    (invoiceData || []).forEach((inv: { order_id: string | null; total_amount_cents: number }) => {
      if (inv.order_id) {
        invoicedByOrder[inv.order_id] = (invoicedByOrder[inv.order_id] || 0) + (inv.total_amount_cents || 0);
      }
    });

supabase/migrations/20260617031416_delete_invoices_softdelete_rpc.sql:68-71
    IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft', 'unposted', 'voided') THEN CONTINUE; END IF;
    UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;

**Failure scenario.** An admin creates a $40,000 draft invoice against order ORD-500, notices it was cut against the wrong order, and deletes it with the Invoices page batch delete (`delete_invoices` → `deleted_at` set, status stays 'draft'). Back on the Orders list, ORD-500 still shows Invoiced 100% because the deleted invoice's cents are still summed. Office staff filtering the list for un-invoiced orders skip ORD-500 and the customer is never billed.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim verified against current source on branch claude/ordering-cycle-review-t41vat (HEAD 7da1896a), and it is absent from both baselines.

1) The query is exactly as quoted. src/pages/Orders.tsx:106-109 reads `supabase.from('invoices').select('order_id, total_amount_cents').not('status','in','(\"voided\",\"cancelled\")')` with no `.in('order_id', orderIds)`, no `.is('deleted_at', null)`, no `.limit()`. The three siblings in the same Promise.all DO scope: order_items at :102-105 (`.in('order_id', orderIds.length > 0 ? orderIds : ['__none__'])`), customers at :110-115 (`.in('id', parentIds)`), deliveries at :116-120 (`.in('order_id', …)`). Aggregation at :146-152 and use at :180-181 (`const invCents = invoicedByOrder[o.id] || 0; const invPct = orderCents > 0 ? Math.round((invCents / orderCents) * 100) : 0;` → `invoiced_pct: Math.min(invPct, 100)`) are as quoted.

2) No missed enforcement mechanism — this is "the DB does not enforce it", checked in the migrations, not "I couldn't find it":
- supabase/migrations/20260617031416_delete_invoices_softdelete_rpc.sql:68-71 confirmed verbatim: `IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft','unposted','voided') THEN CONTINUE; END IF;` then `UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;` — status is never changed, so a soft-deleted draft still passes the `not status in (voided,cancelled)` filter.
- RLS cannot save it. The only SELECT policy on invoices is supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262 — `USING (is_admin() OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))` — no deleted_at predicate, and no later migration recreates invoices_select (grep across supabase/migrations returns only that file plus a comment reference in 20260729015706_application_service_cost_admin_only.sql:51). There is no view, trigger, or later superseding migration that hides soft-deleted invoices from a direct PostgREST select.
- The DB-side aggregates everywhere else DO filter (`AND deleted_at IS NULL` in 20260217200000_accounting_periods.sql:116-172, 20260333900000_mega_audit_phase1_fixes.sql:49-104, etc.), which confirms the intended semantics that Orders.tsx violates.
- Invoices.tsx:134-135 confirmed: `.select('*, customer:customers!invoices_customer_id_fkey(farm_name)').is('deleted_at', null)`. The two screens genuinely disagree.

3) Not already documented. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md contains no entry for Orders.tsx (grep: zero hits) and no entry for the Invoiced % badge. The 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline (all 101 lines read) is entirely about bulk_import_order lifecycle bypass; Orders.tsx and soft-deleted-invoice aggregation appear nowhere in it.

4) The bug class is already an accepted, live-remediated defect elsewhere — which makes this a surviving instance, not a novel theory. KNOWN_ISSUES.md:1066 records "unbilled-delivery guard now ignores soft-deleted invoices (20260712170000 + dashboard companion 20260712180000)", and supabase/migrations/20260712180000...sql:1-13 states the reasoning almost word-for-word as the reviewer's: "delete_invoices soft-deletes a draft/unposted invoice (sets deleted_at) while LEAVING status='draft', so a delivery whose only invoice was soft-deleted was wrongly judged 'covered' and dropped off the dashboard 'unbilled deliveries' action item — the office never learned it still needed billing." That fix covered get_dashboard_action_items and the OfficeCockpit tile; the Orders list badge was not fixed.

Failure scenario can occur as written: admin-only delete_invoices (PERFORM require_admin()) matches the admin persona in the scenario; the deleted draft's total_amount_cents keeps flowing into invoicedByOrder, and Math.min(...,100) caps the badge at Invoiced 100%.

Severity stays MED, not raised: the defect is display-only — it misreports a badge, writes no money and corrupts no row — and the DB-side billing guards (20260712170000/180000) already ignore soft-deleted invoices, so the actual create-invoice path is not blocked by it. It is not lowered because the identical class was judged worth a live migration two months ago.

Two calibrations on the reviewer's secondary points (neither weakens the primary finding):
- Part 2's truncation risk is stated conditionally ("If PostgREST ever applies a max-rows cap") and I did not verify the live Supabase Max-rows setting (read-only-tool constraint), so treat it as unverified/plausible rather than proven.
- The reviewer understated the query's breakage: because invoices_select restricts non-admins to `created_by`/`salesman_id`, a sales_rep's Orders list also under-counts invoices raised by anyone else, so Invoiced % reads low for them. Not part of the claim, but same line.
- Adjacent, uncited: src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:240-245 has the same missing `.is('deleted_at', null)` on its per-order invoice load (it does have `.eq('order_id', id!)`), while OrderDetail.tsx:946 correctly uses `.is('deleted_at', null)`.
