### [MED] Order detail loads invoices and deliveries without a deleted_at filter, so a soft-deleted draft invoice permanently hides Create Invoice and is fed to Post All Drafts

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:236-241 (invoices fetch), 211-216 (deliveries fetch), 903-916 (hasActiveInvoice / hasPendingDelivery / hasActiveDelivery), 1168 (Create Invoice gate), 931-935 (Post All Drafts target selection), 946-956 (pre-check that DOES filter deleted_at)

**Detail.** The order's invoice fetch filters status but not `deleted_at`; `delete_invoices` soft-deletes while leaving the status at `draft`/`unposted`/`voided`, so a deleted draft is still returned. That row then:
- makes `hasActiveInvoice` true forever, which removes the Create Invoice button at line 1168 — the order can never be re-invoiced from the UI, which is precisely what deleting the bad draft was meant to enable;
- renders in the order's invoice table as if live;
- is selected as a Post All Drafts target at line 931. The staleness pre-check at 946-956 DOES filter `.is('deleted_at', null)`, so it returns zero rows, `currentRows.length > 0` is false, the 'already posted' skip never fires, and `post_invoice` is called on a deleted invoice.

The deliveries fetch has the same missing filter. `deliveries.deleted_at` exists in the schema registry but I found no current writer for it, so that half is dormant/defensive rather than live today — stating the distinction explicitly.

**Evidence.** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:236-241
      const { data: invoiceData } = await supabase
        .from('invoices')
        .select('*')
        .eq('order_id', id!)
        .not('status', 'in', '("voided","cancelled")')
        .order('invoice_number');

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:903-905
  const hasActiveInvoice = invoices.some(
    (inv) => !['voided', 'cancelled'].includes(inv.status)
  );

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1168
              {order.status !== 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'fulfilled' && !hasActiveInvoice && !hasActiveDelivery && (<>

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:931
    const drafts = invoices.filter((i) => i.status === 'draft' || i.status === 'unposted');

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:946 (the pre-check that DOES filter, causing the skip to misfire)
        const statusQuery = supabase.from('invoices').select('status').is('deleted_at', null);

**Failure scenario.** An admin generates a draft invoice from ORD-620, spots wrong quantities, and deletes it from the Invoices page. Returning to ORD-620, the deleted draft is still listed and Create Invoice is gone — there is no in-app way to re-invoice the order. Clicking Post All Drafts targets the deleted invoice, the staleness pre-check finds zero non-deleted rows so it is not skipped, `post_invoice` is called and raises 'Invoice not found', and the user sees an unexplained per-target failure on an invoice they already deleted.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim checks out against current source and migrations; one factual correction to the error text.

1) Fetch is genuinely unfiltered. src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:237-242 selects from `invoices` with only `.eq('order_id', id!)` and `.not('status','in','("voided","cancelled")')` — no `.is('deleted_at', null)`. Deliveries at OrderDetail.tsx:212-216 likewise has no `deleted_at` filter.

2) No DB-side mechanism hides the deleted row (checked the migrations, not merely "didn't find"). The only `invoices` SELECT policy is `invoices_select` in supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262 — `USING (is_admin() OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))`, no `deleted_at` predicate. `grep -rn "invoices_select" supabase/migrations/*.sql` returns only that definition plus a comment reference in 20260729015706, so no later migration supersedes it, and no view is interposed (the page reads the base table).

3) Soft-delete leaves status intact. supabase/migrations/20260617031416_delete_invoices_softdelete_rpc.sql:64-68: `IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft','unposted','voided') THEN CONTINUE; END IF;` then `UPDATE invoices SET deleted_at = now() WHERE id = v_inv.id;` — status is never changed, so a deleted draft survives the page's `("voided","cancelled")` status filter.

4) The permanent gate is real. `hasActiveInvoice` (OrderDetail.tsx:903-905) only excludes voided/cancelled, and it gates both Create Invoice buttons (OrderDetail.tsx:1169 and the split path at 1195). `grep -rn "create_invoice_from_order|create_split_invoices_from_order" src/` shows OrderDetail.tsx:862/878 are the only callers, and there is no restore/undelete RPC (`grep -rn "restore_invoice|undelete" src/ supabase/migrations/*.sql` → no matches; `restore_cancelled_order`/`restore_cancelled_delivery` exist, no invoice equivalent). So the order is un-re-invoiceable from the UI with no in-app remedy. The code comment at OrderDetail.tsx:1190-1194 confirms the design intent the bug defeats: "a fulfilled allocated order whose split group was later VOIDED (flag already cleared, hasActiveInvoice now false) can be RE-billed here" — voided was handled, soft-deleted was not.

5) The Post All Drafts misfire is real. Line 931 selects `status === 'draft' || 'unposted'` from the unfiltered array; the staleness pre-check at line 946 uses `supabase.from('invoices').select('status').is('deleted_at', null)`, which returns 0 rows for a deleted standalone invoice, so `currentRows && currentRows.length > 0` is false, the `alreadyPosted` skip never fires, and `post_invoice` is invoked.

CORRECTION to the reviewer's evidence: `post_invoice` does not raise "Invoice not found" — supabase/migrations/20260721014858_20260721010000_govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:647-652 raises `INVOICE_DELETED: a soft-deleted invoice cannot be posted`. (The 20260721145936 wrapper only adds the idempotency-key requirement and delegates to the renamed `_post_invoice_idem_impl_20260721`, so the guard is still live.) The user-visible outcome the reviewer describes — an unexplained per-target error toast on an invoice they already deleted — is unchanged, only the message text differs.

Not in the baselines: `grep -i "delete_invoices|soft-deleted invoice|Create Invoice"` over docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md hits only the 2026-07-12 line about a *different* fix ("unbilled-delivery guard now ignores soft-deleted invoices", migration 20260712170000) — same bug class, different call site, which supports rather than duplicates this finding. The 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet doc has no `deleted_at`, "soft-delet", "hasActiveInvoice", or "Create Invoice" hits; its only OrderDetail references are 862-885 and 963-970 (split/atomic posting).

Deliveries half: the reviewer's stated distinction is accurate. I found no migration writing `deliveries.deleted_at` (`grep -rn "deliveries" supabase/migrations/*.sql | grep -i "SET deleted_at"` → no matches), though readers assume it (post_invoice checks `d.deleted_at IS NULL`). Dormant/defensive today, correctly labeled as such.

Severity: MED is defensible and I am not changing it, though HIGH would also be arguable — the state is unrecoverable through the app on a billing path, requiring direct DB intervention.

### [MED] Integrity/reconciliation report runs unpaginated PostgREST reads — silently truncated at the ~1000-row API cap, producing both false PASS and false discrepancies

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** src/lib/reconciliation.ts:653-658, :688-693, :728-735, :780-781, :808-813, :843-847, :888-892, :922-923, :950-951

**Detail.** Every table read in runReconciliation() is an unbounded `.select()` with no `.range()` paging and no `.limit()`. The repo elsewhere documents and handles this exact cap: `src/pages/Products.tsx:104` — "PostgREST caps a single response (default 1000), so we page through with .range()." and `src/pages/DispatchBoard.tsx:80` — "caps a single response at ~1000 rows, so we page via .range() until a short page ends the scan". The reconciliation module got none of that treatment. Two distinct failure modes: (a) a check reports `passed: true` after inspecting only the first 1000 rows, and `entitiesChecked` reports the truncated count so the number looks plausible; (b) paired queries truncate independently, so e.g. `orders` rows 1-1000 are compared against `order_items` rows 1-1000, which do not correspond — the same for delivery_items vs invoice_items, quotes vs inventory_holds, inventory vs inventory_transactions, and invoices vs invoice_line_allocations. `inventory_transactions`, `invoice_line_allocations`, `order_items`, `invoice_items` and `delivery_items` all exceed 1000 rows in a production ag-chem distributor's ledger. This is the reporting surface Mason would use to decide whether the books are sound.

**Evidence.** src/lib/reconciliation.ts:727-735:
      supabase
        .from('invoices')
        .select('id, invoice_number, order_id, invoice_type, paid_amount_cents, prepay_applied_cents, write_off_cents, credit_applied_cents, total_amount_cents, balance_cents')
        .eq('status', 'posted')
        .is('deleted_at', null),
      supabase
        .from('invoice_line_allocations')
        .select('invoice_id, amount_cents'),

src/pages/Products.tsx:104: `// PostgREST caps a single response (default 1000), so we page through with .range().`
src/pages/DispatchBoard.tsx:80: `// caps a single response at ~1000 rows, so we page via .range() until a short page`

**Failure scenario.** Once `invoice_line_allocations` exceeds 1000 rows, the Integrity page's "Invoice Payments" check compares each posted invoice's `paid_amount_cents` against a sum built from only the first 1000 allocation rows. Every invoice whose allocations fall outside that window reports a discrepancy equal to its full paid amount — hundreds of false BLOCKER-looking rows — while any genuine mismatch beyond row 1000 of `invoices` is never examined and the check still prints a count that implies full coverage.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — I attempted to refute this and could not. Verification performed:

1. CITATIONS ARE ACCURATE. I re-read every cited line of /home/user/CRX_Manager_V1.0/src/lib/reconciliation.ts at HEAD (7da1896a). All ten DB reads in `runReconciliationChecks()` (the finding calls it `runReconciliation()` — minor naming slip, the real export at :646 is `runReconciliationChecks`) are unbounded `.select()` with no `.range()`, no `.limit()`, and no `.order()`: :652-658 (orders/order_items), :687-693 (inventory/inventory_transactions), :727-734 (invoices/invoice_line_allocations), :779-781 (commissions), :807-813 (quotes/inventory_holds), :842-847 (delivery_items/invoice_items), :887-893 (inventory/order_items), :921-924 (returns), :949-952 (invoices). `grep -n "range(\|limit("` over the file returns nothing. The comparison quotes are also accurate: src/pages/Products.tsx:103-105 ("PostgREST caps a single response (default 1000), so we page through with .range()", `const PAGE_SIZE = 1000`) and src/pages/DispatchBoard.tsx:79-83 ("caps a single response at ~1000 rows, so we page via .range()", `const DISPATCH_PAGE = 1000`). So the repo demonstrably knows about the cap and reconciliation.ts is the outlier.

2. NO MISSED ENFORCEMENT MECHANISM. There is no client-level default page size that would change this: src/lib/db.ts:15-42 constructs the only Supabase client with `auth` and `global.fetch` options only — no `db` schema/limit config, no query wrapper that injects `.range()`. There is no RPC-side aggregation these checks fall back to; the module header at src/lib/reconciliation.ts:11-15 states the design explicitly ("DB wrappers are thin and only fetch + delegate"), and IntegrityReportPanel.tsx:15 calls `runReconciliationChecks()` directly with no paging shim. This is "the code does not paginate" (verified by reading it), not "I couldn't find where paging happens."

3. THE FAILURE MECHANISM IS MECHANICALLY DEMONSTRABLE, not speculative. checkInvoicePayments (src/lib/reconciliation.ts:257-285) does `const allocatedTotal = allocatedByInvoice.get(inv.id) ?? 0;` — a truncated allocation set does not skip the invoice, it scores it as paid-zero and emits a discrepancy of the full paid amount. checkOrderTotals (:79-111) has the identical `itemsByOrder.get(order.id) ?? []` shape. Because none of the queries carry `.order()`, PostgREST row order is unspecified, so *which* 1000 rows return is not even stable between the paired queries or between runs. The silent-PASS direction is real too: `entitiesChecked` is set from the truncated array length (e.g. :672 `entitiesChecked: orders.length`), so the UI prints a coverage number that looks plausible.

4. NOT IN THE BASELINE. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md contains no entry for reconciliation pagination, PostgREST row caps, or the Integrity page (greps for "pagination", "1000", "range(", "truncat", "Integrity"/"reconcil" return only unrelated matter — profile ACLs, migration drift, commission-name guards). docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md mentions "reconciliation" only in the sense of profit-figure reconciliation inside the bulk-import migration (lines 59, 91), nothing about client read paging. So this is genuinely beyond the baseline.

The one thing I could not verify with the read-only toolset permitted here is current live row counts for invoice_line_allocations / inventory_transactions / order_items — I did not query the live DB. So the defect is a latent capability failure whose trigger condition (any of those tables passing ~1000 rows) I confirmed is not guarded in code, but did not confirm has already been crossed.

### [MED] Three mutually inconsistent AR derivations across the three pages that report a customer's AR

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260404040200_get_customer_summary_rpc.sql:31-35; supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:372-377; supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:176-181, :222-228

**Detail.** CustomerDetail's AR Balance card (via CustomerSummaryBar.tsx:46 → get_customer_summary) sums RAW `balance_cents` for `status IN ('posted','overdue')` with no `deleted_at` filter and no `invoice_type` exclusion — so a posted credit memo's negative balance is netted in, and a soft-deleted posted invoice is still counted. FinancialDashboard (financial_dashboard_summary) sums `GREATEST(balance_cents, 0)` with `deleted_at IS NULL` — credits contribute zero and soft-deletes are excluded. get_ar_aging excludes `invoice_type = 'credit_memo'` outright, filters voided/deleted at the cutoff, requires a positive reconstructed statement balance, and reports credit in its own `open_credit_cents` column. Three reports of the same quantity, three answers. QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:87 asserts `invoices.balance_cents` is the single source of truth, which is true of the column but not of the aggregations built on it.

**Evidence.** supabase/migrations/20260404040200_get_customer_summary_rpc.sql:30-35:
  -- AR Balance (sum of unpaid invoice balances)
  SELECT COALESCE(sum(balance_cents), 0)
  INTO v_ar_balance
  FROM invoices
  WHERE customer_id = p_customer_id
    AND status IN ('posted', 'overdue');

supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:372-377:
  ar AS (
    SELECT COALESCE(SUM(GREATEST(balance_cents, 0)), 0) / 100.0 AS balance
    FROM public.invoices
    WHERE status IN ('posted', 'overdue')
      AND deleted_at IS NULL
  ),

supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:178: `WHERE i.invoice_type <> 'credit_memo'`

**Failure scenario.** A customer owes $10,000 on posted invoices and holds a $4,000 posted credit memo not yet applied. CustomerDetail shows AR Balance $6,000. AR Aging shows Total Outstanding $10,000 with $4,000 in a separate "Credit on file" column. The Financial Dashboard's open AR contribution for that customer is $10,000. A sales rep reading CustomerDetail and an admin reading AR Aging disagree by $4,000 on the same account with no visible reason.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED, with one sub-claim refuted. (1) Not in baseline: grep of docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md (1085 lines) and the full 2026-08-05 section-04 doc (101 lines, entirely about BulkOrderImport/bulk_import_order) found no AR-derivation item. (2) Migration text verified verbatim as quoted at all three cited locations. (3) Supersession check: `grep -rln get_customer_summary supabase/migrations/` returns only 3 files — 20260404040200 (the sole CREATE), 20260429140635 (a comment at :24 only), and 20260529214355:56-57 (REVOKE/GRANT only). No later migration redefines the body, so `SELECT COALESCE(sum(balance_cents),0) ... WHERE customer_id = p_customer_id AND status IN ('posted','overdue')` with no invoice_type and no deleted_at filter is current. (4) The credit-memo netting is DB-guaranteed real, not hypothetical: .claude/schema-registry.json:3000 carries CHECK `((invoice_type <> 'credit_memo') OR (balance_cents <= 0))`, and 20260609131312_credit_memo_draft_insert_exemption.sql:27 exempts credit memos from the draft-on-insert rule precisely because issue_return_credit inserts them at status='posted'. So a posted credit memo is a posted-status row with negative balance_cents that get_customer_summary sums. (5) The strongest confirming evidence the reviewer did not cite: supabase/migrations/20260712210000_ar_credit_visibility.sql:1-22 records a settled decision — "Option B credit visibility (Mason's decision 2026-07-11): open (unapplied) credit-memo balances NEVER auto-net into AR — instead every AR report surfaces them as a separate 'credit on file' figure". That migration updated exactly four functions (get_ar_reminder_candidates, get_ar_aging, preview_finance_charges, get_customer_balance_listing). get_customer_summary was not among them, so the AR Balance card is a live violation of the settled policy, not a deliberate "net AR" view. (6) The failure scenario is reachable on ONE screen, which the reviewer understated: src/pages/CustomerDetail.tsx:974 renders `<CustomerSummaryBar customerId={id} .../>` (always visible), whose src/components/customers/CustomerSummaryBar.tsx:46 calls get_customer_summary and :88-93 renders it as the card labeled `'AR Balance'`; the same page at src/pages/CustomerDetail.tsx:531 calls get_ar_aging and at :1750/:1777-1779 renders `aging.total_outstanding` buckets plus "Credit on file: ... in unapplied credit memos". Same customer, same page, two numbers differing by the open credit. (7) REFUTED sub-claim: "a soft-deleted posted invoice is still counted" cannot occur. Every disk version of delete_invoices gates identically — 20260617031416:69 `IF v_inv.status NOT IN ('draft','unposted','voided') THEN CONTINUE;` (header at :8-10: "Posted / paid / overdue invoices are NOT deletable"), repeated at 20260620140000:106, 20260707060000:1467, 20260707140000:1084, 20260711050000:793 — and there is no raw frontend `.update({deleted_at})` on invoices (src/pages/Invoices.tsx:437 comment: "of a raw .update({ deleted_at }). See migration delete_invoices"). deleted_at is therefore never set on a row in ('posted','overdue'), so the missing deleted_at filter in get_customer_summary is dead code, not a defect. The credit-memo half of the finding stands on its own. (8) Minor framing correction: financial_dashboard_summary's `ar` CTE (20260714223000:372-377) is company-wide, not per-customer, so "the Financial Dashboard's open AR contribution for that customer" is loose phrasing — but the GREATEST(balance_cents,0) vs raw-sum divergence is real and does make the company AR total inconsistent with the sum of the per-customer cards. Severity MED is correct and I would not raise it: this is a reporting/display inconsistency with no ledger or generated-column corruption, the netted figure understates receivables (conservative direction for collections risk but wrong for a rep quoting a balance), and the authoritative dunning and finance-charge paths (get_ar_reminder_candidates, preview_finance_charges) are already Option-B correct.

### [MED] Quote-Hold Parity integrity check is one-directional and blind to soft-deleted quotes, while Quotes.tsx soft-delete leaves planned holds active

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** src/lib/reconciliation.ts:414, :431-443, :808-811, :824-827; src/pages/Quotes.tsx:32, :376-412

**Detail.** `checkQuoteHoldParity` only flags a planned quote in `draft/sent/revised` that has NO active hold. It never flags the converse — an active `inventory_holds` row whose source quote reached a terminal status or was soft-deleted. The feeding query at :808-811 also filters `.is('deleted_at', null)`, so a soft-deleted quote's stranded holds are excluded from the input entirely. Meanwhile `Quotes.tsx` bulk delete is a direct `.update({ deleted_at })` (:406-410) on quotes whose status is in `DELETABLE = ['draft','sent','revised']` (:32). Its own comment acknowledges the mechanism — "deletion changes no status, so the hold-release trigger never fires" — but the guard it added only skips quotes with a draw-down or job draw (:388-400). A planned quote with active holds and zero draws is deletable, and the release trigger fires only on a status transition (supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:133-134: `IF NEW.status IN ('accepted','declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short') AND OLD.status NOT IN (...)`). So the holds survive, and the only report that could catch them is structurally unable to. Caveat: the trigger body cited is the newest on-disk re-emit; I did not query live pg_proc, and I did not query live data for existing stranded holds.

**Evidence.** src/lib/reconciliation.ts:431-433:
    // Only check planned quotes in active statuses
    if (!q.is_planned || !ACTIVE_QUOTE_STATUSES.has(q.status)) continue;

    if (!sourceIdsWithActiveHold.has(q.id)) {

src/lib/reconciliation.ts:807-811:
      supabase
        .from('quotes')
        .select('id, quote_number, is_planned, status')
        .is('deleted_at', null),

src/pages/Quotes.tsx:380-382:
      // Open bookings with partial draw-downs must not be soft-deleted:
      // deletion changes no status, so the hold-release trigger never fires —
      // the booking's reserved inventory would stay held on a hidden quote.

**Failure scenario.** A rep bulk-deletes three stale planned quotes that were never drawn against. Their `inventory_holds` rows stay `is_active = true` with `source_id` pointing at hidden quotes, so Net Position keeps that stock reserved forever. The Integrity page's Quote-Hold Parity check reports PASS because the deleted quotes are filtered out of its input and it never looks for orphaned holds in the first place.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED as a real gap — but the failure scenario is materially overstated and the reviewer missed one enforcement mechanism (which also misses this case, so the conclusion survives).

WHAT HELD UP (verified in current source, not inferred):
1. `checkQuoteHoldParity` is one-directional. src/lib/reconciliation.ts:414 `const ACTIVE_QUOTE_STATUSES = new Set(['draft','sent','revised']);` and :430-433 `if (!q.is_planned || !ACTIVE_QUOTE_STATUSES.has(q.status)) continue; if (!sourceIdsWithActiveHold.has(q.id))`. It iterates `quotes`, never `holds` — there is no loop over holds looking for a missing/terminal/deleted parent. Confirmed.
2. Feeding query excludes soft-deleted quotes. src/lib/reconciliation.ts:807-811 `.from('quotes').select('id, quote_number, is_planned, status').is('deleted_at', null)`. Confirmed. `entitiesChecked` at :824-827 likewise counts only live planned quotes.
3. Quotes.tsx soft-delete leaves status untouched. src/pages/Quotes.tsx:405-410 `.from('quotes').update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() }).in('id', ids)` — no status write. The only skip-guard is the draw-down/job-draw filter at :387-400. `DELETABLE = ['draft','sent','revised']` (:32) gates selection by STATUS ONLY — :323 `isSelectable: (q) => DELETABLE.includes(q.status)` and :327 — so an `is_planned` booking in sent/revised with active holds and zero draws IS selectable and deletable. Confirmed.
4. The release trigger cannot fire. Newest on-disk emit of `release_holds_on_quote_status_change()` is supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:125-152 (I checked every later migration touching the object; none re-emits it). Its guard is `IF NEW.status IN ('accepted','declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short') AND OLD.status NOT IN (...)`. A soft-delete leaves NEW.status = OLD.status = 'sent', so the guard is false regardless of the trigger's UPDATE-OF clause. This is "the DB does not enforce it," checked against migrations — not "I couldn't find it."
5. No recovery path exists. `auto_expire_quotes()` (supabase/migrations/20260614143649_auto_expire_quotes_skip_planned_and_schedule.sql:33-40) filters `AND q.deleted_at IS NULL ... AND q.is_planned = false` — it skips soft-deleted quotes AND planned quotes, so it never sweeps these. `_sync_planned_holds` (supabase/migrations/20260702171000_layer2_sync_planned_holds_job_aware.sql:41-44) does contain a release arm, but it opens with `SELECT * INTO v_quote FROM quotes WHERE id = p_quote_id AND deleted_at IS NULL FOR UPDATE; IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN 0; END IF;` — it returns early on a deleted quote and never reaches the `UPDATE inventory_holds SET is_active = false` at :50-52. Confirmed no cleanup.
6. Not in either baseline. Neither docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md nor docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md mentions quote soft-delete, stranded holds, or Quote-Hold Parity. Novel.

CORRECTION A — reviewer missed a mechanism (which also misses this case). The reviewer says "the only report that could catch them is structurally unable to." False as stated: a DB-side sentinel exists. `run_data_integrity_sweep()` emits a `stale_quote_hold` alert — newest emit supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:643-655: `FROM inventory_holds h JOIN quotes q ON q.id = h.source_id WHERE h.is_active = true AND h.hold_type = 'crop_program' AND q.status IN ('declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short')`. That is exactly the converse check the reviewer says doesn't exist. HOWEVER it keys on terminal STATUS, and a soft-deleted quote keeps status 'sent'/'revised' — and there is no `q.deleted_at IS NOT NULL` arm. So the sentinel misses this precise case too, and the reviewer's conclusion (nothing catches it) stands. The evidence should cite the sentinel and its status-keyed blind spot rather than claiming no converse check exists.

CORRECTION B — the failure scenario is wrong on two counts, and should be restated:
(i) "Net Position keeps that stock reserved forever" is false. `net_position` never subtracts holds: supabase/migrations/20260716120120_gauntlet_inventory_accuracy.sql:169 `(b.quantity_available - b.quantity_prebooked + COALESCE(oo.qty, 0)) AS net_position`, and src/types/index.ts:1007 states it outright — "holds_qty and planned_qty are reported separately; they are NOT subtracted from net_position." The REAL impacts are (a) the displayed reservation, src/pages/InventoryPage.tsx:213 `total_planned_qty: p.holds_qty + p.planned_qty` rendered as the "Planned" column (:143), and (b) an operational block — `create_inventory_hold` computes `v_todays_free := quantity_available - quantity_prebooked - v_active_holds` over ALL active holds and raises `INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY` (supabase/migrations/20260630173022_parked_010_create_inventory_hold_auth_first.sql:170-187), so a phantom hold can refuse a legitimate new reservation.
(ii) "Forever" only holds for undated sections. Planned holds are inserted with `expires_at` = `v_item.needed_by_date + INTERVAL '14 days'` (supabase/migrations/20260702171000_layer2_sync_planned_holds_job_aware.sql:91-99), and every consumer filters `(expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at >= CURRENT_DATE)` — the position RPC's `holds` CTE (20260716120120:59-63) and `create_inventory_hold`'s free calc (20260630173022:174). So a stranded hold on a DATED section ages out of all free/position math 14 days past needed-by (still bounded but potentially many months for a season booking). It is permanent only when `quote_sections.needed_by_date IS NULL` — nullable per supabase/migrations/20260316500000_planned_programs.sql:6 `ALTER TABLE quote_sections ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS needed_by_date date;`, src/types/index.ts:707 `needed_by_date: string | null`, and .claude/schema-registry.json not_null_columns.quote_sections = {no_default: ['quote_id'], with_default: ['id','section_name','sort_order']} — not listed, so nullable live. `NULL + INTERVAL` yields NULL, so those holds never expire out of any filter.

ADJACENT LEAK THE REVIEWER MISSED (same root cause, worth folding in): the position RPC's `planned_quotes` CTE also omits a deleted_at filter — supabase/migrations/20260716120120_gauntlet_inventory_accuracy.sql:81-84 `FROM quote_items qi JOIN quotes q ON q.id = qi.quote_id WHERE q.is_planned = true AND q.status IN ('draft','sent','revised')`. A soft-deleted planned quote keeps that status, so its undrawn booking remainder ALSO keeps inflating `planned_qty` forever — with no expires_at escape hatch at all. That half is strictly worse than the hold half.

SEVERITY: MED sustained. Impact is reporting distortion plus a real `INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY` block on new reservations, with no report or sentinel anywhere that surfaces it; not money, not data loss, and partially self-healing via expires_at. Caveat carried forward: like the reviewer, I read on-disk migrations only — I did not query live pg_proc or live data for existing stranded holds.

### [LOW] generate_order_number() and generate_quote_number() remain EXECUTE-able by anon and call nextval() — a logged-out visitor can burn the order and quote number sequences

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:1026-1031, 1650-1655; baseline public_schema.sql lines 25465, 25479

**Detail.** Both functions are invoker-rights but call `nextval('order_number_seq')` / `nextval('quote_number_seq')`, which is a mutation, and `anon` holds `SELECT, UPDATE, USAGE` on sequences via the standing `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`. Their ACLs still carry `TO PUBLIC` and `TO anon`. The 2026-07-28 hardening (`20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions`, KNOWN_ISSUES section 0c) explicitly identified the document-number allocators as "the actual live exposure" and revoked the six `next_*_number()` functions — it did not include these two, which are the allocators the order and quote creation RPCs actually use. I diffed the full set of 95 anon-EXECUTE grants in the DR baseline against the 43 revoked by the two 2026-07-28 migrations, and grepped every migration for a later revoke of either name: none exists. Note this is a class the project already decided to close; these two are the residue, not a new judgement call.

**Evidence.** acl_lockdown.sql:1026-1031 —
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_order_number() TO PUBLIC;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_order_number() TO anon;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_order_number() TO authenticated;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_order_number() TO service_role;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_quote_number() TO PUBLIC;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_quote_number() TO anon;

acl_lockdown.sql:1650,1653 —
ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public GRANT SELECT, UPDATE, USAGE ON SEQUENCES TO anon;
ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS TO anon;

function bodies —
CREATE FUNCTION "public"."generate_order_number"() RETURNS "text"
    LANGUAGE "plpgsql"
    SET "search_path" TO 'public'
    AS $$
BEGIN
  RETURN 'ORD-' || extract(year FROM current_date)::text || '-' || lpad(nextval('order_number_seq')::text, 4, '0');
END;
$$;

20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql (header) — "the six next_*_number() document-number allocators had no auth gate of any kind"; revoked names are next_application_record_number, next_commission_payment_number, next_cycle_count_number, next_delivery_number, next_job_number, next_po_number.

**Failure scenario.** An unauthenticated visitor with the publishable anon key (embedded in the shipped bundle) loops POST /rest/v1/rpc/generate_order_number a few thousand times. `order_number_seq` advances with no order rows created. The next real order created by a sales_rep is numbered ORD-2026-4821 instead of ORD-2026-0143, leaving a permanent unexplained gap in the business's order-number series (and the same for quote numbers), which is an audit and reconciliation problem for a distributor whose order numbers are customer-facing references.

**Verifier verdict.** NOT REFUTED — every load-bearing element checks out against current source; severity corrected MED → LOW because the verified impact is cosmetic/informational, not integrity- or money-affecting.

WHAT I VERIFIED (each independently, not taking the reviewer's word):

1. Grants exist as quoted. `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:1026-1033` reads exactly:
   `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.generate_order_number() TO PUBLIC;` / `... TO anon;` and the same four-line block for `generate_quote_number()`. The reviewer's line cite 1026-1031 is right (the block actually runs 1026-1033).

2. No later migration revokes them. `grep -rn "generate_order_number\|generate_quote_number" supabase/` returns 60+ hits and NOT ONE is in `20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql` or `20260728233459_revoke_anon_execute_rls_role_helpers.sql`. I read the complete REVOKE list of both files (lines 61-80, 92-99, 108-119 of half 1; lines 56-58 of half 2 = 43 signatures total): the six revoked allocators are `next_application_record_number`, `next_commission_payment_number`, `next_cycle_count_number`, `next_delivery_number`, `next_job_number`, `next_po_number`. Neither `generate_*` name appears. I also enumerated all 29 migrations with timestamps after the baseline (`20260728231350` … `20260807220323`) and grepped for `REVOKE` on sequences or `ALL FUNCTIONS` — zero hits. The earlier `20260529214355_revoke_anon_execute_on_report_dashboard_secdef.sql` covers 37 report/dashboard SECDEF functions; neither name is in it either. So there is no superseding migration: this is "the DB does not enforce it," checked in the migrations, not "I couldn't find where it's enforced."

3. The functions really are invoker-rights and really do mutate. Decompressed `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br` (brotli) — body is `CREATE FUNCTION "public"."generate_order_number"() RETURNS "text" LANGUAGE "plpgsql" SET "search_path" TO 'public' AS $$ ... nextval('order_number_seq') ... $$;` with NO `SECURITY DEFINER` clause. Original definition at `supabase/migrations/20260209200000_tier1_audit_fixes.sql:133-149` confirms the same (only `search_path` was pinned later). Zero-arg VOLATILE plpgsql returning text = PostgREST exposes it at `POST /rest/v1/rpc/...`.

4. The sequence privilege the reviewer asserts is actually stronger than claimed, and explicit. Not merely via `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` (that only covers future sequences) — `acl_lockdown.sql:407` and `:473` carry direct grants: `GRANT SELECT, UPDATE, USAGE ON SEQUENCE public.order_number_seq TO anon;` and the same for `quote_number_seq`. Invoker-rights + anon holding USAGE/UPDATE = `nextval()` succeeds as anon. This is the one place the reviewer's mechanism description is imprecise, and the imprecision is in the finding's disfavor, not its favor.

5. Not already documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md:532-590` (section 0c) names exactly 40+3 functions and calls the six `next_*_number()` "the actual live exposure"; `generate_order_number`/`generate_quote_number` are absent from that section and from the whole file. `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md` never mentions either name or anon function grants. No `docs/` or `.claude/` file tracks this as an open item. The reviewer's framing — residue of a class the project already closed — is accurate.

6. The callers claim is accurate. `generate_order_number()` is called by the live order-creation path including `20260702172500_layer2_convert_quote_to_order_job_aware.sql:155`, `20260614142939_create_direct_order_customer_po_param.sql:93`, `20260614144757_create_rush_order.sql:118`; `generate_quote_number()` by `20260609195843_strict_actor_quote_rpcs.sql:126,328` and `20260611211058_idempotency_operation_scope_sweep.sql:1113,2027`. These are the real allocators, unlike `next_po_number` which had no live callsite.

WHY I LOWERED SEVERITY TO LOW. I tried to find a harder impact and could not:
- Both `orders.order_number` and `quotes.quote_number` are `"text" NOT NULL` (baseline public_schema), so an inflated `lpad(...,4,'0')` value cannot overflow a column and cause insert failures — no DoS on order creation.
- `CREATE SEQUENCE "public"."order_number_seq" START WITH 1 INCREMENT BY 1 NO MINVALUE NO MAXVALUE CACHE 1` — bigint ceiling 9.2e18, unreachable over HTTP, so no exhaustion failure mode.
- anon holds UPDATE on the sequences, which in principle permits `setval()` (rewinding into collisions with existing numbers would be genuinely bad), but `setval` lives in `pg_catalog`, is not in an exposed schema, and PostgREST will not route to it — so the only reachable primitive is monotonic advance.
- Residual impact is therefore: (a) permanent unexplained gaps in customer-facing order/quote number series, remediable only by an admin `setval` and not retroactively for numbers already issued, and (b) minor business-volume disclosure — an unauthenticated poller learns CRX's cumulative order and quote counts and, over time, their velocity.
No confidentiality of customer/money data, no record-integrity violation, no privilege escalation, one-line fix (`REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon` on both, naming both grantees per the section-0c mechanics note). That is LOW. The counter-argument for holding MED is class parity — the six `next_*_number()` siblings are mechanically identical and the project revoked them as a security fix — which is a consistency argument for fixing it, not evidence of higher impact.

CAVEAT ON EVIDENCE STRENGTH: this is an offline determination. The strongest artifact available to me is the `20260727174805` DR baseline, which is a capture of live ACLs at that timestamp, plus an exhaustive read of the 29 post-baseline migrations. I did not query the live database. If any ACL change reached production through the Management API without a repo migration file, the conclusion about current live grants could differ; note that `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md:802-806` records that 654 live ledger versions have no matching repo file, so that channel demonstrably exists. Confirming with one read-only `has_function_privilege('anon', 'public.generate_order_number()', 'EXECUTE')` against live would close the gap.

---

### [LOW] invoices_select / invoice_items_select ownership arms carry no role and no is_active predicate, and both policies are granted to PUBLIC rather than authenticated

- **Finder:** `rls-security`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:254-285; baseline public_schema.sql lines 66652 (invoices_select), 66529 (invoice_items_select)

**Detail.** `invoices_select` is `USING (is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid())` and `invoice_items_select` inherits the same predicate through an EXISTS on the parent. Two defects compose. (1) The two ownership arms have no role test and no `profiles.is_active` test — `is_admin()` requires an active profile, but the ownership arms do not, so any account of any role whose uuid appears in `created_by`/`salesman_id` reads the invoice and its `cost_cents`/`total_cost_cents` lines, and a deactivated account keeps that read for the life of an already-issued access token (deactivation kills sessions per 20260727174805 but cannot revoke an outstanding JWT). The 2026-07-27 sweep that added `is_active` to 38 policies (KNOWN_ISSUES 0a) targeted policies gating on `profiles.role` inline; ownership-uuid arms were outside its enumeration. (2) Neither `CREATE POLICY` has a `TO` clause, so the grantee is PUBLIC — including `anon`. That is the exact grantee drift 20260727231652 pinned for `rebate_programs_insert/update/delete` ("leaving them PUBLIC means anon writes are refused by policy evaluation rather than refused outright"). It is harmless today only because `anon` holds no DML grant on invoices and `auth.uid()` is NULL for anon; it is not true by construction. The same PUBLIC-grantee shape applies to `invoices_insert/update/delete`, `invoice_items_insert/update/delete`, `orders_insert`, `oitems_insert`, `invoice_line_alloc_select`, and `order_line_alloc_select`.

**Evidence.** 20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262 —
DROP POLICY IF EXISTS invoices_select ON invoices;
CREATE POLICY invoices_select ON invoices FOR SELECT
  USING (
    is_admin()
    OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid())
    OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid())
  );

(no TO clause anywhere in the statement; live deparse confirms it — baseline line 66652:)
CREATE POLICY "invoices_select" ON "public"."invoices" FOR SELECT USING (("public"."is_admin"() OR ("created_by" = ( SELECT "auth"."uid"() AS "uid")) OR ("salesman_id" = ( SELECT "auth"."uid"() AS "uid"))));

20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:276-284 —
CREATE POLICY invoice_items_select ON invoice_items FOR SELECT
  USING (
    EXISTS (
      SELECT 1 FROM invoices
      WHERE invoices.id = invoice_items.invoice_id
      AND (is_admin() OR invoices.created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR invoices.salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))
    )
  );

Contrast — 20260727231652_quote_and_rate_reads_office_only.sql:194-196 pinned exactly this drift elsewhere:
ALTER POLICY rebate_programs_insert ON public.rebate_programs TO authenticated;

**Failure scenario.** A sales_rep is deactivated at 09:00; `profiles.is_active` flips false, their sessions and refresh tokens are deleted. Their browser still holds an access token minted at 08:45. Until ~09:45 every query gated by is_admin()/is_sales_rep() correctly returns nothing, but SELECT on `invoices` and `invoice_items` still returns every invoice where salesman_id or created_by is their uuid — including per-line cost_cents and total_cost_cents — because those two arms never consult profiles at all.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on the facts, but severity overstated — corrected MED -> LOW. What I verified independently: (1) Policy text is exactly as quoted. `supabase/migrations/20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262` creates `invoices_select` as `USING (is_admin() OR created_by = (SELECT auth.uid()) OR salesman_id = (SELECT auth.uid()))` with no TO clause and no is_active predicate; `:276-284` creates `invoice_items_select` inheriting the same predicate via EXISTS on the parent, also with no TO clause. (2) No supersession. Only two migrations in the whole tree mention these policy names — the creating file and `20260729015706_application_service_cost_admin_only.sql`, and in that later file the mention is a comment block (`:51-58`), not DDL: "Both are reachable under `invoices_select` / `invoice_items_select`, verified live as `is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid()`". `grep 'ALTER POLICY' supabase/migrations/*.sql` returns zero hits for any invoice policy. (3) Live shape confirmed. Decompressing `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_public_schema.sql.br` reproduces the cited lines verbatim — 66652 `invoices_select`, 66529 `invoice_items_select` — and a full policy enumeration on both tables (lines 66515/66522/66529/66538/66638/66645/66652/66659) shows only four PERMISSIVE policies per table, no AS RESTRICTIVE policy, and no is_active gate anywhere. pg_dump omits the TO clause only when the grantee is PUBLIC, so the PUBLIC-grantee half is confirmed. (4) The is_active gap is genuinely outside both remediation sweeps. `20260727145843_inline_role_checks_require_active_profile.sql` and `20260727174657_broad_reads_require_active_profile.sql` contain zero occurrences of "invoice" — the first targeted inline `profiles.role` predicates (38 -> 0), the second targeted `USING (true)` reads (31 -> 0). An ownership-uuid arm is neither shape, so the reviewer is right that KNOWN_ISSUES 0a's enumeration never covered it. Why LOW, not MED: (a) The exploitable delta is only self-scoped data the actor could already read, retained for at most one access-token lifetime. `20260727174805_deactivation_revokes_auth_access.sql:94-99` sets `banned_until = '9999-12-31'` AND does `DELETE FROM auth.refresh_tokens` + `DELETE FROM auth.sessions`, so the token cannot be renewed — the window is hard-bounded (~1h), unlike the pre-2026-07-27 unbounded case that made 0a serious. (b) The cost-disclosure consequence the finding leads with is already documented. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md:380` (section 0e, OPEN OWNER DECISIONS): "Sales reps can still recover internal application-service cost for invoices assigned to them from `invoice_items.cost_cents` plus acres or `invoices.total_cost_cents`... Narrowing sales-rep visibility on their own invoices is a product decision for Mason, not a grant-only correction." The finding does not make that materially worse; it only adds the deactivation window on top. (c) The PUBLIC-grantee half has zero exploitability and is a measured, accepted project-wide posture, not a new discovery. KNOWN_ISSUES.md:563 records "30 tables / 70 PUBLIC-audience policies for `is_admin`" as the deliberately-judged blast radius of `20260728233459` — `invoices_select` is one of those 70. Note a factual slip in the finding's own reasoning here: it says the PUBLIC grantee is "harmless today only because `anon` holds no DML grant on invoices", but `supabase/baselines/20260727174805_acl_lockdown.sql:304` shows `GRANT MAINTAIN, SELECT ON TABLE public.invoices TO anon` — anon does hold SELECT. The real reason there is no leak is that the predicate cannot be true for anon (auth.uid() IS NULL) and, since `20260728233459_revoke_anon_execute_rls_role_helpers.sql`, anon's `is_admin()` call raises 42501 outright. Conclusion is right, stated mechanism is wrong. (d) The "any account of any role" framing is weak: `invoices_insert` (`:265-267`) is `WITH CHECK (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())`, so `created_by` is office-only in practice; `salesman_id` is only FK-constrained to `profiles(id)` (`:51`) with no role check, so a driver/applicator uuid there is DB-permitted but requires someone to deliberately set it. Net: real, novel, correctly evidenced residual — a bounded-window is_active gap on two ownership-uuid RLS arms plus cosmetic grantee drift. Worth a forward-only follow-up (add `is_active_profile()` to both ownership arms and `ALTER POLICY ... TO authenticated` on the listed policies), but not MED.

### [LOW] Quotes list Duplicate reuses a stale, page-scoped idempotency key across different quotes — a lost response silently re-opens the previous quote's copy instead of duplicating the one clicked

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:50, 108-128 (getKey at 111, error return at 117-120, resetKey at 121); supabase/migrations/20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-51; supabase/migrations/20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql (duplicate_quote body)

**Detail.** `duplicateQuoteIdem` is one hook instance for the whole page, and `handleDuplicate` never resets it before the call — it only resets AFTER a successful response (line 121) and returns early without resetting on error (lines 117-120). Every row's Duplicate button shares that one key.

`check_idempotency` is key-only — it compares only `idempotency_key` and `operation`, never the arguments — so a replay for `duplicate_quote` returns the cached result regardless of which `p_source_quote_id` was sent. This is confirmed in the shipped helper, not inferred.

Every sibling handler in this codebase resets per target and this one does not: `openConvert` does `convertQuoteIdem.resetKey()` (Quotes.tsx:137), CommissionPayments resets at each row button (503/526/552), Deliveries resets at modal open (1135-1136), InvoiceDetail resets at 1458, OrderDetail at `onCreateInvoiceClick`.

**Evidence.** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:110-123
    try {
      const key = duplicateQuoteIdem.getKey();
      const { data: result, error } = await supabase.rpc('duplicate_quote', {
        p_source_quote_id: quoteId,
        p_performed_by: profile!.id,
        p_idempotency_key: key,
      });
      if (error) {
        toast('error', `Failed to duplicate quote: ${error.message}`);
        return;
      }
      duplicateQuoteIdem.resetKey();

supabase/migrations/20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:35-48 (check_idempotency — no payload comparison)
  SELECT *
    INTO v_existing
    FROM public.idempotency_keys
   WHERE idempotency_key = p_key;
  IF FOUND AND v_existing.operation IS DISTINCT FROM p_operation THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'IDEMPOTENCY_CROSS_OP_KEY_REUSE: ...';
  END IF;
  IF FOUND THEN
    RETURN v_existing.result;
  END IF;

duplicate_quote body (20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql)
  IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL THEN
    v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key, 'duplicate_quote');
    IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN v_existing; END IF;
  END IF;

src/pages/Quotes.tsx:137 (the correct pattern, in the same file)
    convertQuoteIdem.resetKey();

**Failure scenario.** A rep clicks Duplicate on quote Q-1001. The RPC commits (Q-1099 created) but the response is lost to a dropped connection, so the client shows 'Failed to duplicate quote' and the key is NOT reset. The rep scrolls down and clicks Duplicate on a different quote, Q-1042. `duplicate_quote` sees the same key/operation, returns the cached `{quote_id: <Q-1099>, quote_number: 'Q-1099'}`, and the UI toasts 'Quote duplicated as Q-1099' and navigates there. Q-1042 was never duplicated; the rep believes it was, edits Q-1099 (a copy of Q-1001) and sends the wrong products and pricing to the customer.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on mechanism; severity corrected MED -> LOW. Every factual leg of the claim holds against current source, and I found no missed enforcement anywhere.

(1) Client key lifecycle is as described. `src/hooks/useIdempotencyKey.ts:28-38` scopes the ref to `JSON.stringify([operation, userId])` only — the target row is NOT part of the scope, so one hook instance yields one key for every row. `src/pages/Quotes.tsx:50` declares one page-level `duplicateQuoteIdem`; `handleDuplicate` (108-127) calls `getKey()` at 111 with no pre-reset, returns early at 117-120 on `error` without resetting, and the outer `catch` at 125-127 also does not reset. `resetKey()` at 121 runs only on success. The per-row button at 505 (`onClick={(e) => handleDuplicate(row.id, e)}`) feeds different `quoteId`s into that one key.

(2) The server side is genuinely key-only — this is "the DB does not enforce it", checked in the migrations, not "I couldn't find it". The newest `check_idempotency` is `supabase/migrations/20260714230000_gauntlet_core_guards.sql:5-51`; its lookup is `WHERE idempotency_key = p_key` with only an operation-mismatch raise, no argument/fingerprint comparison. I checked for a superseding definition: the only later files defining it are none — 20260210000000, 20260526201319 (grants only), 20260714220000, then 20260714230000 is last. `duplicate_quote`'s newest definition is `20260526151856_execute_full_codebase_ultra_review.sql:975-1096`; nothing after it redefines the function (grep over all migrations). Its guard at 1006-1010 is `v_existing := check_idempotency(p_idempotency_key,'duplicate_quote'); IF v_existing IS NOT NULL THEN RETURN v_existing; END IF;` — placed BEFORE `SELECT * INTO v_orig FROM quotes WHERE id = p_source_quote_id`, so `p_source_quote_id` is never consulted on replay. The receipt is written in-transaction via `save_idempotency(...)` at 1087, so a committed duplication always leaves a replayable receipt.

(3) I specifically hunted for the enforcement the reviewer might have missed and it does not cover this RPC. The payload-binding mechanism exists — `supabase/migrations/20260803010917_bind_idempotency_to_mutation_intent.sql` adds `idempotency_keys.request_fingerprint` / `request_actor_id` and `IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH` — but it wraps only `save_invoice` and `create_quick_delivery` (grep for IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH: only 20260803010917, 20260806004644, 20260806012423, 20260806023048, all invoice/bulk-import). `duplicate_quote` is listed in `src/lib/rpcIdempotencyScope.test.ts:116` under HELPER_SCOPED, i.e. operation-scoped only — the test contract itself asserts key+operation scoping and nothing more. The BEFORE INSERT trigger `guard_idempotency_key_insert` (20260714230000:65-110) only raises on cross-op reuse or concurrent insert; it never fires on the replay path (the replay returns before any insert).

(4) Not in the baseline. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` and `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md` contain no mention of `duplicate_quote` or a stale duplicate-key; section 04 line 22 only blesses the CONVERT caller. Notably `docs/archive/2026-summer-closeout/build-loops/ui-overhaul-v2/STATE.md:68` records that this exact class ("idempotency-key reuse across different targets on the 3 write buttons") was fixed by adding `resetKey()` on modal-open in Quotes/ReceivingHub/Deliveries — that pass fixed the convert button (`Quotes.tsx:137 convertQuoteIdem.resetKey()`) and left the Duplicate button unfixed, which strengthens rather than refutes the finding.

Two corrections to the finding as written:
- The rhetorical claim "Every sibling handler in this codebase resets per target and this one does not" is inaccurate. `src/pages/CustomerDetail.tsx:1417-1425` ("New from Last") has the identical unreset shape for the same RPC: `getKey()` at 1417, `if (error) { ...; return; }` at 1423, `resetKey()` only at 1425 after success. It is a second instance of the same defect, not a counterexample — but the claimed universal convention does not exist.
- Severity: the failure requires a committed-but-lost response (application errors roll back, so no receipt is left and a stale key is then harmless) AND a subsequent Duplicate on a different row in the same page mount. The blast radius is a silently skipped duplication, not corrupted data: no money, no inventory (the copy is inserted as `'draft'` and `is_planned` is not copied, so no holds are created), and the user is navigated to a quote page showing the other quote's number and customer name. That visible mismatch makes the "sends wrong pricing to the customer" narrative require the rep to ignore a different customer name on the landing page. Real, silent-success-on-a-skipped-write, but LOW rather than MED.

### [LOW] Voided orders still offer Edit Order, Create Invoice and Schedule Delivery — the UI gates test 'fulfilled'/'cancelled' but never 'voided'

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:149 (canEdit), 1168 (Create Invoice), 1209 (Schedule Delivery); .claude/schema-registry.json orders.status = ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided']; supabase/migrations/20260620130000_commission_batch_freeze_guard.sql (void_order: only fulfilled→voided)

**Detail.** `void_order` only runs on a `fulfilled` order and sets it to `voided`, so `voided` is a real, reachable terminal state on this page. Every action gate on the page tests `!== 'fulfilled'` and `!== 'cancelled'` but omits `voided`, so all three primary actions light up on a voided order.

The database does refuse each one — this is a UI-only leak, and I verified the refusals in the migrations rather than assuming them:
- `update_order_items` impl: `IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed','pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %'`
- `create_invoice_from_order`: `IF v_order_deleted_at IS NOT NULL OR v_order_status IN ('cancelled','voided') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_INVOICE_TERMINAL: ...'`
So the damage is wasted work and a raw, unexplained DB error rather than corruption. Note line 1164 in the same block DOES correctly test `!== 'voided'` for the booking-draw notice, which shows the omission is an oversight, not a policy.

**Evidence.** src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:149
  const canEdit = (role === 'admin' || role === 'sales_rep') && order?.status !== 'fulfilled' && order?.status !== 'cancelled' && order?.status !== 'partially_fulfilled' && !order?.booking_draw;

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1164 (the same block, done correctly)
              {order.booking_draw && (role === 'admin' || role === 'sales_rep') && order.status !== 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'voided' && (

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1168
              {order.status !== 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'fulfilled' && !hasActiveInvoice && !hasActiveDelivery && (<>

src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1209
              {order.status !== 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'fulfilled' && (<>

supabase/migrations/20260617123503_refresh_item_profit_on_sameproduct_order_edit.sql:71-72
  IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'pending') THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %', v_order.status;

supabase/migrations/20260721014858_20260721010000_govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:219-220
  IF v_order_deleted_at IS NOT NULL OR v_order_status IN ('cancelled', 'voided') THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_INVOICE_TERMINAL: cannot create an invoice for a cancelled, voided, or deleted order';

**Failure scenario.** An admin voids fulfilled order ORD-700 (inventory restored, commissions cancelled, draft invoices voided). Opening ORD-700 afterwards, the page still shows Edit Order, Create Invoice and Schedule Delivery. The admin clicks Edit Order, spends time correcting a line, and clicks Save — the RPC rejects with the raw text 'Cannot edit order in status: voided'. Clicking Schedule Delivery navigates to /deliveries/new?order=ORD-700, whose order dropdown is filtered to confirmed/partially_fulfilled, so the form loads the order's items but the save fails with 'Order not found'.

**Verifier verdict.** Could not refute — every cited fact holds against current source, and I found no missed enforcement mechanism. Verification: (1) `voided` is a real, reachable, non-deleted orders status — `.claude/schema-registry.json` `orders.status = ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided']`, and the newest `void_order` body (`supabase/migrations/20260620130000_commission_batch_freeze_guard.sql:389-415`: `IF v_order.status != 'fulfilled' THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'INVALID_ORDER_STATUS'` … `UPDATE public.orders SET status = 'voided'`) is still the live impl — `20260714222000_return_credit_source_of_truth.sql:304-311` only renames it to `_void_order_impl_20260714` behind a wrapper, and no later migration redefines it (`20260721014858` mentions `void_order` only inside an error string at line 1412). (2) The UI is reachable: `src/pages/Orders.tsx:659` offers a `<option value="voided">Voided</option>` filter, and `src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1347` renders the Void Order button on `fulfilled`, so an admin lands back on this page post-void. (3) No wrapper hides the action bar for voided — I read `src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1080-1230`; the only early return is `if (!order)`, and the action bar's sole condition is the `editing ? … : …` ternary. Line 149 `canEdit` and lines 1169/1210 are quoted exactly as claimed, and line 1164 does test `!== 'voided'`, confirming the omission is an oversight. (4) The DB backstops are real, at the current definitions: the live `update_order_items` impl is the `20260617123503` body renamed by `20260714221000_order_and_cycle_count_concurrency_guards.sql:4-11` (`IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed','pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot edit order in status: %'`), and `20260721014858_…_govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:219-220` raises `ORDER_INVOICE_TERMINAL` for `voided`. (5) The Schedule Delivery scenario reproduces exactly: `src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:118` filters the picker `.in('status', ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled'])` while `fetchOrderDetails` (line ~159) loads the order by id with no status filter, so items render and `submitDelivery` fails at `src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:402-403` with `toast('error','Order not found')`. (6) Not documented: no hit for `canEdit`/`Schedule Delivery`/voided-order gating in `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` or `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-…md` (its only OrderDetail reference is line 25, about split-billing RPC selection at OrderDetail.tsx:862-885). One narrowing the reviewer overstated: the Create Invoice gate also requires `!hasActiveInvoice && !hasActiveDelivery` (`src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:903-914`, where active = any delivery not cancelled/voided). A voided order reached via a completed delivery still has a `delivered` delivery row, so Create Invoice stays hidden on that common path; it only leaks on the narrower fulfilled-via-remainder-close path where deliveries were cancelled and the invoice voided. Edit Order (non-booking_draw) and Schedule Delivery leak unconditionally on any voided order. Severity corrected MED→LOW: this is cosmetic/UX only — the DB refuses all three actions, no money, inventory, or AR state can drift, the state is admin-only and rare, and the worst outcome is wasted keystrokes plus an unfriendly raw exception string.

### [LOW] NewDelivery deep-links load an order's items without re-checking its status, then fail at save with a bare 'Order not found'

- **Finder:** `frontend-compliance`
- **Location:** src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:116-119 (list query filters status), 156-172 (fetchOrderDetails — no status filter), 401-406 (save-time lookup against the filtered list)

**Detail.** `fetchOrders()` restricts the selectable order list to `['confirmed','partially_fulfilled']`, but `fetchOrderDetails(orderId)` — reached via the `?order=` query param that OrderDetail's Schedule Delivery button emits — loads the order and its items by id with no status filter. The form renders fully populated. At submit, `orders.find((o) => o.id === selectedOrderId)` searches the FILTERED list, misses, and aborts with 'Order not found'.

This fails closed (no bad delivery is created), so the impact is wasted work and a misleading message rather than data damage. It compounds with the voided-order finding above, which is what produces the deep link in the first place. The same `orders.find` also depends on the whole confirmed/partially_fulfilled set being returned by an unlimited PostgREST query, so a very large order set could reproduce the same 'Order not found' for a perfectly valid order.

**Evidence.** src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:116-119
      .from('orders')
      .select('*, customer:customers(farm_name)')
      .in('status', ['confirmed', 'partially_fulfilled'])
      .is('deleted_at', null);

src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:156-172
  const fetchOrderDetails = useCallback(async (orderId: string) => {
    setLoadingDetails(true);
    const { data: orderData, error: orderErr } = await supabase
      .from('orders')
      .select('*')
      .eq('id', orderId)
      .maybeSingle();
    ...
    if (!orderData) {
      setLoadingDetails(false);
      return;
    }

src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:401-406
    const order = orders.find((o) => o.id === selectedOrderId);
    if (!order) {
      toast('error', 'Order not found');
      setSaving(false);
      return;
    }

**Failure scenario.** From voided order ORD-700 (see the voided-gates finding) a user clicks Schedule Delivery and lands on /deliveries/new?order=<ORD-700>. The page loads ORD-700's customer, addresses and line items and pre-fills delivery quantities, so it looks like a normal new delivery. The user picks a driver and date, clicks Schedule Delivery, and gets 'Order not found' with no indication that the real reason is the order's voided status.

**Verifier verdict.** MECHANISM CONFIRMED, STATED FAILURE SCENARIO REFUTED. Severity stays LOW.

WHAT VERIFIED CORRECT (all citations exact):
- src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:116-119 — `.from('orders').select('*, customer:customers(farm_name)').in('status', ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled']).is('deleted_at', null)` (status filter on :118). No `.limit()`/`.range()` anywhere on that query.
- src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:156-172 — `fetchOrderDetails` is `.from('orders').select('*').eq('id', orderId).maybeSingle()` with NO status filter and NO deleted_at filter. Confirmed.
- src/pages/NewDelivery.tsx:401-403 — `const order = orders.find((o) => o.id === selectedOrderId); if (!order) { toast('error', 'Order not found'); }` searching the FILTERED list. Confirmed.
- Deep-link source exists: src/pages/OrderDetail.tsx:1209 gates the button on `order.status !== 'cancelled' && order.status !== 'fulfilled'`, so a `voided` order does render `navigate('/deliveries/new?order=' + order.id)` at :1213. orders.status enum is ['confirmed','partially_fulfilled','fulfilled','cancelled','voided'] (.claude/schema-registry.json /status_enums/orders.status), so `voided` slips the gate.

WHY THE NARRATED SCENARIO CANNOT HAPPEN (the load-bearing part of the finding is wrong):
A voided order can only have come from `fulfilled` — supabase/migrations/20260616142001_void_order_restore_logs_void_delivery_reversal.sql:55-57 `IF v_order.status != 'fulfilled' THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'INVALID_ORDER_STATUS: %'`. And `fulfilled` implies every order_item has quantity_remaining = 0: normal fulfillment only flips to fulfilled when no `quantity_remaining > 0` rows survive, and the only other route (cancel_order remainder-close, supabase/migrations/20260721014858_...govern_invoice_order_money_lifecycle.sql:1140 `quantity_remaining = 0,` → :1247-1248 `'mode','remainder_closed','status','fulfilled'`) explicitly zeroes it. OrderDetail.tsx:625-632 restricts manual transitions to `confirmed:['cancelled'], partially_fulfilled:['cancelled']`, so no manual jump to fulfilled with remaining stock.
Consequence: on a deep link from a voided order, NewDelivery.tsx:207-219 builds `drafts` from `items.filter((item) => item.quantity_remaining > 0)` → EMPTY. The form is NOT "fully populated with pre-filled delivery quantities", and handleSave aborts at NewDelivery.tsx:351-353 with 'Add at least one item with quantity > 0' — line 401 is never reached. The claimed compounding with the voided-order finding does not hold.

WHY IT IS STILL A REAL (LOW) DEFECT — a path the reviewer missed:
Soft-deleted orders reach the same dead end with items populated. Orders.tsx:426 soft-deletes: `supabase.from('orders').update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() }).in('id', ids)`. OrderDetail.tsx:156-159 loads the order with `.select('*').eq('id', id!).maybeSingle()` and NO deleted_at filter, so a soft-deleted `confirmed` order still renders and still shows Schedule Delivery (:1209 gate passes). NewDelivery's list query excludes it via `.is('deleted_at', null)` (:119), but fetchOrderDetails does not, quantity_remaining is still > 0, the form fills in, and save hits the bare 'Order not found'. That is the finding's mechanism, reached by a different door.

ENFORCEMENT CHECK (migrations read, not just "not found"):
The DB DOES enforce order status — supabase/migrations/20260518010000_create_delivery_with_items_validate_order_status_and_address.sql:104-107 `IF v_order.status NOT IN ('confirmed','partially_fulfilled') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'ORDER_NOT_SCHEDULABLE...'`, inside the `SELECT customer_id, status ... FOR UPDATE` at :92-95. I checked every later migration touching the function (20260517010000, 20260530121534, 20260614150249, 20260617013523, 20260620190000) — none redefine it, the last two only mention it in comments. So the status half fails closed twice over.
BUT the RPC does NOT check `deleted_at` on the order (verified by reading the full 20260518010000 body). That makes the frontend `orders.find` guard at :401 load-bearing for the soft-delete case — a "fix" that replaces it with a direct id lookup would open a real hole. This is worth flagging to whoever remediates.

SUB-CLAIM ON PAGINATION: unverified, not refuted. There is genuinely no `.limit()`/`.range()` on the fetchOrders query (I read it), so truncation would depend on the Supabase project's PostgREST max-rows setting, which I cannot check read-only from source. State it as "no client-side limit exists", not as "truncation occurs".

BASELINE: not in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md and not in docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-...md — that section's only NewDelivery mention (line 23) is a positive note that delivery creation uses the canonical RPC.

NET: keep the finding at LOW, but the failure_scenario must be rewritten from the voided-order story to the soft-deleted-order story, and the "compounds with the voided-order finding" sentence removed.

### [LOW] "Unused Prepay" is read from two different sources on two panels of the same page

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** src/pages/AccountsReceivable.tsx:52-54 vs supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:228 (`c.prepay_balance_cents`) rendered at src/pages/ARaging.tsx:264-270; also supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:407-410

**Detail.** The AccountsReceivable "Unused Prepay" tile sums the `prepay_credits` ledger. The AR Aging tab rendered directly beneath it (AccountsReceivable.tsx:137) shows a per-customer "Prepay on file" column sourced from the denormalized `customers.prepay_balance_cents` column returned by get_ar_aging, and FinancialDashboard's `total_prepay_unallocated` uses that same denormalized column. Those two are not guaranteed equal: the denormalized column is maintained by hand-rolled arithmetic in several RPCs and is clamped, e.g. `supabase/migrations/20260516060000_prepay_edit_delete_role_null_safe.sql:101` — `SET prepay_balance_cents = GREATEST(prepay_balance_cents + v_delta, 0)` and `:204` — `GREATEST(prepay_balance_cents - v_old_balance, 0)`. A clamp that fires silently discards the difference, permanently desynchronising the column from the ledger. Additionally the tile's read is unpaginated (`.select('balance_cents')` with no `.range()`), so it is subject to the same ~1000-row cap documented at src/pages/Products.tsx:104.

**Evidence.** src/pages/AccountsReceivable.tsx:52-54:
        const { data: prepayRows, error: prepayErr } = await supabase.from('prepay_credits').select('balance_cents');
        if (prepayErr) throw prepayErr;
        const prepaySum = (prepayRows || []).reduce((s, r) => s + Number(r.balance_cents || 0), 0) / 100;

supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:228: `    c.prepay_balance_cents,`

supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:407-410:
  prepay_bal AS (
    SELECT COALESCE(SUM(prepay_balance_cents), 0) / 100.0 AS total_unallocated
    FROM public.customers
    WHERE prepay_balance_cents > 0

**Failure scenario.** A prepay edit reduces a customer's ledger below zero and the `GREATEST(..., 0)` clamp absorbs $1,200. The AccountsReceivable header tile (ledger) and the AR Aging column and Financial Dashboard tile (customers column) now disagree by $1,200 on the same screen, with no error and nothing that reconciles them.

**Verifier verdict.** NOT REFUTED in substance — the dual-source claim is factually correct and is neither in docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md nor in the 2026-08-05 section-04 baseline (that file has zero "prepay" matches) — but three of the reviewer's supporting claims are wrong or imprecise, and the failure scenario does not hold as written, which is why I drop MED→LOW.

VERIFIED AS STATED:
1. src/pages/AccountsReceivable.tsx:52-54 does sum the ledger: `.from('prepay_credits').select('balance_cents')` … `reduce(...)/100`, rendered as the "Unused Prepay" tile at :98-100. The read has no `.range()` — genuinely unpaginated, and Products.tsx:104-106 confirms the project's own documented PostgREST 1000-row cap ("PostgREST caps a single response (default 1000), so we page through with .range()"). This sub-claim is real and latent.
2. get_ar_aging's current definition (supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:228 `c.prepay_balance_cents,` from `FROM public.customers c`) does return the denormalized column; ARaging.tsx:264-270 renders it as "Prepay on file"; AccountsReceivable.tsx:137 `{tab === 'aging' && <ARaging />}` puts it on the same page. Confirmed.
3. financial_dashboard_summary's current body is 20260714223000:407-410 (`SUM(prepay_balance_cents)` FROM customers WHERE > 0); the only later migration touching it, 20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql:110, is REVOKE-only and does not redefine the body. Confirmed.

REVIEWER ERRORS:
A. Stale citation. 20260516060000_prepay_edit_delete_role_null_safe.sql:101/:204 is SUPERSEDED — edit_prepay_credit and delete_prepay_credit were both re-created later by supabase/migrations/20260611211058_idempotency_operation_scope_sweep.sql:1647 and :1376. The clamps do survive verbatim there (`:1711 SET prepay_balance_cents = GREATEST(prepay_balance_cents + v_delta, 0)`, `:1431 SET prepay_balance_cents = GREATEST(prepay_balance_cents - v_old_balance, 0)`), so the substance stands, but the cited file is not current source.
B. "nothing that reconciles them" is FALSE. `reconcile_prepay_balances()` exists (supabase/migrations/20260305200000_audit_safety_fixes.sql:709-762): admin-gated, per-customer `SELECT COALESCE(SUM(pc.balance_cents),0) … WHERE pc.customer_id = … AND pc.balance_cents > 0` then `UPDATE customers SET prepay_balance_cents = v_actual`, with a financial_audit_log row. It is documented at docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:174 and characterized as "convergent repair sets balances to recomputed ledger truth" in src/lib/rpcContracts.test.ts:2259. The correct, narrower statement is: a converging reconciler EXISTS in the DB but has NO frontend caller — grep of src/ finds it only in the generated types (src/types/supabase.ts:13421) and tests — so it never runs from the app.
C. The failure scenario does not hold as an origin. `GREATEST(prepay_balance_cents + v_delta, 0)` can only absorb value when the cache is ALREADY below the amount being removed, so the clamp entrenches a pre-existing desync rather than creating one. I checked the write paths and found no current originating path: the creation path writes BOTH sides in one statement pair (20260516100000_create_prepay_check_splits_active_admin_check.sql:82 `INSERT INTO prepay_credits (` … :99 `SET prepay_balance_cents = COALESCE(prepay_balance_cents,0) + v_total_cents`); the apply-path double-decrement was removed by 20260622040000_apply_prepay_remove_double_decrement.sql; and bulk-apply, the one known ledger-bypassing path, is hard-disabled (`RAISE 'PREPAY_BULK_APPLY_DISABLED'`, 20260620200000, per docs/reference/gotchas.md:181). `increment_customer_prepay` (20260516040000) DOES bump the cache with no prepay_credits row, but it is REVOKEd from anon+authenticated and has zero callers in src/ or in any other migration body. So the desync is latent/historical, not demonstrably producible today — a "the DB does not enforce equality" finding, not a reproducible bug.

FINDING UNDERSTATED IN ONE RESPECT (worth carrying forward): the divergence is more visible than the reviewer said. The Prepayments tab of the SAME page also uses the cache — src/components/prepay/PrepaymentManagerPanel.tsx:124-125 `.select('id, farm_name, prepay_balance_cents').gt('prepay_balance_cents', 0)` and :458 `const totalPrepay = customers.reduce((s, c) => s + c.prepay_balance_cents, 0)` — so the header tile (ledger) and its own sibling tab total (cache) are directly comparable on one screen. And src/components/dashboard/FinanceSnapshotCard.tsx:64 renders an identically-labelled `{ label: 'Unused Prepay', value: formatUSD(prepay) … to: '/accounts-receivable?tab=prepayments' }` fed by financial_dashboard_summary (cache), deep-linking straight to the page whose header tile reads the ledger.

Net: real, undocumented, but display-integrity only, with no proven current desync origin, live prepay volume ~0 (docs/audits/overnight-bug-hunt/LEDGER.json:410 "Dormant on live (0 prepay rows)"), and an existing (if unwired) converging reconciler. LOW.
