### [LOW] The point-of-sale credit-limit guardrail and the Financial Dashboard's "customers over credit" count use different invoice sets

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** src/hooks/useGuardrails.ts:44-53 vs supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:378-389

**Detail.** The guardrail that warns a user at quote/order time includes `draft` and `unposted` invoices in the customer's current balance, does not filter `deleted_at IS NULL`, and sums raw `balance_cents` so a credit memo's negative balance offsets. The dashboard counter uses `status IN ('posted','overdue')`, `deleted_at IS NULL`, and `GREATEST(balance_cents, 0)` so credits do not offset. Nothing reconciles them and neither is documented as the canonical credit-exposure definition.

**Evidence.** src/hooks/useGuardrails.ts:44-53:
      const { data: invoices } = await supabase
        .from('invoices')
        .select('balance_cents')
        .eq('customer_id', customerId)
        .in('status', ['draft', 'unposted', 'posted', 'overdue']);

      const currentBalance = (invoices || []).reduce(
        (sum, inv) => sum + (inv.balance_cents || 0),
        0
      );

supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:378-389:
  over_credit AS (
    SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt
    FROM public.customers c
    WHERE c.credit_limit_cents IS NOT NULL
      AND c.credit_limit_cents > 0
      AND (
        SELECT COALESCE(SUM(GREATEST(i.balance_cents, 0)), 0)
        FROM public.invoices i
        WHERE i.customer_id = c.id
          AND i.status IN ('posted', 'overdue')
          AND i.deleted_at IS NULL
      ) > c.credit_limit_cents

**Failure scenario.** A customer with a $50,000 limit has $45,000 posted plus $20,000 sitting in draft invoices and one soft-deleted $10,000 draft. The guardrail blocks the next order claiming they are over limit (it counts $75,000, including the soft-deleted row), while the Financial Dashboard's over-credit tile does not list them at all ($45,000 posted). The rep is stopped by a number no report will confirm.

**Verifier verdict.** PARTIALLY CONFIRMED — the code divergence is real and verified, but the finding's framing is factually wrong on two points and the impact is smaller than MED.

VERIFIED AS QUOTED. src/hooks/useGuardrails.ts:42-53 matches the evidence verbatim: `.in('status', ['draft', 'unposted', 'posted', 'overdue'])` with no `.is('deleted_at', null)` and `sum + (inv.balance_cents || 0)` with no zero-clamp. supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:378-389 matches verbatim (`status IN ('posted','overdue')`, `deleted_at IS NULL`, `GREATEST(i.balance_cents, 0)`). No later migration supersedes it: the only files touching `over_credit`/`financial_dashboard_summary` after it are 20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql (grants only). Soft-deleted rows ARE visible to the hook — `invoices_select` (20260213100000_phase2_billing_architecture.sql:255-262) is `USING (is_admin() OR created_by = auth.uid() OR salesman_id = auth.uid())` with no `deleted_at` predicate, and it is never redefined. `draft` and `unposted` are both live invoice statuses (.claude/schema-registry.json `/check_constraints/invoices.status` = ['draft','unposted','posted','paid','overdue','voided','cancelled']). `balance_cents` is `bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS (total_amount_cents - paid_amount_cents - prepay_applied_cents) STORED` (20260213100000:58), so it can go negative and does offset in the unclamped hook sum. Not in KNOWN_ISSUES.md (only #25 fail-closed and #105 spray-job exposure, both different) and the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet file contains zero occurrences of "credit".

REVIEWER ERROR 1 — a canonical definition exists and was missed. `public.check_customer_credit_limit(p_customer_id uuid)` is the server-side authority: supabase/migrations/20260712130000_credit_limit_count_unposted.sql:31-35 uses `COALESCE(SUM(GREATEST(balance_cents, 0)), 0) ... WHERE status IN ('posted','overdue','unposted') AND deleted_at IS NULL`, and it is documented at docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:226. The `unposted` inclusion is a deliberate, documented fix (KNOWN_ISSUES.md:1070, finding #105, applied live 2026-07-12), so RPC-vs-dashboard is an intentional one-status delta, not drift. "Nothing reconciles them and neither is documented as the canonical credit-exposure definition" is false.

REVIEWER ERROR 2 — wrong location for "the point-of-sale guardrail". The quote→order point-of-sale checks call the canonical RPC, not the hook: src/pages/Quotes.tsx:219, src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:2579, src/pages/NewOrder.tsx:550, src/lib/creditLimit.ts:18, and QuickDeliveryModal.tsx:414 explicitly declines to re-run it. The hook is used only at src/pages/NewOrder.tsx:458, src/pages/InvoiceDetail.tsx:819, src/pages/FieldAppSplitInvoiceEditor.tsx:725.

WHAT SURVIVES. useGuardrails.ts:44-53 is a THIRD, client-side credit-exposure definition that diverges from the canonical RPC — the correct comparison the reviewer should have drawn. NewOrder.tsx shows both inside one flow: the loose hook gates submission at :458-459 (`if (!creditOk && !creditWarning?.dismissed) return;`) while the canonical RPC produces the post-create toast at :550-557, so the two numbers on the same page can disagree.

WHY LOW, NOT MED. (a) The hook is advisory, not a hard block — `!creditWarning?.dismissed` means one click on the GuardrailBanner and the same submit proceeds; the authoritative RPC-based warnings are explicitly "warn (not block)" (NewOrder.tsx:547 comment). (b) The failure scenario's "no report will confirm it" premise is weaker than stated: the canonical RPC (which the quote/order paths and CustomerDetail AR use) is the reconciling number, and it does exist. (c) A further real defect the reviewer did not spot cuts the other way — under `invoices_select`, a non-admin rep's hook query only returns invoices they created or are salesman on, so for reps the hook systematically UNDER-counts exposure rather than over-blocking; the RPC is SECURITY DEFINER and immune. That makes the hook's number unreliable in both directions, but it is duplicated advisory UI logic layered over a correct server check, which is a LOW-severity cleanup (delete the hook's direct query and call `check_customer_credit_limit`), not a MED money defect.

### [LOW] Quote soft-delete writes no activity_feed record — the only quote lifecycle event with no audit trail

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:406-412 (zero logActivity calls in the whole file); contrast docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:50 and :252

**Detail.** Audited logActivity coverage across the cycle: quote save/send/accept/decline are logged in-transaction by `save_quote` (acknowledged at src/pages/QuoteBuilder.tsx:1541-1542), conversion by `convert_quote_to_order` (2 activity_feed inserts in 20260730235031), the two terminal closures by `close_quote_as_applied`/`close_quote_as_short` (5 activity_feed refs each), payment by `allocate_payment`, invoice post by the `post_invoice` impl chain (20260702160000 body contains `INSERT INTO activity_feed`), unpost by `unpost_invoice`, and order/delivery transitions client-side at OrderDetail.tsx:643 and DeliveryDetail.tsx:904. The one uncovered mutation is quote deletion: `Quotes.tsx` performs a direct table UPDATE setting `deleted_at`, calls no RPC, and the file imports no activity logger at all. Deleting a booking — which by the finding above can also strand inventory — leaves no record of who did it or when.

**Evidence.** src/pages/Quotes.tsx:406-412:
      const result = await supabase
        .from('quotes')
        .update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() })
        .in('id', ids)
        .select();
      checkMutationResult(result, 'Delete quotes');
      toast('success', `Deleted ${ids.length} quote(s)`);

`grep -c logActivity src/pages/Quotes.tsx` → 0

docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:252: `- [ ] Call \`logActivity()\` for status transitions`

**Failure scenario.** Five planned bookings vanish from the Quotes list mid-season. There is no activity_feed row, no financial_audit_log row, and no RPC receipt — the only evidence is the `deleted_at` timestamp on the rows themselves, with no actor. Nobody can determine who deleted them or why.

**Verifier verdict.** CORE CLAIM CONFIRMED, but the framing and half the supporting evidence do not hold up, so severity drops MED -> LOW.

WHAT I INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED (all four legs hold):
1. src/pages/Quotes.tsx:406-412 does perform a direct table soft-delete with no RPC: `.from('quotes').update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() }).in('id', ids).select()` followed by `checkMutationResult(result, 'Delete quotes')`. `grep -c logActivity src/pages/Quotes.tsx` -> 0, reproduced.
2. THE DB DOES NOT ENFORCE IT (checked the migrations, not merely "I didn't find it"). I enumerated every trigger on `public.quotes` across all migrations: `enforce_quote_status_transition` (20260228300000:463), `trg_release_holds_on_quote_status` (20260312200000:64), `enforce_quote_accepted_fully_drawn` (20260610181612:293), `trg_enforce_quote_terminal_not_drawn` (20260614151956:51), `trg_validate_quote_commission_split` (20260719064000:142), `quotes_bump_row_version` (20260730235031:64), `trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids` (20260722174029:626). Exactly one writes activity_feed — `release_holds_on_quote_status_change()` — and it is bound `AFTER UPDATE OF status ON quotes` (20260312200000:65-67) with body guard `IF NEW.status IN ('accepted','declined','expired') AND OLD.status NOT IN (...)`. A `deleted_at`-only UPDATE changes no status, so that trigger never fires. No later migration supersedes it with an audit trigger.
3. There is no `deleted_by` column to fall back on. `.claude/schema-registry.json` `columns.quotes` = [... "created_at","updated_at","sent_at","is_planned","season","salesman_id","deleted_at","pdf_template_id","pdf_columns_override","row_version"] — `deleted_at` present, `deleted_by` absent. Contrast src/components/customers/CustomerDocuments.tsx:282 which DOES write `{ deleted_at: ..., deleted_by: deletedBy }`, proving the pattern exists elsewhere in the codebase.
4. Not in either baseline. docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04 covers only the Bulk Order Import HIGH; its sole `activity_feed` mention (line 37) is about `bulk_import_order`. KNOWN_ISSUES.md has only a generic parked LOW bucket ("audit-log completeness gaps", line 935) whose built predicate (line 1032, `predicates/audit-log-completeness.sql`) asserts coverage over "39 money-mutating SECDEF functions" — a direct table write from the Quotes list page is out of that predicate's scope.

WHERE THE FINDING IS WRONG (basis for the downgrade):
a. The title's "the only quote lifecycle event with no audit trail" implies a quote-specific defect. It is the project-wide convention for list-page bulk soft-deletes. Identical unlogged direct `deleted_at` updates: src/pages/Orders.tsx:426 (`grep -c logActivity src/pages/Orders.tsx` -> 0 as well), src/pages/Jobs.tsx:1367, src/pages/BlendTickets.tsx:250, src/pages/BlendRecipes.tsx:296, src/pages/Returns.tsx:599, src/pages/TeamBoard.tsx:455. This is a systemic convention gap, not a quote regression.
b. The cited "contrast" docs do not establish a violated requirement. QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:50 reads "Always call `logActivity()` after send/accept/decline events" and :252 reads "- [ ] Call `logActivity()` for status transitions". A soft-delete is neither — and the code's own comment at Quotes.tsx:378 states "deletion changes no status, so the hold-release trigger never fires". The reviewer's own quoted doc lines therefore do not cover delete.
c. "no financial_audit_log row" is not evidence of anything. Quote deletion moves no money; financial_audit_log is the wrong ledger to expect a row in.
d. The failure scenario overstates "no actor". RLS policy `quotes_update` (supabase/migrations/20260206174345_fix_security_and_performance_issues.sql:369-377) restricts the UPDATE to `is_admin() OR (is_sales_rep() AND created_by = (select auth.uid()))`, with no later policy superseding it. So a non-admin deleter is provably the row's own `created_by`; only an admin deleting another rep's quote is genuinely untraceable. The actor set is bounded, not unknown.
e. Impact is reversible: soft-delete leaves the row and all children intact with `deleted_at` clearable, and `quotes_bump_row_version` (20260730235031:64-67) increments `row_version` on the write, so the mutation is at least detectable. This is an observability gap, not data loss.

VERDICT: real, worth an activity_feed insert or a `deleted_by` column, but it is a reversible, RLS-bounded, no-money observability gap that follows six other pages' existing convention and violates no documented rule. LOW, and properly filed as the systemic "bulk soft-deletes are unaudited" item rather than a quote-lifecycle finding.

### [LOW] docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md documents 7 of the 9 live quote statuses and omits every quote lifecycle RPC added since mid-2026

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:29-38 (status list), :40-42 (Key RPC list)

**Detail.** The canonical workflow reference — the file AGENTS.md step 4 tells every agent to read before touching this area — lists `draft -> sent -> revised -> accepted -> declined -> expired -> cancelled`. Live is nine values including `closed_by_application` and `closed_short` (.claude/schema-registry.json). Both are implemented end to end in the frontend (src/types/index.ts:669, Badge.tsx:45-46, Quotes.tsx:448/:655-656, QuoteBuilder.tsx:2806, CustomerDetail.tsx:1475) and the doc never mentions them. The same section's "Key RPC" list names only `save_quote()` and `duplicate_quote()`, omitting `convert_quote_to_order` context aside: `revert_quote_status` (the reopen path, QuoteBuilder.tsx:1873), `draw_down_quote` (partial booking draw), `close_quote_as_applied`, `close_quote_as_short`, `create_quote_version`, `restore_quote_version`, and `create_job_from_quote_section`. Nothing guards this: `npm run check:docs` (scripts/check-doc-drift.mjs, run this session — all PASS) verifies migration-history sequence, page/route counts, hook counts, and manual-doc freshness stamps only. It does not read workflow docs or compare documented status lists against the schema registry, even though the registry is right there and machine-readable. Prior art that this class recurs: docs/audits/2026-07-14-workflow-review.md:42 — "The quote lifecycle diagram omitted two valid terminal states" — was fixed for the SVG generator and never for this doc.

**Evidence.** docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:29-32:
### Status transitions
```
draft -> sent -> revised -> accepted -> declined -> expired -> cancelled
```

docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:40-42:
### Key RPC
- `save_quote()` — atomic save of header + sections + items in one transaction
- `duplicate_quote()` — clones a quote for reuse

.claude/schema-registry.json: quotes.status = [... ,'closed_by_application','closed_short']

`npm run check:docs` output: PASS migration-history sequence / all migrations indexed / pages-routes / wired hooks / manual doc stamps — no workflow-doc or status-list check present.

**Failure scenario.** An agent asked to add a new consumer of quote status reads QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md, writes `status NOT IN ('declined','expired','cancelled')`, and ships the exact defect already live in get_program_completion. The doc-drift gate passes because it never looks at this file.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED on the facts, but severity corrected MED -> LOW.

Verified independently:
1. Status-list drift is real. `docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:29-32` reads exactly `draft -> sent -> revised -> accepted -> declined -> expired -> cancelled` (7 values). `.claude/schema-registry.json` `/status_enums/quotes.status` and `/check_constraints/quotes.status/values` both = `['draft','sent','revised','accepted','declined','expired','cancelled','closed_by_application','closed_short']` (9 values). This is DB-enforced, not just registry prose: `supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql` drops and re-adds `quotes_status_check` with the 9-value ARRAY. `grep -n "closed_by_application|closed_short" docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md` returns ZERO hits across all 326 lines — the doc never mentions them anywhere, so this is not a "listed later in the file" false positive.
2. Frontend implements both end to end, as claimed: `src/types/index.ts:669` (QuoteStatus union), `src/components/ui/Badge.tsx:45-46`, `src/pages/Quotes.tsx:448,655-656`, `QuoteBuilder.tsx:481,1782,1828,2806`, `CustomerDetail.tsx:1475`.
3. No guard exists. I read `scripts/check-doc-drift.mjs` in full (105 lines of checks): it verifies migration-history sequence, all-migrations-indexed, pages/routes counts, wired-hooks-documented, manual-doc "Last verified" stamps, and manual freshness vs newest migration. It never opens anything under `docs/workflows/` and never reads `.claude/schema-registry.json`. Reviewer's characterization of the gate is accurate.
4. Not already known. `grep` for QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY / closed_by_application / closed_short returns nothing in `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, and nothing relevant in the 2026-08-05 section-04 gauntlet baseline (only match is an unrelated line 72 about the SQL validator). Prior gauntlets list the doc as *read* (2026-07-22:7, 2026-07-26:48) but never flagged it as drifted.
5. Doc is actively maintained yet stale on this point: `git log -1 -- docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md` = 2026-07-30 (#290), well after the 2026-07-03 and 2026-07-06 migrations that added the statuses.

Two parts of the finding do NOT hold up, which is why severity drops:

A. The failure scenario's prior-art anchor is wrong. The reviewer says an agent would "ship the exact defect already live in get_program_completion." `supabase/migrations/20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql:50-65` explicitly lists `get_program_completion` under "Deliberately NOT touched ... each auto-handles the new terminal status correctly": "get_program_completion (excludes only declined/expired/cancelled -> a short-closed program keeps its partial-applied amounts visible, same as closed_by_application)." That is a recorded, reasoned design decision, not a live defect. The finding's claimed live-bug precedent evaporates, leaving a hypothetical.

B. The "omits every quote lifecycle RPC" half is largely mitigated. `docs/reference/rpc-functions.md` — the canonical RPC surface — documents `create_quote_version` (:78), `restore_quote_version` (:79), `revert_quote_status` (:83), `draw_down_quote` (:87), and `create_job_from_quote_section` (:452). The workflow doc's "Key RPC" line is a highlights list, not the canonical surface, and it does already name `convert_quote_to_order()`, `create_direct_order()`, `cancel_order()`, `update_order_items()` in Stage 2. Only `close_quote_as_applied` / `close_quote_as_short` appear undocumented in rpc-functions.md (grep for `close_quote_as` there returned nothing, while the definitions exist in `20260703200000_layer2_close_quote_as_applied.sql` and `20260706030000_closed_short_booking_closure.sql`) — a genuine but narrow gap.

Severity anchor: the directly analogous finding — `docs/audits/2026-07-14-workflow-review.md`, "The quote lifecycle diagram omitted two valid terminal states ... Runtime behavior was correct; only the review artifact was stale" — was adjudicated LOW in that same audit's LOW section, not MED. This finding is the same class (doc/artifact stale, runtime correct, zero money/RLS/data exposure) and should carry the same LOW rating for consistency with settled precedent.

### [LOW] QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md names a prepay_credits column that does not exist

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:199

**Detail.** The Stage 5 table inventory says `prepay_credits` — prepayment credits (remaining_cents). The live column is `balance_cents`; there is no `remaining_cents` on that table. Registry columns for prepay_credits: id, customer_id, season, original_amount_cents, balance_cents, payment_method, reference_number, notes, created_by, created_at, updated_at, source_type, source_reference, bucket_label, quote_id, allocation_set_id. Every real caller uses `balance_cents` (src/pages/AccountsReceivable.tsx:52, src/components/prepay/PrepaymentManagerPanel.tsx:124-125). Same doc, same paragraph as the correct and carefully-maintained note at :166 about `extended_cents` vs `line_total_cents`, so the file is selectively maintained.

**Evidence.** docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:199: `- \`prepay_credits\` — prepayment credits (remaining_cents)`

.claude/schema-registry.json columns.prepay_credits: ['id','customer_id','season','original_amount_cents','balance_cents','payment_method','reference_number','notes','created_by','created_at','updated_at','source_type','source_reference','bucket_label','quote_id','allocation_set_id']

**Failure scenario.** An agent writing a prepay report from the doc emits `.select('remaining_cents')`, gets a PostgREST 42703 at runtime, and the panel renders empty rather than failing loudly.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED (LOW, documentation-only). I verified every element of the claim against current source and could not find any mechanism that rescues it.

1. The doc text is exactly as quoted. `docs/workflows/QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md:199` (Stage 5 "Tables involved") reads verbatim: `- \`prepay_credits\` — prepayment credits (remaining_cents)`.

2. The column does not exist. `.claude/schema-registry.json` `columns.prepay_credits` = ["id","customer_id","season","original_amount_cents","balance_cents","payment_method","reference_number","notes","created_by","created_at","updated_at","source_type","source_reference","bucket_label","quote_id","allocation_set_id"] — no `remaining_cents`. This is a "the schema does not have it" statement, not a "I could not find it": I also searched every migration under supabase/migrations/ for `remaining_cents`; no migration ever adds a `remaining_cents` column to `prepay_credits`. The only migration-level hits are (a) local PL/pgSQL variables and JSON keys in the booking/rollover settlement functions (`20260614151115_prepay_booking_link_and_settlement.sql:62,154,216`; `20260614151613_open_booking_rollover.sql:104-148`; `20260702180000_layer2_rollover_settlement_job_aware.sql:99-301`) — those are computed booking-remainder values, unrelated to the prepay_credits table — and (b) a historical bug: `supabase/migrations/20260302200000_business_logic_enhancements.sql:486` wrote `remaining_cents = remaining_cents + v_prepay_app.amount_cents` against prepay_credits, which was explicitly corrected by the later migration `supabase/migrations/20260305200000_audit_safety_fixes.sql:64` ("F3: remaining_cents → balance_cents on prepay_credits") and :137 ("-- F3 FIX: Column is balance_cents, not remaining_cents"). So the doc line preserves a name that was proven wrong in SQL on 2026-03-05 and never existed in the live table.

3. No later migration supersedes this in the doc's favor — the timestamp-ordered chain moves in the opposite direction (wrong name fixed, never reintroduced).

4. Real callers use `balance_cents`, confirming the finding's substance: `src/pages/AccountsReceivable.tsx:52` `.from('prepay_credits').select('balance_cents')` and `src/components/prepay/PrepaymentManagerPanel.tsx:168-169` `.from('prepay_credits').select('id, customer_id, original_amount_cents, balance_cents, ...')`. One citation slip in the finding: the quoted `PrepaymentManagerPanel.tsx:124-125` is actually `customers.prepay_balance_cents`, a different table/column; the correct prepay_credits evidence in that file is at :168-169. This weakens a citation, not the claim.

5. Not already baselined. `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` has no hit for `remaining_cents`, `QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY`, or this doc-drift item. `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-quote-order-delivery-invoice-payment-lifecycle-refresh.md` has zero hits for `remaining_cents`, `prepay_credits`, or `QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY`. I also checked the dedicated `docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-07-28-section-15-documentation-drift-refresh.md` — also zero hits. So this is genuinely beyond the baseline.

Severity holds at LOW: the failure mode is an agent copying the name into a `.select('remaining_cents')` and getting a PostgREST 42703 — an immediate, loud, non-silent failure at development time, with no live-data or money-correctness consequence. It is a one-line doc correction (`remaining_cents` → `balance_cents`) in the same paragraph that already carries the carefully-maintained `extended_cents` vs `line_total_cents` note at :166.

### [LOW] financial_dashboard_summary quote counts include soft-deleted quotes and the pipeline value silently drops every 'revised' quote

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:336-342

**Detail.** The `quote_agg` CTE has no `deleted_at IS NULL` predicate, while the Quotes list page filters it (src/pages/Quotes.tsx:150, :257) and quotes are soft-deleted at :409 — so deleted quotes inflate the dashboard's draft/sent/accepted counts and pipeline value permanently. Separately, `pipeline_value` filters `status IN ('draft','sent')` and `sent_count` counts only `'sent'`, excluding `'revised'`. A revised quote is a live, convertible opportunity everywhere else in the app: QuoteBuilder.tsx:455 `canConvert = ['sent','revised','accepted']`, :456-457 `canDraw = currentStatus === 'sent' || currentStatus === 'revised'`, and src/lib/openBookings.ts:21 `.in('status', ['sent','revised'])`. Note the sibling CTEs in the same file do filter deleted rows (`orders ... WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` at :333), so this is an omission, not a house convention.

**Evidence.** supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:336-342:
  quote_agg AS (
    SELECT
      COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'draft') AS draft_count,
      COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'sent') AS sent_count,
      COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'accepted') AS accepted_count,
      COALESCE(SUM(total_price) FILTER (WHERE status IN ('draft', 'sent')), 0) AS pipeline_value
    FROM public.quotes
  ),

src/lib/openBookings.ts:21: `      .in('status', ['sent', 'revised'])`

**Failure scenario.** Every quote the office edits after sending flips to `revised` and drops out of the pipeline number on the executive dashboard. The most actively worked opportunities are precisely the ones excluded, so the pipeline tile trends down during the busiest week of the season.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED — every load-bearing claim held up against current source.

1) Cited SQL is accurate and is the LIVE definition. supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:335-342 reads exactly as quoted: `quote_agg AS ( SELECT COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'draft') ... COALESCE(SUM(total_price) FILTER (WHERE status IN ('draft', 'sent')), 0) AS pipeline_value FROM public.quotes ),` with no `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. Supersession check: only 8 migrations mention `financial_dashboard_summary`; sorted by timestamp the last one that redefines the body is 20260714223000. The only later file, 20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql, touches it solely at :110 `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.financial_dashboard_summary() FROM PUBLIC, anon;` plus an assertion list at :160 — it does not replace the body. No trigger/view can compensate since this is a read-side aggregate: the DB does not filter deleted quotes here (verified in the migrations, not merely "did not find").

2) Same-file sibling contrast is real. :328-333 `FROM public.orders WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND status NOT IN ('cancelled','voided','draft')`; the ar / over_credit / ar_aging CTEs at :373-404 all carry `deleted_at IS NULL`. quote_agg is the only CTE reading a soft-deletable table without the predicate.

3) Soft-delete is real and permanent. src/pages/Quotes.tsx bulk delete does `.from('quotes').update({ deleted_at: new Date().toISOString() }).in('id', ids)`, and the list query does `.from('quotes').select(...).is('deleted_at', null)`. I searched for any hard-delete path (`from('quotes')...delete()` across src/, and `DELETE FROM quotes` across supabase/migrations/) — zero hits. Deleted quote rows persist forever and are counted forever.

4) The 'revised' exclusion is real and reachable. quotes.status in .claude/schema-registry.json is ["draft","sent","revised","accepted","declined","expired","cancelled","closed_by_application","closed_short"]; QuoteBuilder.tsx:1688 `const savedId = await saveQuote('revised')` is the ordinary edit-after-send path, and :455-457 (`canConvert = ['sent','revised','accepted']`, `canDraw = currentStatus === 'sent' || currentStatus === 'revised'`) plus src/lib/openBookings.ts:21 `.in('status', ['sent','revised'])` confirm revised is a live convertible booking that `status IN ('draft','sent')` drops.

5) It reaches a real UI. jsonb_build_object at :541-548 emits `quote_counts` {draft,sent,accepted} and `quote_pipeline_value`; src/pages/FinancialDashboard.tsx:194-195 consumes both and renders them at :332 (pipeline tile) and :334-336 (draft/sent/accepted badges).

6) Not baseline. docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-quote-order-delivery-invoice-payment-lifecycle-refresh.md (101 lines, read in full) is entirely about Bulk Order Import and never mentions the dashboard, pipeline value, or revised quotes. docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md has no entry for financial_dashboard_summary quote aggregation (its "pipeline" hit at :1004 is an unrelated pre-quote sales-pipeline feature note).

One correction to the reviewer's framing, not enough to refute: the identical draft/sent-only, unfiltered-quotes pattern also appears in the sibling operational dashboard — supabase/migrations/20260323100000_operational_dashboard_summary.sql:43-48 `open_quotes AS (SELECT COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'draft') AS draft_cnt, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE status = 'sent') AS sent_cnt FROM quotes)`, again at :146-151, and at :216-222. So this is a repeated pattern across both dashboard RPCs rather than a one-off slip in one CTE. That widens the defect but weakens the reviewer's rhetorical point that sibling CTEs establish a quote-filtering convention.

Severity LOW is correct and should not be raised: admin-gated (`IF NOT public.is_admin() THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'permission denied: admin role required'`), read-only display aggregate, no money movement, nothing stored, no invariant breached. The failure mode is a misleading executive tile, exactly as described.

### [LOW] AR aging bucket boundaries disagree between the AR Aging report and the Financial Dashboard, and the note saying so lives only in a migration comment

- **Finder:** `reporting-drift`
- **Location:** supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:210-214 vs supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:391-396; acknowledged at supabase/migrations/20260702161000_a8_aging_basis_unification.sql:26-27

**Detail.** get_ar_aging buckets are `<= 29` / `30-59` / `60-89` / `90-119` / `>= 120` (rendered as Current / 30-59 / 60-89 / 90-119 / 120+ at src/pages/ARaging.tsx:231-260). financial_dashboard_summary buckets are `<= 30` / `31-60` / `61-90` / `> 90`. The A8 unification migration explicitly chose not to reconcile them — "The pre-existing bucket-BOUNDARY label differences ... are NOT changed here" — so the divergence is deliberate. Reporting it because the decision is recorded nowhere a reader of either report will find it: not in QUOTE_TO_DELIVERY.md, not in docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:205, not in docs/manual/DECISION_LOG.md, and not on either screen. An invoice exactly 30 days past due appears in "Current" on one page and "30-59 Days" on the other.

**Evidence.** supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:210-211:
    (COALESCE(sum(CASE WHEN p_as_of_date - ei.aging_date <= 29 THEN ei.statement_balance_cents ELSE 0 END), 0)::numeric / 100.0),
    (COALESCE(sum(CASE WHEN p_as_of_date - ei.aging_date BETWEEN 30 AND 59 THEN ei.statement_balance_cents ELSE 0 END), 0)::numeric / 100.0),

supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:392-393:
      COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN age_days <= 30 THEN balance ELSE 0 END), 0) AS current_bucket,
      COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN age_days BETWEEN 31 AND 60 THEN balance ELSE 0 END), 0) AS days_31_60,

supabase/migrations/20260702161000_a8_aging_basis_unification.sql:26-27:
--   The pre-existing bucket-BOUNDARY label differences (get_ar_aging Current<=29 vs statement
--   Current<=30; the dashboard's coarser 4-bucket JSON shape read by the frontend) are NOT

**Failure scenario.** Mason compares the dashboard's "Current" AR against the AR Aging report's "Current" column on the same day and they differ by the value of every invoice sitting exactly 30 days past due. With no on-screen note he treats the difference as a data bug and asks for an investigation that ends in a migration comment written five weeks earlier.

**Verifier verdict.** CONFIRMED. Both bucket definitions verified verbatim in current source and neither is superseded. get_ar_aging: supabase/migrations/20260805151605_fix_historical_ar_report_cutoffs.sql:213-214 uses `WHEN p_as_of_date - ei.aging_date <= 29` for Current and `BETWEEN 30 AND 59` for the next bucket, on basis `COALESCE(i.due_date, i.invoice_date)::date AS aging_date` (line 161). This file is the latest of the 11 migrations matching get_ar_aging, so it is the live definition. financial_dashboard_summary: supabase/migrations/20260714223000_remaining_money_inventory_hardening.sql:392-393 uses `WHEN age_days <= 30` for current_bucket and `BETWEEN 31 AND 60`, on basis `EXTRACT(DAY FROM NOW() - COALESCE(i.due_date, i.invoice_date))::int`. I checked for a superseding definition: the only later migration touching financial_dashboard_summary is 20260728231350_revoke_anon_execute_non_policy_functions.sql, which contains only `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.financial_dashboard_summary() FROM PUBLIC, anon;` (line 110) plus a caller-analysis comment (line 43) and a grant assertion (line 160) — it does not touch the body. So no later migration reconciles the boundaries; this is a DB-level fact from the migrations, not a "could not find enforcement" inference. The failure scenario is reachable: an invoice exactly 30 days past its due date yields `p_as_of_date - aging_date = 30`, which fails `<= 29` and lands in the AR Aging report's second column, while the dashboard's EXTRACT(DAY FROM interval) day component is also 30, which satisfies `age_days <= 30` and lands in current_bucket. Both surfaces render a bucket labeled exactly "Current": src/pages/ARaging.tsx:212-215 (`key: 'current_amount', header: 'Current'`) and src/pages/FinancialDashboard.tsx:267 (`{ label: 'Current', value: data.arAgingBuckets.current, ... }`). Documentation gap confirmed: grep of docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md returns no hit for financial_dashboard_summary or an aging-bucket discrepancy, and docs/audits/gauntlet/2026-08-05-section-04-quote-order-delivery-invoice-payment-lifecycle-refresh.md returns zero hits for "aging" or "bucket", so this is outside both baselines. docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:205 describes get_ar_aging only as "current/30/60/90/120+ day buckets" without stating the <=29 edge, and :252 describes financial_dashboard_summary only as "AR aging buckets" with no boundaries at all. Two corrections to the reviewer's text, neither of which refutes: (1) the AR Aging column headers in src/pages/ARaging.tsx are "Current" (:213), "30 Days" (:219), "60 Days" (:231), "90-119 Days" (:239), "120+ Days" (:251) — not "30-59"/"60-89" as the finding states; the vaguer real headers make the divergence harder to notice, not easier, and the reviewer's cited line range 231-260 covers only the 60/90/120 columns, with Current at 211-215. (2) There is a third producer, get_detailed_statement_data, which per docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:209 uses "current(0-30)", matching the dashboard — so get_ar_aging is the lone outlier of three aging producers rather than one half of a two-way split, which slightly strengthens the case. Severity LOW is correct and should not be raised: no money is miscomputed, no balance is wrong, nothing is lost or double-counted; the impact is a reconciliation/reader-confusion cost on two admin reporting screens plus an undocumented deliberate decision that currently lives only in the header comment at supabase/migrations/20260702161000_a8_aging_basis_unification.sql:26-30 ("The pre-existing bucket-BOUNDARY label differences ... are NOT renumbered ... Both are called out for a possible later pass"). Practically this is a documentation/label finding, and the cheapest correct fix is a DECISION_LOG entry plus explicit boundary text in docs/reference/rpc-functions.md:205 and :252 and an on-screen bucket-range label, not a SQL change.
